#### What is good

- Keep the mobile shell's basic fit. The header, market-session context, first-use heading, explanatory copy, and all four trader questions fit within a 390-pixel page with no document-level horizontal overflow.
- Keep the question-led entry and domain disclaimer. `Did options flow precede this stock move?` is recognizable trader language, and its preview separates observable chronology from unproven causality or private information before offering any analysis.
- Keep the ordered evidence path and explicit link semantics. The four steps progress from bounded historical reconstruction through concentration and structure to later Live follow-through, and `Start step 1` exposed the exact Historical path before activation.
- Keep the offer disclosure and mobile dialog fit. The gate named the seven-day duration, included credits, no-card condition, and existing-user sign-in route without clipping inside the tested viewport.
- Keep the access gateway's final routing contract. Although its interim presentation is misleading, successful active authentication ultimately recovered the exact requested Historical path and settled real data rather than dropping the user on generic Home or a paywall.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. The round ended with the seeded active identity, visible build, loaded Historical rows, desktop viewport, and exactly one useful Browser tab.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-020 · High · Mobile Home puts the first useful action several screens below the question**
>
> Selecting `Did options flow precede this stock move?` did not bring its explanation or action next to the selected button. Four large demo cards came first; `Use template` sat about 2,634 pixels below the page top, and the loaded `Start step 1` sat about 4,138 pixels down in a roughly 6,068-pixel Home. Between those actions, the user crosses the internal ability catalog. The workflow is technically present but not shaped as a mobile decision: a first-time trader can easily conclude that selecting a question did nothing or abandon before finding the next action. A greenfield Home would update an adjacent selected-workflow summary, keep one primary load/start action within the same or next viewport, and move deep demos and catalog composition behind progressive disclosure. Acceptance signal: after tapping any question at 390 pixels, the selected explanation and primary action are visible without crossing unrelated sections; after loading, the ordered checklist and first-step action remain in that same task region.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-003 · High · The gate recovers the route eventually but visibly discards the task while doing it**
>
> Before activation, the loaded first step truthfully targeted `/app/option-trades/historical`. Once the gate opened, Home immediately reverted to the default covered-call question, default symbol-volatility/GEX/tradeability/history build, and `/app/rank/symbols` start link. The dialog did not name Historical or promise to resume it. Successful active sign-in ultimately recovered the correct Historical route and 100-row result set, which is an important pass, but the first post-auth check still showed the unrelated default Home with no progress or resume cue. Combined with Round 001's dismiss path, the gateway has two user-visible stories for one intent: dismiss loses it, while success appears to lose it before a delayed redirect. A greenfield gateway would preserve the loaded Home state, name `Historical Option Trades` in the gate, show `Resuming…` after verification, and replace directly into the pending route. Acceptance signal: gate, cancel, successful sign-in, and failure all retain or explicitly resolve the same named destination without rendering an unrelated default workflow.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-002 · Medium · The mobile first-use path still teaches internal architecture before value**
>
> The opening question and chronology disclaimer are novice-readable, but the same path soon introduces `ATOMIC ABILITY LIBRARY`, `atomic ability`, `lenses`, `LOCAL COMPOSITION`, `Current Build`, navigation-group taxonomy, and 13 abilities. On a roughly 6,068-pixel mobile page, this is not incidental footer copy; it forms the bridge between loading a template and starting it. A new trader must understand the product's construction system before opening one evidence tool. A greenfield public Home would call these `Analysis tools` and `focus`, keep the loaded `Research checklist` primary, and defer composition mechanics until the user chooses to customize. Acceptance signal: a novice can choose, load, and start a workflow using only trader jobs, evidence types, and destination names; internal component taxonomy is absent from that path.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-001 · Medium · Public Home still sometimes opens underneath a returning-user dialog**
>
> Round 010 re-observed the full Premium/auth dialog immediately after logout and again on the first fresh signed-out root tab. It covered the public orientation page and addressed the visitor as `Welcome back`; a later mobile retry showed Home directly, so this round does not claim a deterministic trigger. The reasonable first-use contract is still clear: public Home should orient before asking for identity, and authentication should follow a chosen gated action. Acceptance signal: every direct signed-out root visit exposes usable Home with no unsolicited modal; when a gated action opens auth, the dialog names that action and uses neutral copy unless the browser has established a returning identity.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The underlying access handoff is more correct than Round 001 could prove: successful sign-in eventually restores the exact premium Historical destination and real data. The visible journey is still not a good greenfield design. Mobile Home stretches one first-value task across several screens and internal taxonomy, while the access gateway temporarily renders a reset, unrelated workflow and never tells the user what it is resuming. The credible redesign is one adjacent question → checklist → start region plus a destination-naming gateway with an explicit resuming state.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-020` (High); strengthened `CM-UX-001` (Medium), `CM-UX-002` (Medium), and `CM-UX-003` (High). Also recorded the successful final recovery as a design strength rather than misclassifying the intermediate reset as a failed destination handoff.
- Coverage rows updated: Guest mobile first entry and guided workflow are now findings-backed; guest → active mobile Historical intent recovery is now findings-backed with exact-route and paid-row success; mobile cross-surface recovery now distinguishes the bad interim state from the good final destination.
- What remains unknown: Truly never-used mobile-browser behavior; registration and eligible-trial paths; canceled/unpaid gating; authentication failure, timeout, resend, and close behavior; Browser Back after successful recovery; whether the observed interim Home duration varies; other guest workflows and premium destinations; mobile screenshots below the first viewport; active Home; loading/error/degraded states; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 011 — sign into the seeded canceled persona at `390 × 844`, enter one direct premium Option Trades destination, evaluate the signed-in paywall's explanation and safe return path without billing mutation, then restore the active account and desktop handoff.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: not required. The plan-before-Browser rule, bounded screenshot fallback, evidence labels, read-only boundary, and cleanup contract were sufficient; only this living report changed.

### Round 011 — Canceled-user mobile access gate and recovery

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 13:35:38 EDT (UTC-04:00)
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; prior visible build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, to be rechecked before account switching and after active-session restoration
- Why this round is next: Signed-in unpaid access is the highest-risk uncovered persona in the completion gate. Round 010 proved that a guest who authenticates as active eventually reaches the exact Historical destination, but it did not prove that a canceled account is recognized as signed in, denied all premium data, shown an honest recovery explanation, and kept separate from the previous active account's rows and preferences.
- User job: On a phone after a subscription has ended, understand the current account state, try to return to Historical Option Trades, learn why access is unavailable and what a safe next step is, dismiss or leave the gate without accidental purchase, and retain a trustworthy path back to the intended research surface if access is restored later.
- Surface and entry point: Begin from the one active `/app/option-trades/historical` desktop handoff left by Round 010 only to prove identity, build, and paid rows. Sign out through the visible account menu, close any unsolicited guest prompt, apply and prove `390 × 844`, sign in as the seeded canceled persona, and prove its identity plus read-only `/app/billing` state before directly entering `/app/option-trades/historical`. A direct premium deep link is intentional because preservation and gating of that exact destination are the subject of the round.
- Persona/account proof: Pre-run and final active state must show `active+clerk_test@example.com`, entitled Historical rows, and the visible build. The tested unpaid state must show `canceled+clerk_test@example.com` in `User menu` and canceled/no-active-subscription evidence on `/app/billing`; login uses only the current seeded fixture and test verification challenge. No registration or billing mutation is allowed.
- Viewport and state(s): Desktop `1280 × 720` only for the initial and final active proof; mobile `390 × 844` for canceled sign-in, Billing, direct Historical gate, close/return behavior, and any naturally reached verifying-access state. The round must end with the viewport override removed, one active desktop Historical tab, and no canceled session left open.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: The shared access gateway should distinguish signed-in unpaid from guest and unresolved access, name the blocked `Historical Option Trades` destination, explain the account's ended state without promising an ineligible new-account trial, and offer one safe account/billing path plus Close. It must preserve the exact pending destination for later entitlement recovery while Option Trades mounts no table, rows, filters, or stale active-account state.
- Novice expectation: See that the app knows which account is signed in, understand whether the plan ended or access is merely being checked, know what clicking the primary action will do before clicking it, close the prompt without being trapped in a redirect loop, and never glimpse the previous account's paid data.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove the active Historical handoff, open `User menu` for identity/build, sign out, dismiss any unsolicited guest dialog, set `390 × 844`, and sign in as `canceled+clerk_test@example.com`. Re-open `User menu`, follow its visible Billing destination, and inventory the settled plan/status explanation, CTA labels/hrefs, layout, and account identity without activating Checkout, portal, plan, payment-method, or cancellation controls.
  2. Directly navigate to `/app/option-trades/historical`, record every settled route/access transition, and verify that no Option Trades table, filter, metric, row, or stale active state mounts. Inspect the paywall's heading, account-aware copy, plan/trial language, destination/resume explanation, controls/hrefs, mobile fit, and background Home state. Close it once and, only if coherent, retry the same direct route or use Browser Back once to evaluate recovery and loop behavior; never activate a purchase-capable CTA.
  3. Sign out the canceled persona through visible account UI, sign back into the seeded active persona, wait for `/app/option-trades/historical` and paid rows to settle, restore `1280 × 720`, prove identity/build/date/row state, close obsolete tabs, and finalize one useful handoff tab.

#### What is good

- Keep the access resolver's neutral state. The direct canceled request remained on Historical with `Verifying session…` while entitlement was unresolved; it did not label the user unpaid early or flash an upgrade prompt during the check.
- Keep the hard premium boundary. Across the first attempt, retry, paywall, Close, and Back, no Option Trades table, row, filter, metric, or stale active-account value appeared.
- Keep the distinction between guest acquisition and signed-in recovery. The canceled paywall said `restore access` and did not promise the seven-day new-account offer observed for guests.
- Keep the base Billing status language. `NO ACTIVE SUBSCRIPTION` is direct and consistent with the canceled identity; it avoids pretending that the account is signed out or that access is merely loading.
- Keep app-dialog priority over third-party chrome. The Featurebase notification, survey, messenger, and badge collapsed to hidden zero-size frames while the premium dialog was open.
- Keep the safe escape behavior. Both Close and Browser Back returned to a usable signed-in Home without reopening the gate or creating a redirect loop, and a retry repeated the same no-data boundary.
- Keep the account-switch cleanup. The round ended after a deliberate active sign-in with the desktop Historical route, current date, `Default`, 100 rows, visible build, and finalized handoff.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-021 · High · Featurebase interrupts the canceled Billing conversion flow**
>
> The no-active-subscription Billing page initially presented a coherent recovery surface, then an unsolicited Featurebase welcome message and voting survey covered its lower half. At `390 × 844`, the notification occupied about `372 × 328` pixels and the survey about `390 × 290`; together they obscured the upgrade card and Billing History while competing with both `View Plans` actions. The survey offered no visible close, and the notification's close was hidden behind a hover-only opacity rule even though a phone has no dependable hover. This directly conflicts with the product invariant that feedback surfaces must not interrupt sign-in, upgrade, or paywall conversion. A greenfield system would suppress all unsolicited feedback chrome throughout Billing and access-recovery routes, then expose feedback from an intentional launcher after the user resolves or leaves the conversion task. Acceptance signal: canceled Billing at 390 pixels remains unobstructed for the full session; no survey or message auto-opens; any user-opened mobile overlay has a persistent, visible, operable close control.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-022 · High · The mobile paywall visually removes the reason for upgrading**
>
> Accessibility text contained `Upgrade to Premium` and `Choose a Premium plan to restore access to paid TradingFlow features`, but their rendered boxes were approximately one pixel wide at the left edge. The viewport screenshot confirmed that neither message was visibly legible; the first meaningful content was a `30% Off` promotion followed by plan tabs. The enabled `Start This Plan` button was muted gray like a disabled action, and the lower close action sat against the viewport edge. A canceled novice therefore meets price and urgency before learning what was blocked or why this account needs a plan, while the primary purchase state is visually ambiguous. A greenfield paywall would lead with the named blocked job and current access state, show one unmistakably enabled recovery action, place promotion second, and keep both Close and heading fully visible. Acceptance signal: at `390 × 844`, the complete heading and explanation have normal content width, `Historical Option Trades` is named before price, the enabled CTA is visually distinct from disabled controls, and all dismissal controls fit without edge clipping.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-003 · High · The canceled gate still does not name or promise recovery of Historical**
>
> The system correctly recognized a signed-in canceled user, prevented data from mounting, and used honest `restore access` language. It nevertheless redirected the direct `/app/option-trades/historical` request to generic Home and described only `paid TradingFlow features`; neither the route transition nor paywall named `Historical Option Trades` or said that this destination would resume after access returned. Round 010 already showed the same missing destination contract for guest sign-in. A greenfield gateway would keep one pending destination object across guest and unpaid states, render `Restore access to Historical Option Trades`, and either resume it after entitlement changes or clearly explain that Close/Back abandons it. Acceptance signal: direct guest and canceled gates display the exact destination; successful authentication or entitlement restoration settles there; cancel, Close, and Back explicitly preserve or abandon the same intent without an unrelated Home interlude.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-001 · Medium · Logout again triggers an unsolicited returning-user dialog**
>
> Logging out the active account in this round again settled on public Home with a full authentication dialog even though the user had just explicitly chosen Logout and had not selected a gated action. This repeats the Round 010 behavior and makes Logout feel like an immediate request to reverse itself. A greenfield sign-out should expose a calm public landing state and let the next gated action initiate authentication; if a reauthentication shortcut is useful, it should be a non-modal secondary action. Acceptance signal: explicit Logout always lands on usable Home with no modal, while a later gated action opens destination-specific authentication.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The entitlement architecture is substantially better than the conversion experience around it. It separates unresolved from unpaid, blocks all premium data, distinguishes a canceled account from a guest, and offers safe Close/Back behavior. The greenfield design is still not acceptable because third-party feedback takes over Billing and the mobile paywall hides its own explanation while leading with a promotion. The correct boundary is a quiet shared access gateway that names the pending trader job, a Billing surface free of unsolicited overlays, and a visually unambiguous recovery action.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-021` (High) and `CM-UX-022` (High); strengthened `CM-UX-003` (High) with the canceled destination contract and `CM-UX-001` (Medium) with another explicit-logout recurrence. Recorded the neutral verification state, no-data boundary, honest canceled language, third-party suppression during the app dialog, and loop-free escape paths as passes.
- Coverage rows updated: Canceled mobile account/Billing/access recovery, canceled direct Historical gating, and canceled mobile Close/retry/Back continuity are now findings-backed. Remaining trial, active Billing, other unpaid states, desktop canceled behavior, other premium destinations, and actual post-purchase entitlement restoration remain untested.
- What remains unknown: Whether an actual subscription restart preserves and resumes Historical; Checkout and Stripe behavior; plan eligibility and pricing correctness; Billing History; trial-without-payment versus trial-with-payment distinctions; canceled desktop layout; other premium routes; entitlement unavailable/error/timeout states; authentication failure/resend; production parity; and whether Featurebase timing varies by profile or visit.
- Next recommended round: Round 012 — use the seeded trial-without-payment persona at `390 × 844` to inspect read-only Billing status and one direct premium access path, verify whether trial access and payment-method requirements are explained without contradiction, then restore the active desktop Historical handoff. Do not activate plan, payment, portal, or account controls.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: not required. The plan-before-Browser, account-boundary, third-party-overlay, no-data-leak, screenshot/geometry, safe-recovery, and deterministic-cleanup rules all produced decisive evidence; only this living report changed.

### Round 012 — Trial-without-payment mobile clarity and premium continuity

#### Round Test Plan

Domain truth says a seven-day trial is fully entitled now, starts without Checkout or a card, and stops rather than charging when it ends; Billing must still explain that adding a payment method prevents interruption. A contradictory banner, conversion hierarchy, or gate could make a new trader believe the trial is not actually free, that current premium access is blocked, or that adding a card causes an immediate charge.
- User job: Understand that Premium access is active now, how much trial time remains, whether a payment method is currently attached, what happens at trial end, whether adding one charges immediately, safely defer that decision, and continue into Historical Option Trades without losing context or seeing an upgrade paywall.
- Surface and entry point: Start from a fresh in-app Browser tab at active `/app/option-trades/historical` to prove current identity/build/rows. #### What is good

- Keep runtime entitlement resolution authoritative. Direct Historical and its reload stayed neutral while access was unresolved, then mounted only after the current account resolved entitled; neither path flashed a paywall or stale row set.
- Keep account-owned Option Trades state isolated. The trial-labeled identity settled on 10 rows with explicit Size and Open Interest filters, while the active identity restored 100 rows on its own default view.
- Keep Billing queries account-scoped. The read-only comparison showed different credit balances, monthly usage, and reset dates for the two identities, ruling out one cached active-account panel being reused across the switch.
- Keep mobile paid access complete. Once entitlement resolved, the authenticated identity received the full Historical table and background Live connection rather than a partial trial mode.
- Keep honest evidence boundaries. The live Billing state contradicted the fixture label, so this round does not misreport active-subscription behavior as proof of a trial/no-card experience.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. The seeded active identity, desktop viewport, Historical route, current date/time, 100 rows, and visible build were all re-proved before finalization.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **R012-BLOCKER-001 · Environment blocker · The seeded trial/no-payment persona is no longer in its documented state**
>
> The current auth fixture and companion runbook identify `trialing+clerk_test@example.com` as `trial_no_pm`, expected to show trial access plus payment-method-needed messaging. In Browser, that exact authenticated email persistently showed an active Premium subscription, a paid monthly credit allowance, and subscription/payment-management controls; neither initial settlement nor a clean Billing reload exposed trial expiry or an add-payment action. Its distinct filters and Billing aggregates, compared with the restored active account, rule out stale active-account rendering and instead indicate that the seeded subscription baseline has drifted. The intended user job—understanding a cardless trial—therefore did not exist to review, and active-plan copy must not be filed as a trial UX defect. Acceptance signal for unblocking: restore or replace the seeded identity with a current `trialing`, not-canceling subscription that has no default or attached payment method; then Browser Billing must visibly prove that state before this round is rerun.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-021 · High · Featurebase again takes over mobile Billing after login**
>
> After the separate trial-labeled login, the same unsolicited Featurebase welcome notification and voting survey seen in Round 011 covered the lower half of Billing. The screenshot showed the notification over the subscription card and the survey, message composer, launcher, and unread badge filling the lower viewport. This recurrence is independent of whether the account's current subscription is trial or active: Billing is still a focused subscription/payment surface, and the product contract says automatic Featurebase surfaces never open there. A greenfield Billing flow would suppress notification, survey, popup, and messenger chrome for the entire route, leaving only a user-invoked feedback entry after the billing task. Acceptance signal: repeated fresh logins into `/app/billing` at 390 pixels never auto-open Featurebase content, while deliberately opened feedback remains available elsewhere.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-001 · Medium · Both account switches again reopen authentication immediately after Logout**
>
> Logging out of the active identity and later the trial-labeled identity each returned to Home with the full Premium/auth dialog already open, despite Logout being the user's explicit action and no gated destination being selected. This is now a repeated multi-account pattern rather than an isolated first-visit race. A greenfield sign-out should end at calm public Home and preserve reauthentication as a visible secondary action; only a later gated intent should open a modal. Acceptance signal: Logout from any seeded account always exposes usable Home without an unsolicited dialog, and explicit Sign in remains immediately available.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The planned cardless-trial experience could not be judged because the live test persona no longer represented that state. The observed entitlement and account-isolation mechanics were sound, but they describe two currently active subscriptions, not the clean greenfield promise of `trial active now → no card/no charge → access stops on date X unless you opt in`. That promise remains unproven rather than failed. Separately, third-party feedback again violated the clean Billing boundary.
- Findings added or strengthened: No new product UX finding was created from the fixture contradiction. Added round-local environment blocker `R012-BLOCKER-001`; strengthened `CM-UX-021` (High) with a second login and screenshot-backed Billing recurrence, and `CM-UX-001` (Medium) with two more explicit-Logout recurrences. Recorded neutral entitlement resolution, full entitled access, account-scoped state, and active cleanup as passes.
- Coverage rows updated: Trial-without-payment mobile Billing/access is now `blocked`, not passed or failed; direct Historical under that intended state is also blocked because the live account was active. The Browser still proved current entitled access and account isolation, but those observations do not close trial coverage.
- What remains unknown: All intended trial/no-card UX questions remain open: remaining-time/end-date language, no-immediate-charge disclosure, consequence of doing nothing, Add Payment Method hierarchy and destination, current access reassurance, true trial Historical continuity, and comparison with trial-with-payment. The cause and owner of the seeded subscription drift, production parity, and whether Featurebase timing varies across further fresh sessions also remain unknown.
- Next recommended round: Round 013 — from the restored active desktop session, review Cookbooks as one read-only research job: discover the gallery, open one official report, understand its live-versus-pinned session and evidence boundaries, test mobile fit and Browser Back, then restore the desktop report or Historical handoff. Do not click Run, AI Insight, Edit, New recipe, Save, delete, or any assistant control.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing requirements to prove live persona state, preserve Browser authority, record a blocked round, add an execution-time adjustment, and avoid external billing mutation handled the drift correctly; only this living report changed.

#### What is good

- Keep the gallery's explicit live-run explanation. It tells readers that each cookbook covers one U.S. equity option-flow session, that `Open` runs the latest session or a past date, and that AI is an optional on-demand read rather than baked report prose.
- Keep the separation and provenance signals that do exist: `My recipes` has a visible count, private/forked labels, dates, and distinct report/edit affordances; `Official templates` has seven concise categorized cards. The Pre-Move Positioning card explicitly says its pattern is descriptive and does not identify a trader or motive.
- Keep the settled report's session and source contract. `SESSION · Aug 10, 2026 · Latest available` was explicit; the opening copy named AggregatedOptionTrades, OptionChainTable, SymbolMetaData, T+1 chain/meta snapshots, the in-tape price proxy, and the fact that neutral prints are not directional.
- Keep the desktop chapter index. Eleven visible links stayed in a narrow rail, and the tested GEX anchor settled the correct heading at y=160 without losing the report.
- Keep the complete result and canonical disclaimer. The official report rendered all planned chapters, four data tables with 38 rows, charts, takeaways, and the platform disclaimer; on mobile the 358-pixel-wide disclaimer remained fully inside the viewport.
- Keep the mobile header's wrapping behavior. Run, date, AI Insight, Edit with AI, and Back formed two unclipped rows, and wide tables used local horizontal scrollers rather than widening the document.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. The final Browser proof showed desktop `1280 × 720`, `/app/cookbooks`, `active+clerk_test@example.com`, `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, and no remaining agent tabs.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-023 · High · Private drafts bury the curated Cookbooks starting point**
>
> The active gallery put `New recipe` and 18 private recipes before `Official templates`. The curated heading began 1,419 pixels into a 656-pixel desktop scroller and 3,950 pixels into a 780-pixel mobile scroller; the first official card began at y=4,058 on mobile. A new reader inheriting any meaningful recipe history therefore encounters an authoring archive for more than five mobile screens before the product's safest curated path. A greenfield gallery would lead with official questions/templates and expose personal work as a secondary tab, filter, or collapsible library whose size cannot displace first useful value. Acceptance signal: with 0, 18, or many more private recipes, the gallery's opening viewport or first normal scroll exposes the `Official templates` heading plus at least one official card, while `My recipes` remains one obvious action away.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-024 · Medium · `Open` silently changes from editing to reading**
>
> In `My recipes`, `View report` targeted `/app/cookbooks/r_…` while `Open` targeted `/app/cookbooks/r_…/edit`; in `Official templates`, `Open` targeted the read-only report. The same verb therefore means enter authoring for a private recipe but read a report for an official one, even though `View report` already establishes the reader-facing term. A novice can enter AI/edit machinery while intending only to open the analysis, weakening confidence around durable versus read-only actions. A greenfield gallery would use task nouns consistently: `View report` for every report and `Edit` or `Open editor` for authoring. Acceptance signal: no gallery action labeled `Open` changes meaning by section, and every edit destination is explicitly labeled before navigation.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-025 · High · An official report looks empty for at least seven seconds**
>
> After the visible Daily Market Recap `Open`, Browser sampled the settled report route every 350 ms. For at least about 7.35 seconds the main surface had no report heading, controls, announced loading copy, skeleton text, or recovery action; only the persistent footer remained. The full report eventually appeared, but a first-time trader has no evidence that live resolution is intentional rather than a broken route. This also contradicts the Cookbooks invariant that route loading and report resolution expose an explicit pending presentation. A greenfield live report would mount its identity and represented request immediately, then show an announced, bounded progress state until content or an actionable failure replaces it. Acceptance signal: from the first rendered report-route frame through live resolution, a visible and accessible pending state names the report/session; it can never collapse to an empty content shell and always terminates in content or Run/Back recovery.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-004 · Critical · The official report turns feed classifications into conviction**
>
> Daily Market Recap first notes that neutral premium is not directional, but later says every ticket's bullish/bearish/neutral tag shows how the whole tape `leaned`, that DEI measures exposure the flow `actually shifted`, and that DEI/DEX reveal and `confirm` `real directional conviction`. These statements transform feed-precomputed classifications and exposure proxies into trader intent and certainty. A novice can treat an official guided report as validation of directional positioning rather than a descriptive model with unresolved trade structure and counterparty context. A greenfield report would name the classification method, call DEI/DEX signed exposure proxies, keep uncertainty beside every interpretation, and reserve conviction/confirmation for evidence the product actually has. Acceptance signal: official Cookbooks copy contains no `conviction`, `confirm`, or `actually shifted` claim for inferred option flow; it discloses classification limits at the metric and takeaway where the inference is made.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-009 · Critical · Cookbooks expands GEX into dealer behavior and price prediction**
>
> The official gallery's `Gamma Walls & Magnet` card promised call `resistance`, put `support`, a `gamma magnet`, and where price gets `pinned` versus where it can `break and trend`. The opened official report then said dealers `must hedge` positive GEX by selling strength/buying weakness and negative GEX by doing the reverse, labeling the outcomes mean-reverting or trend-following. These are not merely glossary terms: they assert dealer inventory/behavior and a future price regime from modeled exposure. The bottom disclaimer cannot repair a decision-shaping claim made at selection and interpretation time. A greenfield GEX experience would present estimated exposure, assumptions, and conditional sensitivity scenarios without naming a magnet, support/resistance, known dealer action, pin, break, or trend outcome. Acceptance signal: gallery and report copy frame GEX as an estimate with bounded scenarios and never state dealer intent/hedging behavior or a predicted price path as fact.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-026 · High · The long mobile report removes chapter navigation and consumes Back**
>
> At `390 × 844`, Daily Market Recap reflowed to 12,536 pixels while every one of its 11 desktop TOC links measured zero by zero; no visible chapter picker, current-section control, or substitute appeared. After one legitimate TOC jump and reading to the disclaimer, one Browser Back only removed the chapter hash and returned to report top, not the gallery; a second Back was required. The user must therefore traverse a very long document by scrolling, and the natural escape gesture behaves like an invisible chapter-history step. A greenfield mobile report would expose a compact sticky chapter picker/current-section control and keep a persistent gallery return action reachable at reading depth; internal chapter navigation would not create surprising history traps. Acceptance signal: all chapters are reachable from a visible mobile control, current position is understandable, and after chapter use a single normal Back returns to the gallery or an always-visible equivalent does so explicitly.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Cookbooks has a strong underlying live-report model and the tested report eventually presents unusually explicit session, source, caveat, chapter, and disclaimer context. The first-use product shape is still unreasonable: personal authoring history displaces curated value, a shared verb changes action type, live resolution looks broken for several seconds, official metric copy overstates intent and dealer behavior, and mobile removes navigation from a twelve-thousand-pixel document.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-023` (High), `CM-UX-024` (Medium), `CM-UX-025` (High), and `CM-UX-026` (High). Strengthened `CM-UX-004` (Critical) with official sentiment/DEI language and `CM-UX-009` (Critical) with official GEX gallery/report language. Recorded the live-model explanation, grouping/provenance, settled session/source boundary, desktop TOC, complete result, disclaimer, mobile wrapping, and deterministic cleanup as design strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Cookbooks desktop/mobile discovery and one official read-only report now have `findings`; added a distinct gallery/report continuity row. The tested success state is settled, but explicit Run/date/pinned-session, access, error/offline recovery, other reports, and screenshot appearance remain open.
- What remains unknown: Whether the blank shell or load duration reproduces on other official/private reports, how explicit Run differs from initial Open, date-picker and pinned-session semantics, failure/offline recovery, guest/unpaid access, other templates' domain copy, private report viewing, actual mobile pixels because Browser capture was unavailable, touch discoverability of table scrolling, and production parity. Private authoring and AI remain intentionally untested because this round did not authorize mutation or paid AI.
- Next recommended round: Round 014 — from a proven active session, review Portfolio as a read-only user job on desktop and mobile: discover it from visible navigation, understand the empty or populated state, identify value/freshness/risk context and recovery, test Browser Back, then restore desktop without creating, importing, editing, or deleting any position.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing screenshot-recovery and geometry-fallback rule handled `R013-BROWSER-001`; no durable Browser, safety, report-schema, or round-selection procedure changed. Only this living report changed.

#### What is good

- Keep Portfolio inert on entry. The populated branch opened directly into analysis; it did not initiate broker OAuth, syncing, trading, journaling, or another durable action.
- Keep the Overview KPI definitions. `Today` explicitly meant change since previous close; Unrealized P&L was tied both to account value and open-position cost basis; Cash said available to trade; Buying power said it includes margin; the chart summary acknowledged deposits and withdrawals.
- Keep exact position identity and practical table columns. The equity and option rows retained symbol/type, quantity, average cost, mark, market value, unrealized P&L, portfolio weight, and visible DTE rather than collapsing the option into an underlying-only label.
- Keep the Trades accounting labels. The view separated closed lots from 19 history rows, named FIFO for monthly realized P&L, provided symbol attribution and win–loss counts, and gave every journal mutation an explicit `Add journal entry` accessible label.
- Keep the risk units and exact-contract continuation. Net Delta was share-equivalent, Gamma was per `$1` move, Theta was dollars per day, Vega was dollars per volatility point, and the NVDA position retained cost, mark, P&L, Greeks, IV, and DTE. The unactivated Rank link encoded the exact OCC-style option symbol.
- Keep responsive containment where it works. At `390 × 844`, the document had no page-level horizontal overflow, the four section tabs fit in one row, KPI cards and chart reflowed to the available width, wide data stayed in local scrollers, and the exact-contract Rank action remained inside the card.
- Keep normal route recovery. After a visible Historical → Portfolio entry and safe mobile tab switch, one Browser Back returned to Historical. Final cleanup restored desktop Historical with the active account/build and left no Browser tabs.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-027 · High · Current balances and performance history cannot be reconciled**
>
> With aggregate `All` and `3M` visibly selected on 2026-08-10, the live-looking Total value moved from `$67,124` to `$67,143` during the walkthrough. The adjacent performance card remained `+$1,153 (+1.4%)`, showed only May/June ticks ending `Jul 2`, and used a `$81.3K`–`$86.8K` axis—far from the current total. `value change incl. deposits/withdrawals` is a useful caveat, but no start value/date, final value/date, last successful sync, missing-history interval, or cash-flow reconciliation tells the reader which boundary each number represents. The footer's updating NY clock is global application time, not a labeled Portfolio as-of. Desktop broker status is only an aria-hidden green dot, and both Sync controls lack a visible last-result state. The data may be individually valid; the unreasonable UX is that the reader cannot establish that. A greenfield money surface would make provider status, last sync, series start/end, incomplete coverage, cash flows, and current-total reconciliation first-class. Acceptance signal: each aggregate/provider view names its last successful sync and represented interval; the chart exposes its endpoint and whether history is incomplete; the summary explicitly reconciles or distinguishes that endpoint from current Total value.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-028 · High · Mobile removes the brokerage and read-only trust boundary**
>
> Desktop at least exposed Alpaca, Webull, a bare `Read-only` footer, provider filters, Manage, and Sync. At `390 × 844`, the broker identities/status, the `Read-only` label, provider `All`/Alpaca/Webull controls, Watch holdings, Manage brokerages, and sidebar Sync all measured zero by zero; even the Portfolio parent breadcrumb was hidden. The first money view therefore began as generic `Overview`, with 1M/3M/1Y/ALL and an icon-only Sync control, while silently aggregating two brokerages. Nowhere in the active dashboard did main-surface copy say that TradingFlow cannot place trades or move funds. A new trader cannot verify which accounts feed the balances, whether both are connected/current, or what read-only actually protects before trusting the result. A greenfield mobile header would retain the current broker scope, textual connection/sync state, explicit no-trading/no-fund-movement boundary, and a compact provider selector; management can remain secondary. Acceptance signal: without opening a drawer, the mobile Overview names the aggregate or selected broker(s), connection and last-sync state, and the practical read-only permission boundary, with provider switching one obvious action away.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-029 · High · `Live` option-flow context has no represented session or metric meaning**
>
> The Options view says positions are cross-referenced with TradingFlow's `live option-flow data`, then summarizes the NVDA contract as `GEX Positive`, `Net DEX ▲`, `Net Prem $7.7K (P)`, and `Trades 123`. Neither desktop nor mobile named the session/as-of time, lookback window, source snapshot, DEX units/value, what the arrow encodes, or what `(P)` means. This puts time-sensitive directional-looking context beside personal risk while leaving the reader unable to tell whether it is current, stale, daily, cumulative, or comparable to the exact position. A greenfield tie-in would lead with the represented session and source window, show values with units and definitions, and explicitly distinguish modeled market context from the user's position Greeks. Acceptance signal: every flow-context card exposes session/as-of and window in its first view; DEX, premium, trade count, arrows, and GEX state have readable definitions and units; stale or unavailable context is labeled rather than called live.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-030 · Medium · Portfolio has no semantic headings and an unnamed interactive chart**
>
> Browser found zero `h1`, `h2`, `h3`, or heading-role elements in Overview, Positions, Trades, and Options. Page names and card titles such as Portfolio risk, Options flow tie-in, Allocation, and Trade history were plain spans/divs. The equity chart was keyboard-focusable with `role=application`, but its SVG title and description were empty and it had no accessible name. A screen-reader or headings-navigation user receives a long undifferentiated money surface, then lands on an interactive chart without knowing what it represents or what data boundary applies. A greenfield analytical document would expose one Portfolio heading, hierarchical section/card headings, and a named chart with a concise data summary or table fallback. Acceptance signal: heading navigation yields the page and all major analytical sections in order, while the focusable chart announces its name, selected period, represented endpoint, summary change, and an equivalent nonvisual data path.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-016 · High · Portfolio repeats the mobile hidden-table pattern**
>
> The mobile Overview placed an eight-column, 687-pixel Positions table inside a 278-pixel scroller. Only a fraction of the row is available at once, with no visible/accessibly named swipe cue, frozen symbol/contract identity, column chooser, or compact row alternative. Local overflow correctly protects the page width, but a trader still has to remember which AAPL, MSFT, or exact option row belongs to a distant P&L/weight value while panning. This is the same comprehension failure already observed on Option Trades and Rank, now attached to the user's own holdings. A greenfield mobile Portfolio would use a compact position card or disclosure row for identity, quantity, value, and P&L, with secondary fields expanded on demand. Acceptance signal: a 390-pixel user can associate every displayed value with its position without horizontal memory, while the full table remains available as an explicit dense view.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Portfolio has a credible read-only analytical core: it opens inertly, preserves exact holdings, labels important P&L/cash concepts, offers useful trade/risk views, contains mobile layout, and returns safely. It is not yet a trustworthy first-use money product because current balances and history have no reconcilable time boundary, mobile erases account/read-only scope, option-flow context is called live without a session or definitions, and semantic/navigation structure does not carry the visual hierarchy.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-027` (High), `CM-UX-028` (High), `CM-UX-029` (High), and `CM-UX-030` (Medium). Strengthened `CM-UX-016` (High) with the 687/278 mobile Portfolio table. Recorded inert entry, KPI caveats, exact position identity, FIFO trade accounting, Greek units, exact Rank target, responsive containment, and natural Back/cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active populated Portfolio now has desktop/mobile `findings` coverage across Overview, Positions, Trades, Options, responsive fit, and Back. A separate untested row preserves the no-connection/OAuth, empty, expired, failed, unpaid, sync, provider-filter, range, and management branches.
- What remains unknown: The broker chooser's payoff/security/scope copy; Webull versus Alpaca OAuth consent and credential boundary; empty, reconnect, expired, failed, and unpaid states; actual Sync progress/success/failure; last-sync behavior after a real sync; per-provider reconciliation; 1M/1Y/ALL behavior; whether TWR is correctly unavailable and how that is explained; journal, Watch holdings, Manage, disconnect, and exact Rank destination behavior; screenshot appearance because Browser capture was unavailable; production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 015 — review active Home on desktop and mobile as the first signed-in decision surface: settled hierarchy, first useful value, one safe read-only workflow/template inspection, navigation and Browser Back, with no AI run, save, edit, or other durable action.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing capture-retry, fresh-tab recovery, geometry fallback, direct-recovery exclusion, state restoration, and cleanup rules handled `R014-BROWSER-001`; no durable Browser, safety, report-schema, or round-selection procedure changed. Only this living report changed.

Prior product-review memory supplied read-only and settled-handoff cautions only; current Browser evidence remains authoritative.
- Evidence to collect: Exact host, route, build, active email, viewport, and entitled-row proof; Home load/settled state; headings/roles; first-view text and rectangles; four question states; exact public labels such as `Analysis tools`, `Your checklist`, `focus`, `Load into your checklist`, and any old labels that still appear; selected template explanation/guardrail/step names/targets; button order and enabled state; local-checklist feedback/order/reset disclosure; destination route/leaf/settled content; Back route, selected state, checklist state, scroll; mobile document/main widths/heights and key action offsets; visible or zero-size overlays; bounded screenshot for layout when capture works, with the runbook geometry fallback otherwise; final cleanup proof.
- Explicit non-goals: No `Customize with AI`, global AI/sidebar, prompt, consent, Recipe conversion, save, edit, reorder, remove, or focus change; no paid AI or credit use; no Watchlist, Saved View, filter, sort, export, account, Billing, subscription, notification permission, Featurebase, Portfolio, Learn, Blog, Changelog, or external navigation; no second research destination; no production claim; no repository Playwright, Chrome, ego-browser, Computer Use, curl, web search, code/test/domain-doc/configuration edit, or third-party mutation.

#### Browser Execution

Execution-time adjustments and Browser evidence limits:

- The existing active session was valid, so no authentication was needed. Before Home interaction, Browser proved `/app/option-trades/historical`, `1280 × 720`, `Default`, `Date range: 2026-08-10`, `Time range: Full day`, 100 rows, `active+clerk_test@example.com`, and `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`.
- `R015-BROWSER-001`: semantic locator clicks for the deeply scrolled `Use template` control timed out twice, and `Start step 1` timed out once, even though Browser diagnostics reported one enabled visible match. Measured CDP pointer presses on those same visible controls succeeded and produced the expected checklist and route transitions. Other semantic navigation and question-selection actions worked normally. This is recorded as a Browser-control limitation, not a product finding.
- Desktop screenshot capture worked for the first hierarchy, selected-template action, loaded checklist, and Back result. After mobile emulation was re-proved at `390 × 844`, capture framing retained the host surface rather than a trustworthy mobile crop; all mobile layout claims therefore use exact rectangles, content offsets, computed action styles, and `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth`, not screenshot appearance.
- No AI, Recipe, save, edit, reorder, remove, focus change, Watchlist, filter, export, account, Billing, Featurebase, supporting-destination, or other durable action was activated.

#### What is good

- Keep the intent-first entry. `What are you trying to decide?` is a real semantic `h1`, the four questions are recognizable trader jobs, and all four fit in the first desktop and mobile viewport rather than beginning with a feature taxonomy.
- Keep the compact market/session context. NY time, Market Open, and `U.S. equity options session context` establish the trading-day frame before the user interprets a workflow.
- Keep the IV workflow's evidence sequence and boundary. It separates governed IV-versus-realized comparison, term structure/skew, put/call pricing, and dated flow, then explicitly says volatility context does not recommend selling calls or buying puts.
- Keep honest demo labeling. Every illustrative card was visibly marked `Interactive demo` and `Local test capture · 2026-08-04`; none was presented as a live market observation.
- Keep supporting destinations outside the analysis blocks. Portfolio, Learn, Blog, and Changelog were grouped separately, and external destinations explicitly said they open TradingFlow.com.
- Keep the checklist explicit and reversible. The loaded order, focus for each step, move/remove affordances, exact route targets, and browser-local/reset disclosure made the temporary nature inspectable without saving or running AI.
- Keep responsive containment. At `390 × 844`, the page and main scroller both stayed exactly 390 pixels wide, the question buttons and checklist actions fit, and no page-level horizontal panning was required.
- Keep destination data neutral until identity exists. The first step did not fabricate a ticker or open a Vol drawer for an arbitrary symbol; Rank exposed the current session and full symbol catalog, and no data or account mutation occurred.
- Keep deterministic recovery. The active account, desktop dimensions, Historical state, current rows, date/time, and build were all re-proved before the only Browser tab was finalized.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-002 · Medium · The deployed Home still asks traders to learn the product's internal architecture**
>
> The current active Home repeats the guest evidence from Rounds 001 and 010: a plain-language question path gives way to `Atomic ability library`, `atomic ability`, `lenses`, `Local composition`, `Current Build`, and navigation-group explanations before the first evidence step. Browser found none of the documented customer terms `Analysis tools`, `focus`, or `Your checklist` in build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`. The trader must decide whether to use a template, open one of four steps, assemble abilities, manipulate lenses, start a build, or customize with AI—several product models for one simple job. A greenfield Home would keep the checklist and evidence purpose dominant and disclose builder mechanics only after an explicit customize choice. Acceptance signal: the deployed desktop/mobile path uses customer terms consistently, and a novice can name the next evidence step without knowing what an atomic ability, lens, local composition, or current build is.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-020 · High · The selected question, its safety boundary, and its next action are separated by thousands of pixels**
>
> On active mobile Home, the selected IV question began at content y=588, but its selected-template heading did not begin until y=2,280, after all four large demo cards; the non-recommendation guardrail began at y=2,432, `Use template` at y=2,548, and the loaded `Start step 1` at y=4,052 in a 5,872-pixel scroller. That puts the explanation 1,692 pixels, the load action 1,960 pixels, and the first executable step 3,464 pixels below the user's choice. Desktop uses a side-by-side layout but still puts `Use template` 862 pixels and Start 1,716 pixels below the question. The cards are useful previews, but their size turns the selected plan and safety boundary into late content and makes selection feel nonresponsive. A greenfield mobile selection would immediately reveal a compact summary, guardrail, and one next action, with the four evidence cards progressively disclosed beneath it. Acceptance signal: selecting any question keeps its title, guardrail, and load/start action in the current or next viewport at 390 pixels, and desktop keeps the primary action adjacent to the selected summary.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-031 · High · `Start step 1` drops the exact evidence focus it promises**
>
> The loaded step was explicitly `Compare IV with realized volatility`, yet its link was only `/app/rank/symbols`. The settled destination carried no template, focus, or pending-step state and opened the broad default 6,094-symbol workstation with `Default · 0`, a separate `Vol` control, and the Symbols table. A first-time trader must infer that they should choose a symbol, discover the Vol affordance, and reconstruct the IV30/RV20 question; the saved-view load and broad table make the transition look like a generic module jump rather than step 1 of the chosen plan. Not fabricating a symbol is correct, but dropping the task is not required. A greenfield handoff would carry the focus and render an explicit intermediate state such as `Choose a symbol to compare IV30 with RV20`, with the volatility preset ready after selection. Acceptance signal: the destination visibly names the originating step, explains the missing symbol input, preserves the relevant focus/preset, and can return to the same checklist.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-032 · High · Browser Back silently replaces the research plan while preserving its old deep scroll**
>
> After the IV template was loaded, the four checklist focuses were volatility comparison, term structure/skew, put/call pricing, and historical-flow cross-check. One Browser Back from Rank restored `/app/home` at the exact deep `main.scrollTop=1738.5`, but selected the default call-sale question and replaced the checklist with Gamma exposure, Contract tradeability, and recent call flow. The selected question itself was more than 1,300 pixels above the returned viewport, so the first visible evidence of the replacement was a different lower checklist. This directly contradicts the nearby statement that the browser-local build resets when the page refreshes: the user did not refresh. A trader can now continue the wrong plan without noticing. A greenfield route boundary would retain the selected template, ordered focuses, and meaningful return scroll for the tab's research session. Acceptance signal: same-tab Start → destination → Back restores the identical checklist and selected question; if leaving intentionally discards it, the UI says so before navigation and returns to the top with an explicit reset message.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-033 · Medium · AI customization is visually primary over starting the evidence checklist**
>
> Once the user selected and loaded a complete four-step plan, `Start step 1` and `Customize with AI` were adjacent 44-pixel actions on desktop and mobile. Start used a neutral gray background, while AI used the product's blue primary treatment. The explanatory copy then discussed a clarifying question and Recipe creation, pulling attention back into composition before the trader has completed one evidence step. This is the wrong greenfield priority for first value: AI can be a useful optional modifier, but it should not outrank the direct research action after the plan is already coherent. Acceptance signal: `Start step 1` is the unmistakable primary action after load, AI customization is secondary, and the user reaches evidence before being invited to create a more elaborate workflow.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-010 · Critical · The Home handoff still lands a symbol job under contract-only framing**
>
> The settled destination correctly titled itself `Symbol-level analysis` and described a 6,094-symbol catalog, but the next line said `Contract-level analysis mixes intraday delayed option flow with prior-session open interest and daily volume context`, followed by contract opportunity cards before the Symbols table. This repeats the source/provenance mismatch found in Rounds 005 and 009 at exactly the moment Home promises a symbol-volatility step. The novice cannot tell which values are symbol aggregates, which come from contract flow, or why contract leaders frame an IV-versus-realized screen. A greenfield Symbols destination would lead with the symbol snapshot's own source, freshness, coverage, and aggregation rules, and place any contract-derived context in a separately labeled supporting region. Acceptance signal: the first Symbol-level viewport contains no unlabeled Contract-level caveat or contract leaderboard; each symbol metric identifies its source and represented session before use.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Home has the right substantive ingredients—trader questions, bounded evidence sequences, honest demo art, a strong volatility guardrail, semantic structure, and responsive containment. The orchestration is not yet a good first-use design. The selected plan is buried behind previews and internal architecture, AI visually outranks starting, the first handoff loses its focus, and Back can silently substitute a different research plan while leaving the user at the old scroll position.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-031` (High), `CM-UX-032` (High), and `CM-UX-033` (Medium). Strengthened `CM-UX-002` (Medium) with current active desktop/mobile terminology, `CM-UX-020` (High) with exact active desktop/mobile hierarchy, and `CM-UX-010` (Critical) with the Home-to-Symbols entry context. Recorded the question framing, market context, IV evidence sequence/guardrail, demo labels, supporting-destination separation, semantics, responsive containment, current destination data, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Home now has desktop/mobile findings coverage for initial hierarchy, IV selection, transient checklist, first-step handoff, and return. A new active Home-to-Rank continuity row records the lost focus and Back reset while preserving the broader untested cross-surface row.
- What remains unknown: Current guest parity after the apparent source/deployment terminology divergence; the other three template handoffs; whether a chosen symbol can carry the Home focus into the correct Vol/Chain tab; Home reload, copied-link, alternate-tab, loading, empty, and error behavior; any intentional session-persistence design; AI clarification/Recipe paths; guest/unpaid gates from current Home; production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 016 — test the active global ticker search, Watchlists, navigation, and announcements on desktop/mobile: discover one symbol, inspect the offered destinations, follow one read-only context handoff and Back, and verify notification surfaces without adding/removing a Watchlist item or saving state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing plan-before-Browser, transient-state boundary, action-by-action settlement, screenshot/geometry fallback, Back-state proof, deterministic restoration, and report-schema rules were sufficient. `R015-BROWSER-001` was handled as an execution limitation without being converted into a false product finding; only this living report changed.

### Round 016 — Active shared symbol scope, Watchlists, navigation, and announcements

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 15:03:12 EDT (UTC-04:00)
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; prior visible build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, to be re-proved from the active Historical shell before interaction and during final restoration; any build drift will be recorded before attributing findings
- Why this round is next: The shared symbol/Watchlist control, navigation, and Updates affordance frame every core workstation but remain untested as one first-use job. Domain truth separates transient workbench scope from durable Watchlists and next-visit defaults, while implementation evidence shows that selecting a search result also writes a recent ticker to local storage and opening Updates may affect vendor-owned read state. This round therefore tests discovery, an existing-list transient filter, navigation continuity, and announcement visibility without selecting a search result, changing a Watchlist, toggling a default, or opening Updates.
- User job: From an active Historical workstation, find `NVDA`, understand whether the combined control searches, filters, saves, or navigates, use an already-saved Watchlist ticker only as a reversible current-page scope when available, move to one other workstation and return, then verify that desktop/mobile navigation and announcements remain understandable without changing durable state.
- Surface and entry point: Start in a fresh in-app Browser tab at active Option Trades Historical. Use the visible `Search ticker...` / `Search symbols & watchlists` control, the currently selected Watchlist only, desktop primary navigation, Browser Back, the mobile menu, and the footer Updates affordance. Do not use hidden routes for the tested transitions.
- Persona/account proof: `active+clerk_test@example.com`; prove the visible email in `User menu` plus entitled Historical rows. Login is allowed only if the existing active session is unavailable; no registration, account edit, entitlement, or access mutation.
- Viewport and state(s): Desktop `1280 × 720` for initial scope, combined dialog, one reversible symbol filter, route-away/Back, and footer state; mobile `390 × 844` for the same dialog/menu/footer information architecture without another scope change; restore desktop, all-symbol Historical, and the original active/default Watchlist selection before finalizing.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: The shared shell should distinguish four jobs at the point of action: find a ticker, filter the current workbench, save/remove a ticker, and open deeper evidence. Watchlists own durable collections and next-visit defaults; the open workbench owns transient scope; destination workbenches own their evidence. The user should always know which list is merely being inspected, which scope is applied, whether the action persists, and what Back will restore. Announcements should communicate unread state without competing with research controls or requiring a state-changing open merely to understand their purpose.
- Novice expectation: Typing `NVDA` should show one official identity and explain what selecting it will do before selection. The combined Watchlist region should clearly separate current, default, and applied states; an existing ticker should produce an obvious, reversible page filter without modifying the list; moving to another workstation and Back should preserve the originating scope. Desktop and mobile should expose the same navigational destinations and a comprehensible Updates control without clipping, unexplained badges, or surprise overlays.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove active Historical, build, rows, account, `Default` filter set, empty symbol/watchlist scope, and `1280 × 720`. Inventory desktop primary navigation, footer controls, Updates label/badge/tooltip target, visible overlays, and symbol-control copy/ARIA. Open the combined dialog, type `NVDA`, and inspect official result identity, logo/fallback, exchange/name, groups, selectable semantics, heart/remove affordances, Watchlist names/counts, current/default/applied distinctions, filter/apply actions, auto-apply copy/state, dialog dimensions, and focus. Do not select the search result, heart/remove anything, switch Watchlists, use list actions, or toggle auto-apply.
  2. If `NVDA` already exists in the currently selected Watchlist, activate only that existing ticker row as the documented transient single-symbol scope; otherwise use the first existing ticker and record the adjustment. Wait for the committed symbol label and matching Historical rows, navigate to Rank through the visible desktop primary navigation, wait for its independently settled scope, then use Browser Back once. Verify the originating symbol scope and matching rows return, clear it with the explicit clear action, and prove the original all-symbol rows and default filter set return. Do not apply a Watchlist, select a search result, alter a Saved Filter Set/View, sort, export, refresh, or open row details.
  3. Re-prove and set `390 × 844`; inspect header containment, open the mobile menu, inventory destination order/labels/active state/touch rectangles, close it, open the combined symbol/Watchlist dialog without typing or changing scope, and measure dialog sections, footer/auto-apply visibility, page/dialog overflow, focus, and close affordance. Inspect the mobile Updates control and badge without opening it because Featurebase owns read state. Restore desktop, all-symbol Historical, original Watchlist selection, account/build/data, clear emulation, close obsolete tabs, and finalize the only Browser tab.

#### Browser Execution

Execution-time adjustments and Browser evidence limits:

- The existing active session was valid, so no authentication was needed. Browser proved `active+clerk_test@example.com`, `/app/option-trades/historical`, `1280 × 720` at DPR 2, `Default`, empty symbol/Watchlist scope, `Date range: 2026-08-10`, `Time range: Full day`, 100 entitled rows, and `v0.2.1+01ad1e9` before interaction.
- `NVDA` was not a member of the current `consumered` Watchlist, so Journey 2 used its first existing member, `AAPL`, exactly as the plan allowed. Typing `NVDA` remained discovery-only; the search result was not selected, so no recent-ticker write occurred.
- `R016-BROWSER-001`: `Page.captureScreenshot` timed out twice—once on the baseline and once with the dialog open. The runbook's one fresh-tab recovery was used. The dialog-dismissal failure reproduced for more than 31 seconds in that fresh tab before any screenshot attempt, so it is product evidence; no Round 016 layout claim is labeled screenshot evidence.
- `R016-BROWSER-002`: mobile device emulation reverted between some Browser call boundaries. Every accepted mobile measurement was collected in the same call that set and re-proved `innerWidth=390`, `innerHeight=844`, and DPR 2. Desktop values observed after a reset were excluded from mobile evidence.
- Featurebase owns Updates read state, so Browser inspected the signed-in trigger, badge, vendor-frame geometry, and unsolicited-overlay state without clicking it. The dropdown content and read transition remain untested rather than being mutated for coverage.
- The planned scoped AAPL → Rank → Back sequence could not continue through the visible UI because the closed dialog's backdrop intercepted Rank. Browser cleared the AAPL scope from inside the still-visible dialog and reloaded only to remove that broken transient layer. It then tested ordinary all-symbol Rank navigation and Back separately; this is not reported as scoped-handoff success.
- No search result, heart/remove action, Watchlist rail item, Watchlist apply/default/auto-apply/list action, Saved Filter Set/View, sort, export, row, AI, account, Billing, language/theme, Updates, external link, or other durable action was activated.
