In a separately re-proved mobile state, opened the combined dialog without changing anything and measured its stacked regions, scroll containers, actions, and footer. Inspected Updates without opening it, then restored desktop Historical/account/build/data and finalized the only tab. | The document stayed `390/390`. The menu was 320×844, contained all six desktop destinations in the same order, marked Option Trades current, and closed normally. The dialog fit at 340×684 with a 218.5-pixel Watchlist rail, 124.9-pixel active-symbol region, 162.9-pixel search region, 92.4-pixel scrollable results viewport, and 95-pixel auto-apply footer. Search and Updates collapsed to icon-only controls but retained `Symbol filter: Search ticker...` and `What's new`; Updates had no unread count and no popup covered the job. Touch geometry was materially small: menu links 296×36 and Close 32×32; Watchlist rows 317×26.6–30.4, New 38×22.8, ticker-select buttons 260×19, remove buttons 30.4×30.4, and dialog Close 30.4×30.4. Final proof restored `1280 × 720`, all-symbol Historical, `Default`, current date/full day, 100 rows, active email/build, no dialog, and an empty Browser tab inventory. | `R016-M1`: two same-call re-proved mobile viewport captures; document/main/menu/dialog/section/list/footer/action rectangles and scroll metrics; destination/current-state inventory; Updates name/badge and zero covering popup; final desktop route/filter/date/time/rows/account/build and tab inventory. | Good containment and destination parity; added `CM-UX-036` |

#### What is good

- Keep exact catalog identity first. `NVDA` was the first result for its ticker, with `Nvidia Corp`, an exact `NASDAQ:NVDA` tag, and the explicit accessible action `Filter by NVDA`; related leveraged/income products followed rather than displacing the underlying.
- Keep filter and save actions semantically separate. The result row exposed `Filter by NVDA` while its nested heart said `Add NVDA to watchlist`; the existing-list workspace separately offered ticker selection, removal, and whole-Watchlist filtering.
- Keep current, default, and applied states distinct. `consumered` was visibly `Default` and semantically current, while no `Applied` claim appeared in the all-symbol workbench. The explicit `Filter by this watchlist` action prevented mere inspection from silently changing page scope.
- Keep the auto-apply boundary explicit. `Use your default watchlist when opening Rank or Option Trades. Changes apply on your next visit or reload.` accurately separated the account preference from the mounted all-symbol workstation; it was off and untouched.
- Keep ordinary workbench scopes independent. Clean desktop navigation settled on Rank Contracts with its own empty `Search symbols` scope and `Default · 0`; it did not inherit a stale Option Trades symbol.
- Keep desktop/mobile destination parity. Home, Option Trades, Rank, Cookbooks, Market Recap, and Portfolio appeared in the same order, with a correct current item and no horizontal page overflow at 390 pixels.
- Keep Updates subordinate to research. Desktop exposed the text `Updates`; mobile retained the accessible name `What's new`; the badge was empty, no automatic update popup covered Historical, and the audit did not need to mutate vendor read state to understand the entry.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. The temporary AAPL scope was explicitly cleared, all-symbol rows returned, desktop dimensions/account/build/date/time/default state were re-proved, and every Browser tab was finalized.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · High · The shared symbol/Watchlist dialog never actually dismisses and traps the workstation**
>
> In the screenshot-free recovery tab, selecting the existing `AAPL` Watchlist row changed the dialog to `closed`, but the 896×648 dialog remained fully visible and its full-screen backdrop continued to receive pointer hits over the primary navigation at every poll from 2.1 through 31.1 seconds. The data underneath eventually became correctly AAPL-scoped, yet the trader could not click Rank or any other outside control. Pressing the dialog's own Close reproduced the same visible `closed` surface after 2.5 seconds. A reload was the only safe recovery. This turns the shared entry to ticker scope into a dead end on every core workstation. A greenfield modal must complete dismissal, remove its backdrop, return focus to the trigger, and leave the chosen transient scope usable. Acceptance signal: selecting a ticker, clicking Close, pressing Escape, or clicking the backdrop leaves zero visible dialog/backdrop nodes after the exit transition; primary navigation is immediately pointer/keyboard operable and a symbol-scope route-away/Back loop can complete without reload.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-035 · High · Scope and Back transitions present stale or empty data as an authoritative result**
>
> At 2.1 seconds after selecting AAPL, the visible control had committed to `AAPL` and the live region announced `Filtering by AAPL`, but 99 of 100 rows were still unrelated symbols and no visible applying/loading guard prevented interpretation. The table became correct by 4.6 seconds. A separate clean Rank → Back transition was worse: after nine seconds, Historical showed `0 total records` and one empty table row with no loading, no-results, error, or recovery message, then recovered twelve seconds later to 100 rows and 6,489,890 records. A novice can reasonably read both intermediate states as settled market evidence. A greenfield workbench should keep scope, summaries, total, and rows atomic: either preserve the last-good set under an explicit `Applying AAPL…`/`Refreshing…` state, or block all evidence regions with one loading contract until the new result is authoritative. Acceptance signal: the scope label never names AAPL while non-AAPL rows remain actionable, and route return never renders `0 total records` unless an authoritative empty response has settled with an explanation and recovery path.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-019 · Medium · The shared heart instruction still exposes an opaque save model**
>
> The shared desktop/mobile dialog repeats the Rank drawer label `HEART ADDS TO "CONSUMERED"`. It is technically specific, but a first-time user must infer that a heart means a durable save, that `consumered` is the currently targeted Watchlist, and that clicking the surrounding result instead performs a transient page filter. The same three-pane workspace also contains remove hearts, `New`, list actions, `Filter by this watchlist`, and auto-apply. A greenfield control would use direct verbs—`Filter current page by NVDA` and `Save NVDA to consumered`—and visually separate transient scope from durable collection management. Acceptance signal: every symbol row names both available consequences in plain language without teaching a heart metaphor or requiring the user to infer which Watchlist will persist the ticker.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Mobile navigation and Watchlist controls are too small for safe scope management**
>
> At `390 × 844`, primary menu rows measured 36 pixels high and its Close target 32×32. The higher-risk Watchlist dialog was denser: adjacent list choices were only 26.6–30.4 pixels high, `New` was 38×22.8, ticker selection itself was a 260×19 text strip, remove buttons were 30.4×30.4, and dialog Close was 30.4×30.4. The page fits, but it achieves that fit by shrinking controls around persistent add/remove and scope-changing actions, increasing wrong-list and destructive mis-taps. A greenfield mobile design would use full-row interactive targets at least 44 pixels high, preserve spacing between select and remove, and move infrequent list management behind a clearly labeled secondary mode. Acceptance signal: every mobile nav, list, close, switch, add/remove, and scope action has an effective 44×44 minimum target with non-overlapping hit areas, while the dialog still fits and scrolls at 390 pixels.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The shared model is directionally sound—official ticker identity, explicit transient-versus-durable actions, current/default/applied distinctions, next-visit auto-apply timing, independent workbench scopes, responsive navigation parity, and a quiet Updates entry. The current implementation is not a safe first-use design because its core dialog cannot dismiss, its transitions briefly publish contradictory or false-empty market evidence, and its mobile density makes wrong-scope or destructive taps too easy.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-034` (High), `CM-UX-035` (High), and `CM-UX-036` (Medium). Strengthened `CM-UX-019` (Medium) with the active shared desktop/mobile dialog. Recorded exact NVDA identity, clear current/default/applied state, auto-apply timing, ordinary navigation/scope independence, responsive containment, non-intrusive Updates, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Split active and guest shared-shell coverage; active desktop/mobile ticker search, current Watchlist inspection, transient AAPL scope, navigation, Back, and Updates affordance now have findings evidence. Added the product-blocked AAPL-scope-to-Rank continuity row while preserving broader untested continuity coverage.
- What remains unknown: Guest search and temporary-Watchlist behavior; selecting a search result and its recent-ticker persistence; other current/default Watchlists; whole-Watchlist application; pending, unavailable, empty, missing, deleted, or load-error repair states; symbol-scope route-away/Back after dialog dismissal is fixed; reload and copied-link scope behavior; Updates unread badge/dropdown/content/read transition; production parity; screenshot appearance; keyboard-only focus restoration; other routes that reuse the same dialog.
- Next recommended round: Round 017 — test active Assistant Channels discovery on desktop/mobile: open the visible AI entry and channel surface, understand purpose, provenance, consent, empty/existing state, and safe return without sending a prompt, creating a channel, or changing configuration.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing fresh-tab capture recovery, geometry fallback, same-call viewport re-proof, product-blocker recording, transient-scope restoration, third-party mutation boundary, and report schema handled both Browser limitations and the product dead end. Only this living report changed.

### Round 017 — Can a first-time trader understand and safely discover Assistant Channels?

#### Round Test Plan

- User job and selection rationale: A paid trader notices TradingFlow AI and wants to know whether the same assistant can be used from an existing messaging app, what gets linked or retained, and whether simply exploring the feature can spend credits or change account state. Assistant Channels is the highest-value uncovered visible product surface and follows Round 016's shared-shell discovery naturally.
- Surface and entry: Start from the settled active desktop shell at `https://testapp.tradingflow.com/app/option-trades/historical`. Discover the visible AI entry and the account/settings path from rendered labels rather than a hidden deep link; follow any visible route to the assistant workspace and Settings → Messaging apps. Repeat materially different discovery and layout behavior at `390 × 844`.
- Persona, viewport, and states: `active+clerk_test@example.com`; desktop `1280 × 720` and mobile `390 × 844`, each re-proved in the same Browser call before counting evidence. Cover the current consent/credit state, assistant empty or existing-history state, messaging-app availability, configured/unconfigured or linked/unlinked platform state, and safe return. Do not manufacture another state.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Assistant Channels is a Settings-owned bridge from one paid, consent-gated TradingFlow assistant into external messaging apps. A clean first-use path should say that plainly, name supported platforms and important platform differences, explain identity/privacy/data-retention and the shared credit pool before connection, show current links separately from available destinations, and require one explicit confirmation before minting any short-lived code. Exploring the hub or assistant should not send, spend, link, or persist anything.
- Novice expectation: The global AI entry should identify what TradingFlow AI can and cannot do before a prompt is sent. `Messaging apps` should be findable from language a trader understands; the page should explain whether Slack, Telegram, Discord, and Messenger use the same assistant and credits, what data the bot can access, whether conversations appear in web history, how privacy differs by platform, what a link code does and how long it lasts, and how to disconnect or delete retained data. Existing, unavailable, and not-yet-linked states should be distinguishable without pressing a generative or connect action.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove active account, build, route, entitled rows, desktop viewport, and no covering overlay. Inventory the visible AI entry and its accessible name, open the assistant without submitting, and inspect title, explanatory copy, consent state, credit/balance disclosure, prompt placeholder, suggested actions, current/new/history controls, provenance or limitations, and close/return behavior. Do not type, submit, retry, copy, rate, create/clear/delete a thread, invoke a tool, or accept/change consent.
  2. From rendered desktop navigation/account controls, find Settings and then the visible Messaging apps destination. Inspect page title, purpose, supported platforms, connection states, availability/gating copy, prerequisites, linking steps, link-code explanation, security/privacy/retention/credit disclosures, existing-link management, external destinations, and disabled/error/empty feedback. Do not generate or refresh a code, connect, disconnect, open a third-party service, copy a code, or alter configuration.
  3. Re-prove `390 × 844`; repeat discovery through the mobile shell/account navigation, inspect the assistant and Messaging apps layout, reading order, overflow, target sizes, sticky/fixed regions, and safe Close/Back return. Restore `1280 × 720`, the original route/account state, close overlays, clear emulation, and finalize all Browser tabs.
- Domain sources read by the operator: webapp `AGENTS.md`; `knowledge/basic_concepts.md`; Assistant Channels `domain-invariants.md` and `functionality.md`; `ops/webappp-fullstack/chaos-monkey-ux-review.md`; setup/safety guidance in `browser-e2e-product-review.md`; current runbook-maintainer, greenfield, and in-app Browser skills. Prior memory supplied only the existing shared-assistant/profile boundary and channel-history caution; current source and Browser evidence remain authoritative.
- Evidence to collect: Exact host/route/build/account/viewport baseline; visible AI entry name/role/geometry; assistant title, copy, consent and credit state, history state, suggestions, prompt controls, disclaimers/provenance, Close/return result; every user-visible route and label used to discover Settings/Messaging apps; page heading/description; platform inventory/order/logo/name/status; connect/link-code action names and disabled state; existing links; privacy, access, consent, credit, history/retention, expiry, private-surface, and platform-limit copy; loading/empty/error feedback; desktop/mobile document and panel rectangles, overflow, reading order, target sizes, and focus; exact final cleanup proof. Use screenshots only for hierarchy, clipping, or layout claims when capture is trustworthy, with DOM geometry as fallback.
- Explicit non-goals: No prompt text or assistant submission; no AI credit spend; no retry/copy/rating; no new/clear/delete thread; no consent mutation; no link-code generation/refresh/copy/redemption; no platform connect/disconnect; no Slack, Telegram, Discord, Messenger, or other third-party navigation; no external message; no recipe, skill, memory, Watchlist, Portfolio, account, Billing, subscription, language/theme, notification, or Featurebase mutation; no guest/unpaid persona; no production claim; no repository Playwright, Chrome, ego-browser, Computer Use, curl, web search, code/test/domain-doc/configuration edit, or application-data mutation.

#### Browser Execution

Execution-time adjustments and Browser evidence limits:

- Browser proved `active+clerk_test@example.com`, `/app/option-trades/historical`, `1280 × 720` at DPR 2, `Default`, 100 entitled rows, `Date range: 2026-08-10`, `Time range: Full day`, and `v0.2.1+01ad1e9` before interaction. Market data continued updating during the market-open run, so record totals are cleanup evidence rather than a fixed product fixture.
- The current account already had Slack and Discord links plus 122 web chat threads. Browser inspected only those existing read surfaces. No clean unlinked, unconsented, empty-history, or unavailable-platform state was manufactured.
- `R017-BROWSER-001`: `Page.captureScreenshot` timed out twice in the working tab and once in the runbook's single fresh-tab recovery, even though navigation, DOM inspection, and ordinary interaction remained healthy. No Round 017 claim is labeled screenshot or visual evidence; layout findings use exact rectangles, widths, scroll metrics, overflow, and hit testing.
- `R017-BROWSER-002`: finalizing the fresh screenshot-recovery tab finalized the whole current Browser tab set, including the desktop working tab. Desktop Account Settings → Back therefore remains untested. A fresh mobile journey repeated visible AI/account discovery and completed Account Settings → Browser Back; the tab-finalization behavior is recorded as a Browser operator limitation, not a product finding.
- `R017-BROWSER-003`: mobile device metrics can reset across Browser call boundaries. Every accepted mobile observation was gathered in a call that re-applied and re-proved `390 × 844`, DPR 2; desktop values were restored and re-proved before cleanup.
- After Browser captured the first-use gaps, the exact visible-build commit `01ad1e9` was inspected only to classify them. It confirms that connectable platforms are eligibility/configuration filtered, code expiry and platform commands appear only after minting, channel threads are retained outside web history after unlink, mobile AI uses a fixed 380-pixel panel inside a generic three-quarter-width sheet, and `Delete chat` directly removes a thread. Source is supporting classification, not Browser evidence.
- No AI prompt, suggestion, Send, Retry, Copy, rating, New chat, Clear chat, thread load/delete, consent, link-code, Disconnect, external platform, schedule, skill, Recipe, Watchlist, Portfolio, account, Billing, language/theme, Updates, or other durable action was activated. The `1,968 credits` balance was unchanged across the inspected assistant states.

| Journey | Starting state and expectation | Browser action | Settled visible result | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Discover and understand TradingFlow AI on desktop | Proven active Historical with 100 rows; expected a no-cost orientation surface that explains page context, capability, cost, limitations, history, and safe return before input. | Opened the visible `TradingFlow AI assistant`; inspected its context, credit, composer, suggestions, controls, disclaimer, and existing history without typing or activating any content action; closed it. | The 380×656 docked panel moved main content to x=380 and labeled itself `TradingFlow AI` with `Option Trades · Historical`, `1,968 credits`, a page-specific prompt, two suggestions, `Select element`, and an empty disabled Send button. Its footer said replies use 1 credit, charts/deep analysis use 2, AI runs read-only SQL, recipes require review, and it is not investment advice. New/Clear/History were 24×24 and Close 28×28. History loaded after about five seconds into a 256×334 dialog with a 3,416-pixel inner list and 122 existing titles; no prompt was sent and Close restored the full-width workstation. | `R017-D1`: baseline account/build/route/date/time/rows; trigger and panel rectangles; exact panel copy, credit, controls, disabled Send, context and disclaimer; history loading/resolution, list and target metrics; post-Close main/overlay/focus state. | Strong contextual and cost guardrails; added `CM-UX-040`, strengthened `CM-UX-036` |

Its 768×384 card promised Telegram, Slack, Discord, and Messenger but rendered only Slack `Linked Jul 25, 2026` and Discord `Linked Jul 23, 2026`, two identically named Disconnect buttons, and `Generate link code`. Before generation there was no visible current-availability, 10-minute/single-use replacement, private DM/slash, shared-credit, tool/data, history, privacy, retention, or unlink-data explanation. | `R017-D2`: rendered menu items and route; Account Settings heading/subtitle and section order; before/after scroll positions; card/platform/button rectangles and exact text; disclosure-term inventory; deployed-version source classification only after UI capture. | Added `CM-UX-037`, `CM-UX-038` |

History fit inside a 243.2×317.6 dialog, but its 122-title archive used a 3,416-pixel scroller and 11.4×11.4 one-click `Delete chat` controls. | `R017-M1`: same-call viewport/document proof; header trigger rectangle; AI dialog/client/scroll/overflow metrics; every control rectangle; history dialog/scroller/count; first row/load/delete geometry and center hit test; no activation. | Added `CM-UX-039`, `CM-UX-040`; strengthened `CM-UX-036` |

Messaging Apps began at content y=1,332; after main scrollTop 1,268 its 358×512 card fit the 390-pixel viewport and stacked Slack/Discord clearly. Both Disconnect targets were 93.3×32 and Generate was 141.5×36. One Back restored Historical with 100 rows, `6,512,417 total records`, `Default`, no dialog/menu/assistant, and `aria-pressed=false`; final cleanup restored `1280 × 720`, the same route/build, and an empty Browser tab inventory. | `R017-M2`: same-call mobile shell/menu/route proof; before/after Account Settings main metrics; card, headings, platform and action rectangles; Back route/rows/total/overlay state; desktop restoration and tab inventory. | Responsive card and return are good; strengthened `CM-UX-036` |

#### What is good

- Keep one contextual assistant. The panel identified the attached surface as `Option Trades · Historical`, framed its empty-state question around the current page, and did not pretend to be a separate product.
- Keep cost and decision guardrails visible before submission. The current balance, 1-credit and 2-credit costs, read-only SQL boundary, recipe-review requirement, and not-investment-advice warning were all present without opening secondary help.
- Keep empty submission inert. Send was disabled with no text, and merely opening AI, suggestions, History, and Settings did not spend a credit or create a thread.
- Keep current connection state concrete. Existing Slack and Discord rows named the platform, showed `Linked`, and included the link date; opening Account Settings did not silently generate another secret.
- Keep the Messaging Apps card responsive. At 390 pixels the 358-pixel card, linked rows, explanation, and Generate action stayed contained, and adjacent Scheduled deliveries remained in a coherent reading order.
- Keep recoverable navigation. Mobile Account Center → Browser Back returned to a populated Historical workstation with no residual assistant, menu, dialog, or state mutation.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. Browser closed AI/history, restored desktop device metrics, re-proved route/build/default/rows, and finalized all tabs.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-037 · Medium · Assistant Channels are buried behind a destination that never names them**
>
> The visible AI panel offers no `Use in Slack/Discord` or Messaging Apps continuation. The user menu names only generic `Account Center`; after guessing that destination, a trader must pass Profile, Security, API Keys, and Connect an AI Agent before Messaging Apps appears 1,140 pixels down desktop and 1,332 pixels down mobile. This makes an already-secondary capability dependent on internal information architecture rather than user intent. A greenfield assistant would expose one contextual `Use TradingFlow AI in messaging apps` entry from the AI panel and a direct Account Center sub-navigation item. Acceptance signal: a first-time user can reach the Messaging Apps heading in one clearly named action from either AI or account navigation, with no deep-scroll search and a stable shareable section route or anchor.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-038 · High · Messaging Apps asks for an external identity link without an informed trust contract**
>
> The card promises Telegram, Slack, Discord, and Messenger but the current eligible/configured response renders only Slack and Discord, with no unavailable rows or reason. Before `Generate link code`, it says only that the code is one-time: the 10-minute lifetime, replacement rule, currently eligible destinations, Discord slash-versus-DM difference, private redemption requirement, shared web/channel credit pool, premium data/tool reach, exclusion from web history, retained channel threads, and the fact that Disconnect does not delete those threads are absent. This is not merely missing help text: the action binds an external platform identity to paid account data, while the user cannot make an informed platform/privacy/data-lifecycle decision. A greenfield flow would show available and unavailable platforms separately and put one concise security/credits/history preflight before secret generation. Acceptance signal: before any code is minted, the UI names the exact connectable platforms and why others are unavailable, code lifetime/single-use/replacement, private redemption command per platform, shared credits and accessible data, where channel history lives, what persists after Disconnect, and a path to delete retained channel data.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-039 · Medium · The mobile AI sheet keeps desktop sizing and paints controls into its backdrop**
>
> At `390 × 844`, the left sheet was only 292.5 pixels wide, yet its Close control ended at x=313.5 and the second starter action ended at x=365.5 because the 380-pixel assistant composition was not reflowed to the sheet. The panel explicitly allowed horizontal overflow, so actions visually and interactively escape the surface that owns them. This weakens hierarchy and makes backdrop-versus-action taps ambiguous. A greenfield mobile assistant would either use a true full-width sheet or reflow every header, suggestion, composer, and footer within the declared panel. Acceptance signal: at 390 pixels every assistant element stays inside one panel rectangle with zero horizontal overflow, starter actions wrap or stack, and Close remains fully contained with a 44×44 hit target.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-040 · Medium · Chat history is an unstructured archive with one-step destructive controls**
>
> The active account's 122 web threads resolve into one flat 3,416-pixel list of truncated titles with no search, date, page/surface source, channel distinction, or grouping. Each row pairs a roughly 15-pixel title strip with a repeated 11.4–12-pixel `Delete chat` control; deployed source confirms deletion calls `removeThread` immediately with no confirmation or undo. A novice trying to reopen one prior analysis can neither identify its provenance nor safely distinguish loading from deleting, and the product offers no channel-history view or deletion path at all. A greenfield archive would expose recency and source, support search/grouping, and use an explicit recoverable destructive action. Acceptance signal: history entries show title, timestamp, originating surface/source, and web/channel scope; users can search or group a long archive; deletion has a 44×44 labeled target plus confirmation or undo; retained channel threads have a visible lifecycle-management path.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · High-risk mobile controls continue to use sub-44-pixel targets**
>
> Round 017 extends the shared mobile-target finding beyond navigation and Watchlists: the AI trigger was 40×32, New/Clear/History 24×24, Close 28×28, chat deletion 11.4×11.4, channel Disconnect 93.3×32, and Generate link code 141.5×36. These are not decorative icons; they open overlays, clear or delete personal history, sever external identities, or mint a credential. A greenfield mobile interaction system must size consequences, not just visuals. Acceptance signal: every AI-history and channel-management action has an effective non-overlapping 44×44 minimum target, destructive actions are spatially separated from selection, and responsive layouts preserve those targets without overflow.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: TradingFlow has the right core concept—one page-aware assistant with explicit credit costs and guardrails, plus a deterministic Settings bridge to external platforms. The current first-use design fails at the boundary around that concept: discovery is buried, platform availability contradicts the promise, identity/data/credit/retention consequences are hidden until or beyond secret generation, the mobile shell does not own its children geometrically, and history management is unsafe at realistic scale.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-037` (Medium), `CM-UX-038` (High), `CM-UX-039` (Medium), and `CM-UX-040` (Medium). Strengthened `CM-UX-036` (Medium) with AI, history, and channel-management targets. Recorded page context, balance/cost disclosure, disabled empty Send, read-only/not-advice guardrails, linked-state dates, contained Messaging Apps mobile layout, safe mobile Back, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Assistant Channels and the global AI entry now have desktop/mobile findings coverage for discoverability, current already-consented assistant state, existing web history, two linked platforms, no-action exploration, responsive layout, and mobile return.
- What remains unknown: Never-consented, unpaid, exhausted-credit, no-history, unlinked, one-link, newly eligible, unavailable-platform, loading, and error states; code generation and copy; exact post-mint platform instructions; expiry/replacement and private/public-code handling; Slack/Telegram/Discord/Messenger redemption and message behavior; actual shared-credit charging; external response privacy; Disconnect confirmation/outcome; link refresh after external redemption; channel-thread retention/deletion; keyboard-only history management; true coarse-pointer behavior; screenshot appearance; production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 018 — test active Scheduled deliveries on desktop/mobile: inspect the visible `0 / 10` state, open `New…`, understand recipe selection, linked destination, cadence, timezone, delivery/credit behavior, validation and cancel/recovery without saving or sending.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, post-observation source-classification boundary, screenshot fallback, same-call viewport proof, mutation boundary, and report schema handled this round. The tab-finalization mistake is recorded as current Browser evidence loss, not promoted into durable procedure after one occurrence. Only this living report changed.

### Round 018 — Can a first-time trader understand Scheduled deliveries without accidentally creating one?

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 16:50 EDT (UTC-04:00).
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; begin from the active Historical workstation and re-prove the visible build before counting evidence.
- Why this round is next: Scheduled deliveries is the next uncovered visible Account Center surface after Assistant Channels. It introduces proactive, recurring delivery of paid market research, so weak destination, time, content, cost, or activation feedback could produce a higher-consequence first-use mistake than ordinary read-only exploration.
- User job: A paid trader wants one familiar TradingFlow recipe delivered on a predictable schedule, and must be able to understand exactly what will be sent, where, when, at what cost, and how to stop it before creating anything.
- Surface and entry point: Start at `https://testapp.tradingflow.com/app/option-trades/historical`; use the rendered User menu → `Account Center` path, then find `Scheduled deliveries` in the visible settings page. Open `New…` only as a transient creation surface, inspect it, and exit through its visible Cancel/Close path. Do not begin from a hidden schedule deep link.
- Persona/account proof: `active+clerk_test@example.com`; re-prove the rendered account identity, entitled Historical rows, current route, and build. Use only the existing schedule and linked-destination state; do not manufacture another state.
- Viewport and states: Desktop `1280 × 720` and mobile `390 × 844`, each proved in the same Browser call before accepting measurements. Cover the visible schedule usage count/empty state, creation-surface default state, recipe choices, cadence/time interpretation, target choices, validation affordance, activation semantics, cancel/recovery, responsive containment, and safe Back return. Inspect naturally rendered loading, unavailable, disabled, or explanatory states only; do not trigger delivery or external activity.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Scheduled deliveries is an Account Center-owned automation contract that turns one selected official recipe into a bounded recurring data-only digest. The shortest credible path is `Choose recipe → choose owned destination(s) → choose cadence and local time with explicit timezone → review content, next run, cost, and stop controls → explicitly activate`; exploration and cancellation must never create, send, charge, or leave a draft.
- Novice expectation: Before activation, the surface should explain what a recipe digest contains and excludes, that data resolves fresh at send time, which days count, the exact local timezone and next occurrence, every selected destination, whether email can differ from the account address, whether AI or credits are involved, what happens on market holidays or failures, and how pause/delete/unsubscribe work. The usage count should make the `0 / 10` limit and empty state useful rather than merely administrative.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove the active desktop baseline. Discover Scheduled deliveries from rendered navigation, record section position, heading, purpose, `N / max` usage, empty/loading/error state, first action, and any relation to Messaging Apps. Open `New…`; inventory labels, values, defaults, select options, target availability, helper text, enabled/disabled state, validation cues, activation wording, cost/content/freshness/timezone/next-run disclosures, and Close/Cancel behavior. Do not activate any save/create/run action.
  2. If the form can be inspected without mutation, change only transient in-form selections needed to reveal dependent copy or control states, then use the visible Cancel/Close path. Do not enter an arbitrary email, select a linked channel for delivery, submit blank validation through a create-capable button, or cause a server mutation. Verify the section still shows its original usage count and no schedule row.
  3. Re-prove `390 × 844`; repeat the visible Account Center discovery and creation-surface inspection. Measure document, card, dialog/sheet, field, footer/action, scroll, overflow, reading order, and touch-target geometry. Cancel/close without saving, use Browser Back to restore Historical, restore `1280 × 720`, and finalize all Browser tabs.
- Domain sources read by the operator: webapp `AGENTS.md`; `knowledge/basic_concepts.md`; Scheduled deliveries `domain-invariants.md` and `functionality.md`; `ops/webappp-fullstack/chaos-monkey-ux-review.md`; setup/safety guidance in `browser-e2e-product-review.md`; current runbook-maintainer, greenfield, and in-app Browser skills. A memory quick pass supplied only an older privacy-safe schedule-analytics note; current domain docs and visible Browser evidence remain authoritative.

Use screenshots for material hierarchy, clipping, or layout claims when capture is trustworthy, with exact DOM geometry as the documented fallback.

#### What is good

- Keep the concise section summary and usage boundary. `0 / 10 used` tells the account owner the cap without requiring a secondary quota page, and the one-sentence card scopes recipes, linked channels/email, and the two apparent cadence families before creation.
- Keep the v1 recipe boundary visible in the picker. The creation surface offered only the seven official templates, matching the domain contract rather than implying that every private draft is safely schedulable.
- Keep local and UTC values together. The `09:00` input paired with `13:00 UTC`, which is better than silently storing an unseen conversion; it needs a named timezone and next-run contract, not removal.
- Keep target selection explicit and fail closed when none remain. Channels were initially unchecked, Email was visibly checked, and unchecking the only target immediately disabled Save without a server call.
- Keep the form responsive as a whole. At `390 × 844`, the card, dialog, fields, email address, and stacked actions stayed within the document with no horizontal overflow or scroll trap.
- Keep recovery nondestructive. The dialog's visual exit failed, but Cancel never saved; reload/Back preserved `0 / 10`, and Browser Back restored a populated Historical workstation.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. Browser restored desktop metrics and the original active research state, then finalized both the working and screenshot-recovery tabs.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-041 · Medium · Scheduled deliveries is a deep, moving target inside generic Account Center**
>
> Neither the visible assistant, Cookbooks, nor the user menu names Scheduled deliveries; the trader must guess `Account Center` and scroll past Profile, Security, API Keys, Connect an AI Agent, and Messaging Apps. The settled heading began 1,548 pixels down desktop and 1,868 pixels down mobile. Worse, the desktop page first rendered schedules at y=1,140 and then inserted Messaging Apps above it, moving the target 408 pixels without a placeholder. A novice looking for automation can miss it or lose it while scrolling. A greenfield information architecture would expose `Schedule this recipe` contextually from an official report and provide stable Account Center section navigation/anchors with reserved loading space. Acceptance signal: one clearly named action from Cookbooks, AI, or account navigation reaches a stable, shareable Scheduled deliveries section; async sibling loading causes no material layout shift; the zero state explicitly says no deliveries exist and identifies the safe first step.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-042 · High · “Every day” and “Time (local)” do not describe when delivery will actually happen**
>
> Browser showed the default as `Every day`, `09:00` under `Time (local)`, and `13:00 UTC`; selecting `Weekdays (Mon–Fri)` changed no explanatory copy. The surface never names the local timezone, shows the next occurrence, or says that weekends and market holidays are skipped. Exact deployed source confirms both cadences are claimed-and-skipped on every non-trading date, so `Every day` is materially false. It also converts with today's local offset and stores only one UTC minute; because no timezone is persisted, a 09:00 local intent can shift by an hour after daylight-saving changes. A scheduled research product must make calendar and clock semantics auditable before activation. Acceptance signal: cadence copy says exactly which trading dates run; the UI shows a concrete next delivery with named IANA timezone, UTC equivalent, and holiday rule; DST boundary tests prove the chosen local wall-clock remains stable, or the UI explicitly offers and explains a fixed-UTC schedule.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-043 · High · An enabled default Save creates a recurring outbound job without an informed review**
>
> Merely opening `New…` selected Daily Market Recap, `Every day`, 09:00, checked Email, prefilled `active+clerk_test@example.com`, and left Save enabled. The only visible instruction was `Pick a recipe, a cadence, a time, and where to send it.` Exact deployed source confirms one Save immediately inserts a schedule whose database default is enabled. Before that action the user is not told that the digest resolves live, is data-only, uses no AI credits, includes a disclaimer/link, skips non-trading days, can partially fail, or how pause/delete and recipient unsubscribe work; there is no consolidated destination/next-run review. This makes a proactive external send easier to create than to understand. A greenfield flow would require an intentional destination choice and one review/activation summary. Acceptance signal: the initial form has no pre-authorized target; the final action is labeled `Create & activate`; immediately above it a summary names recipe/content boundary, human-readable recipients, cadence, named timezone, next run, no-AI/no-credit behavior, failure handling, and stop/unsubscribe controls; success confirms the active schedule and next run.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-038 · High · Schedule destinations expose transport IDs instead of verifiable identities**
>
> The only linked destination choices were rendered and announced as `SlackU07H1KA2` and `Discord67883100`. Exact deployed source intentionally appends the first eight characters of `platformUserId`; it does not show a Slack workspace/member name, Discord account/server, avatar, or any other identity the owner can recognize. For proactive paid-data delivery, a platform logo plus an opaque database identifier is not informed targeting—the trader cannot distinguish the right personal account from an old or unintended link before messages begin. This extends the existing Assistant Channels trust-contract finding. Acceptance signal: every selectable destination shows a verified human-readable account plus relevant workspace/server context and link date, with the raw ID hidden behind diagnostics; the final activation review repeats that identity exactly.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · High · Schedule Cancel, Close, and Escape all leave a blocking “closed” dialog mounted**
>
> On desktop, Cancel left the 448px creation dialog and full-screen backdrop visible and pointer-active with `data-closed`; explicit Close and Escape did not remove them. On a separately re-proved `390 × 844` state, Cancel again left a 358×637.5 dialog and a 390×844 backdrop that won hit testing. The schedule itself remained `0 / 10`, but the account page was unusable until reload; mobile Browser Back was the only clean return. This reproduces the shared dismissal defect first found in the Watchlist dialog and proves it affects a second high-value primitive. Acceptance signal: Cancel, Close, Escape, and backdrop dismissal remove every dialog/backdrop node after the declared transition, restore focus to New, retain the original scroll position and `0 / 10` state, and leave all account controls immediately operable on desktop/mobile.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Schedule creation repeats undersized mobile targets around recurring external actions**
>
> At 390 pixels, New was 36px high, Save and Cancel 34.2px, Close 30.4px, and each radio/checkbox only 12.35px inside a 19px-high labeled row. These controls choose recurrence and recipients, activate an outbound schedule, or attempt to escape it; their consequences warrant more than desktop-density hit areas. The dialog fits, but only by compressing every important action below a robust touch target. Acceptance signal: every schedule choice and action exposes a non-overlapping effective target of at least 44×44 pixels, with Save spatially separated from Cancel/Close and full-row cadence/destination selection that remains contained at 390 pixels.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The underlying automation is coherent—an official live recipe, explicit destinations, simple cadence, fresh data-only output, and reversible server state. The current first-use design does not earn activation: discovery is buried and unstable, calendar/time semantics are materially misleading, raw IDs prevent destination verification, defaults authorize a recurring email before any review, and the shared dialog cannot visibly cancel.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-041` (Medium), `CM-UX-042` (High), and `CM-UX-043` (High). Strengthened `CM-UX-038` (High) with raw scheduled-target identity, `CM-UX-034` (High) with schedule Cancel/Close/Escape failure, and `CM-UX-036` (Medium) with schedule-action targets. Recorded quota copy, official-only recipes, local/UTC pairing, no-target disabling, mobile containment, no persistence, Back recovery, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Scheduled deliveries now has desktop/mobile findings coverage for visible discovery, the current `0 / 10` state, official recipe/default form, linked-channel and email choices, cadence/time display, transient no-target validation, failed visual cancellation, reload/Back recovery, and no-save cleanup.
- What remains unknown: Unpaid/gated, unlinked/no-channel, populated-list, cap-reached, create-pending/success/failure, invalid-email, actual delivery, partial/all-target failure, non-trading-day, DST-transition, pause/resume, edit availability, delete confirmation, expired-subscription skip, recipient unsubscribe, target-removal, long-label/localization, keyboard-only, production, and screenshot appearance states.
- Next recommended round: Round 019 — test active Assistant Skills on desktop/mobile: understand the existing `1 / 10` state, scope across web/channel chat, activation and edit affordances, and safe return without pausing, editing, deleting, or creating a skill.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, screenshot fallback, same-call viewport proof, source-classification boundary, product-blocker recovery, mutation boundary, deduplication rules, and report schema covered this round. Only this living report changed.

### Round 019 — Can a first-time trader understand what an Assistant Skill will change before managing one?

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 17:08 EDT (UTC-04:00).
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; begin from the active Historical workstation and re-prove the visible build before counting evidence.
- Why this round is next: Assistant Skills is the remaining uncovered visible Account Center capability and the explicit Round 018 handoff. A skill is not ordinary profile text: it persistently influences every enabled assistant turn on web and linked channels, and can also be created or changed by assistant tools, so first-use scope and control deserve evidence before lower-risk secondary surfaces.
- User job: A paid trader wants the assistant to follow one reusable analysis or formatting preference, and must understand where it applies, what it can and cannot change, how it was created, when edits take effect, and how to pause or remove it without accidentally altering future replies.
- Surface and entry point: Start at `https://testapp.tradingflow.com/app/option-trades/historical`; use the rendered User menu → `Account Center` path and find `Skills` by visible labels rather than a hidden deep link. Inspect the existing `1 / 10` row. Open `Edit` on desktop and `New…` on mobile only as transient dialogs, then use their visible Cancel/Close or route-recovery paths without typing or saving.
- Persona/account proof: `active+clerk_test@example.com`; re-prove rendered account identity, entitled Historical rows, route, date/time state, and build. Use only the already-existing skill and visible rollout state; do not manufacture an empty, paused, unpaid, cap-reached, or error state.
- Viewport and states: Desktop `1280 × 720` and mobile `390 × 844`, each re-proved in the same Browser call before accepting measurements. Cover settled Skills discovery and section position, usage count, existing Active row, preview and action semantics, Edit form defaults and disclosures, blank New form guidance/validation, responsive containment, cancellation/recovery, and safe Back return.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Assistant Skills is an Account Center-owned, owner-private instruction layer that persistently shapes every web and linked-channel assistant turn. The shortest credible path is `Explain global scope and limits → name the reusable preference → write bounded instructions → review affected surfaces and Active state → explicitly save`; every row should expose provenance, last change, effective scope, and reversible pause/delete behavior, while exploration/cancellation must never alter the stored prompt.
- Novice expectation: `Skills` should be discoverable from the assistant as well as account settings. Before authoring or editing, the UI should explain that every enabled skill is loaded on every turn across web and linked channels; edits apply on the next turn; paused skills remain stored; skill text is absent from chat history/analytics; skills can influence emphasis/checks/format but cannot override data access, paid gates, read-only behavior, hidden SQL/system rules, or the no-advice policy; and the assistant itself can create/update/delete skills from chat only with an explicit, reviewable persistence receipt. The user should be able to identify when and from where the existing skill was created or changed.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove the active desktop baseline. Discover Skills through rendered account navigation; record the stable/async section order, depth, purpose copy, `N / max`, row title/preview/status, and accessible names/geometry of Pause, Edit, Delete, and New. Do not activate any management action except Edit.
  2. Open the existing skill's Edit surface without changing any field. Inventory dialog role/name, field labels and current values, placeholders, `maxLength`, visible counters/validation, Save enabled/dirty state, scope/next-turn/safety/provenance/history/analytics disclosures, and Cancel/Close/focus recovery. Open `New…` only to inspect blank first-use guidance, defaults, Save state, field and action fit, scroll/overflow, touch targets, and safe cancellation. Do not type or submit. Recover to Historical with Browser Back if needed, restore `1280 × 720`, and finalize all Browser tabs.

Cancel left the editor visibly active at 448×740 with a pointer-active full-screen overlay; reload removed both and re-proved `1 / 10` and `Active`. | `R019-D2`: dialog role/name and semantic snapshot; field values by length, placeholders, bounds, labels, focus and rectangles; counter/disclosure inventory; unchanged Save state; post-Cancel 1.8-second dialog/overlay visibility, pointer and rectangle evidence; reload proof. | Added `CM-UX-044`, `CM-UX-045`; strengthened `CM-UX-034`; no persistence passed |
| Repeat discovery, blank authoring, and recovery on mobile | Settled Historical at a same-call-proved `390 × 844`; expected route selection to dismiss navigation and a touch-safe authoring/review surface that could be canceled. | Used mobile User menu → Account Center, scrolled to Skills, opened the second rendered `New…`, inspected the blank form without typing, pressed Cancel, reloaded to verify stored state, then used Browser Back. | Selecting Account Center changed route but left the 224×264 user menu visibly mounted at x=150/y=54 over the destination until reload. Skills began at content y=2,100 after 1,828px of main scrolling; its card was contained at 358×360. The blank New dialog fit at 340×410 and focused Name; Save correctly disabled. The 2,000-character Instructions field was only 295×68, with `0 / 2000` but no Name counter or review. Pause/Edit/Delete were 32px high, New 36px, Save/Cancel 34px, and Close 30px. Cancel left the 358×432 New dialog visible and pointer-active; reload restored the unchanged `1 / 10` Active row and removed both dialog and stale menu. Back returned to populated Historical. | `R019-M1`: same-call viewport proof; route, heading positions, scroller/card/menu/dialog/field/action rectangles; semantic names and disabled state; disclosure/counter inventory; post-Cancel settled visibility/pointer evidence; reload unchanged-state and menu/dialog absence; Back/final desktop baseline. | Added `CM-UX-046`; strengthened `CM-UX-034`, `CM-UX-036`, `CM-UX-041`; blank guard, containment, and recovery are good |

#### What is good

- Keep the truthful one-sentence scope cue. The card explicitly says saved instructions tailor assistant answers and apply to web chat and linked channels; this is the right foundation for a more complete standing-instruction contract.
- Keep quota and current state visible. `1 / 10 used`, the skill name, first-line preview, `Active`, and separate Pause/Edit/Delete actions let the owner distinguish retained content from whether it currently applies.
- Keep the form semantic and bounded. Both dialogs had accessible names, explicit Name/Instructions labels, first-field focus, 80/2,000 character caps, realistic examples, and an instruction counter.
- Keep blank creation fail-closed. The mobile New form began empty and Save was disabled until both required values would be nonblank; no server mutation was needed to learn that state.
- Keep responsive containment. The 358px card and 340px New dialog stayed within the 390px document with no horizontal overflow; fields and vertically stacked actions remained readable.
- Keep recovery nondestructive. Visual dismissal failed, but neither Cancel persisted anything. Reload re-proved the same `1 / 10` Active row, Back restored a populated Historical workstation, and Browser finalized the only tab after clearing temporary metrics.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-041 · Medium · Skills is another deep Account Center target with no named first-use path**
>
> The only menu entry is generic `Account Center`; the trader must infer that standing assistant behavior belongs there and then scroll past Profile, Security, API Keys, Connect an AI Agent, Messaging Apps, and Scheduled deliveries. Skills began 1,760 pixels down desktop and 2,100 pixels down mobile—the final section in a 2,132/2,608-pixel account scroller. For a feature that can also be managed in chat, the product provides no visible bridge between `TradingFlow AI` and its persistent instructions. This extends the Account Center discovery problem found for Scheduled deliveries. A greenfield information architecture would expose `Skills` beside assistant controls and provide stable section navigation. Acceptance signal: a clearly named `Manage skills` action from the assistant and account navigation reaches a stable, shareable Skills section; the Account Center has section anchors/search; `0 / 10` explains the safe first step without requiring a full-page hunt.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-044 · High · Skill authoring hides an always-on cross-channel activation contract**
>
> The card says only that skills tailor answers across web and linked channels; Edit and New reduce the contract to `Give the skill a name and describe what the assistant should do for you.` They do not say that every enabled skill is loaded on every turn, that a new skill starts Active and applies from the next turn, that Pause keeps it stored, or that v1 has no per-chat, symbol, recipe, or channel scope. They also omit the core safety boundary: a skill may shape checks, emphasis, and format but cannot override data access, paid gates, tools, read-only behavior, hidden system/SQL rules, or the no-advice policy. Exact deployed source confirms those behaviors. A novice can reasonably author a broad instruction believing it is contextual, then see unexplained behavior across all channels. Acceptance signal: before Save, one plain-language review names `All web and linked-channel assistant turns`, `Starts Active next turn`, retained-on-Pause behavior, unavailable scoping, and capability limits; the final control explicitly says `Create active skill` or lets the user save Paused; success repeats the effective scope and stop control.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-045 · Medium · Persistent instructions have no provenance or trustworthy change history**
>
> The existing Active skill showed only name, preview, and state—no creation time, last update, origin, or actor. That is especially weak because settings, web chat, and linked channels can all create, update, or delete the same owner-scoped instructions. Exact deployed source returns `createdAt` and `updatedAt` to Settings but the row discards them; the schema stores no source/actor, assistant list output also omits timestamps, and Edit leaves Save enabled when nothing changed. Pressing that no-op Save would still bump `updated_at`, erasing useful recency even though the content is identical. When replies unexpectedly change, the owner cannot tell whether they, the assistant, or another linked surface changed the standing rule. Acceptance signal: every row and edit view show created/updated time and source surface/actor; Save stays disabled until a semantic change; successful assistant-side mutations produce a reviewable receipt and append-only audit entry; recent change/restore is available without exposing authored text to analytics.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-046 · Medium · Mobile treats a 2,000-character procedure like a tiny profile field**
>
> At `390 × 844`, the blank Instructions field was only 295×68 while accepting up to 2,000 characters. The form offered an instruction counter but no Name counter, structured checklist guidance, conflict/order preview, or full affected-response review. The dialog technically fits by collapsing the most important authoring surface: a trader can enter a standing multi-step risk procedure but cannot comfortably reread it before activation. A greenfield mobile editor would optimize for reviewing durable behavior, not modal compactness. Acceptance signal: Instructions exposes at least a touch-comfortable six-to-ten-line viewport with auto-grow or a dedicated full-screen editor; both limits are visible; long text can be reviewed without nested clipping; an explicit preview summarizes steps/order and clearly separates instruction content from the activation review.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · High · Account navigation and both Skill dialogs leave stale overlays visibly mounted**
>
> Desktop Edit Cancel left a 448×740 editor and full-screen pointer-active overlay visible after 1.8 seconds, blocking Account Center until reload. Mobile New Cancel left the 358×432 dialog visible and pointer-active. Separately, choosing Account Center from the mobile user menu changed the route but left that 224×264 menu visibly mounted over the destination through the Skills interaction; reload finally removed it. This is the third product area affected by the shared dismissal defect after Watchlists and Scheduled deliveries, and it now includes route-selection cleanup as well as dialogs. Acceptance signal: menu selection, Cancel, Close, Escape, backdrop, and route change remove every stale overlay node after the declared transition; focus moves to the destination heading or returns to the invoking action; scroll and `1 / 10` state remain intact; Account Center is immediately operable on desktop/mobile.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Skill management compresses every consequential mobile action below a robust touch target**
>
> At 390 pixels, Pause, Edit, and Delete were only 32px high, New 36px, Save and Cancel 34px, and Close 30px. These controls change or remove persistent cross-channel instructions, or attempt to escape an editor that already has a dismissal defect. The row stays compact, but accidental Pause/Delete or a missed Cancel has a larger consequence than ordinary dense navigation. Acceptance signal: every skill-management action exposes a non-overlapping effective target of at least 44×44 pixels; Delete is spatially separated and visually consequential; Save is separated from Cancel/Close; the layout remains contained at 390 pixels without shrinking targets.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Assistant Skills is a useful and internally coherent concept—owner-private, named, bounded, reversible standing instructions with clear Active/Paused state and cross-channel reach. The current UI presents it like ordinary account text instead of a durable behavior layer: discovery is buried, authoring hides global activation and limits, provenance is absent across multiple mutation surfaces, the mobile editor cannot support serious review, and shared overlays make safe cancellation unreliable.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-044` (High), `CM-UX-045` (Medium), and `CM-UX-046` (Medium). Strengthened `CM-UX-041` (Medium) with deeper Skills discovery, `CM-UX-034` (High) with Edit/New cancellation and mobile menu persistence, and `CM-UX-036` (Medium) with skill-management targets. Recorded truthful web/channel copy, quota/state visibility, accessible bounded fields, disabled blank Save, responsive containment, unchanged persistence, Back recovery, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Assistant Skills now has desktop/mobile findings coverage for visible discovery, the current `1 / 10` Active row, purpose/preview/actions, unchanged Edit, blank New, scope and disclosure inventory, provenance, responsive fit, failed visual cancellation, reload/Back recovery, and no-mutation cleanup.
- What remains unknown: Empty, paused, unpaid/lapsed, flag-disabled, cap-reached, multi-skill order/conflict, create/update pending/success/failure, duplicate-name, validation-error, Pause/Resume result, delete confirmation/result, assistant-created/updated/deleted receipt, linked-channel mutation receipt, live next-turn behavior, store-read degradation, language/long-label, keyboard-only, coarse-pointer, production, and screenshot-appearance states.
- Next recommended round: Round 020 — inspect the current active Billing experience on desktop/mobile: understand plan identity, price/renewal/payment state, cancellation/downgrade consequences, support/recovery paths, and safe return without changing subscription or payment state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, greenfield concept boundary, screenshot fallback, Browser recovery, same-call viewport proof, post-observation source-classification boundary, mutation boundary, deduplication rules, and report schema covered this round. Only this living report changed.

### Round 020 — Can an active subscriber understand exactly what they pay for and how to manage it safely?

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 17:25 EDT (UTC-04:00).
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; begin from the active Historical workstation and re-prove the visible build, identity, entitlement, and research state before counting Billing evidence.
- Why this round is next: Active Billing is the explicit Round 019 handoff and the remaining uncovered current account job. Round 012 saw only a mobile aggregate during fixture classification; it did not judge plan/price/renewal/payment truth, management ownership, AI-credit composition/history, or responsive first-use comprehension. Billing has higher consequence than another secondary read-only surface because ambiguous copy can cause surprise payment, accidental cancellation, or distrust about access.
- User job: An active paid trader wants to verify which plan is active, its exact price and billing interval, when the next charge or access change occurs, which payment method is on file, what Premium includes, how AI credits reset and were used, and how to change or end the subscription without accidentally mutating anything while exploring.
- Surface and entry point: Start at `https://testapp.tradingflow.com/app/option-trades/historical`; use the rendered User menu → `Billing` path rather than a hidden deep link. Inspect the settled `/app/billing` page and any clearly in-app read-only history surface. If `Manage Subscription` is available, open it once to inspect the Stripe-hosted landing page and ownership/return contract, then use Browser Back without selecting Cancel, downgrade, update, or any confirmation.
- Persona/account proof: `active+clerk_test@example.com`; re-prove the rendered email, entitled Historical rows, current route/date/time state, and build. Use only the account's naturally rendered active plan and credit state; do not switch persona or manufacture trial, past-due, paused, scheduled-cancellation, unpaid, payment-failure, or no-payment-method states.
- Viewport and states: Desktop `1280 × 720` and mobile `390 × 844`, each re-proved in the same Browser call before accepting measurements. Cover Billing loading/settled states, plan/status identity, price/cadence, renewal/end date, payment-method evidence, benefit/access explanation, management and history affordances, AI-credit balance/composition/reset/history, Stripe portal ownership and safe return, Featurebase suppression, responsive hierarchy/containment, touch targets, and final research recovery.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Billing is an account-owned subscription and usage contract whose app summary must make plan, price/interval, renewal or end date, payment method, entitlement consequences, AI-credit allowance/history, and external Stripe ownership auditable before any management action. The shortest credible path is `Current plan and status → next charge/access date and payment method → included access and AI-credit ledger → clearly separated manage/cancel actions with consequences → safe return`; app information is read-only, while every Stripe mutation requires a distinct, explicitly confirmed flow.
- Novice expectation: The page should answer `What am I paying, how often, on what date, with which payment method, and what happens if I cancel?` without sending the user elsewhere. `Active`, `trialing`, `past due`, `paused`, and scheduled cancellation must not collapse into one vague paid label. AI credits should distinguish monthly allowance, welcome credits, purchased packs, used/remaining amounts, reset/cycle date, source surface, and no-rollover/no-surprise-charge behavior. A management CTA should name Stripe or external navigation, preserve a trustworthy return path, and never make exploration look like immediate cancellation or purchase.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove the active desktop Historical baseline, open User menu → Billing, and record early versus settled page state. Inventory page/card headings, status badges, plan name, price, interval, renewal/end date, payment-method copy, benefits/access consequences, support and history links, AI-credit balance components/reset/history rows, loading/empty/error feedback, action names/hrefs/disabled state, semantic order, and geometry. Do not activate purchase, payment-method, plan, cancel, downgrade, or destructive controls.
  2. If `Manage Subscription` is visibly available, activate it once as a read-only ownership/return test. Record the external host, page title, current plan/price/interval, next invoice or access date, payment-method identity, management/cancellation wording, and return affordance. Do not click Cancel subscription, change plan, update/add/remove payment method, download invoices, or confirm anything. Use Browser Back once and verify the same app Billing state and account remain intact. Open Billing History only if it is clearly an in-app read-only surface; otherwise inspect its semantics without activation.
  3. Re-prove `390 × 844`; use the visible User menu → Billing path again, wait for the same account-owned state, and measure page/card/action/history hierarchy, target sizes, overflow, sticky/footer/vendor overlap, and information loss relative to desktop. Do not reopen Stripe on mobile. Use Browser Back to Historical, restore desktop dimensions, re-prove the original route/filter/date/time/rows/build/account, clear temporary viewport state, and finalize all Browser tabs.
- Domain sources read by the operator: webapp `AGENTS.md`; all of `knowledge/basic_concepts.md`; shared `domain-invariants.md` and `functionality.md`; current `ops/webappp-fullstack/chaos-monkey-ux-review.md`; Billing/account/safety and Browser setup sections of `browser-e2e-product-review.md`; current seeded auth fixture; current runbook-maintainer, greenfield, and in-app Browser skills. A memory quick pass supplied an older production read-only cancellation path and the durable rule that reaching a pre-confirmation page does not prove the cancellation lifecycle; current testapp Browser evidence remains authoritative and no remembered live price/state will be reused as current fact.
- Evidence to collect: Exact host/route/build/account/viewport baseline; early/settled Billing text, roles, headings, cards, status, plan/price/interval, next charge/renewal/end, payment-method and entitlement copy; AI-credit remaining/used/source/reset components and usage-history rows/pagination; every management/history/support control's accessible name, href/target, disabled state and rectangle; app/document/card/history client/scroll/overflow; Featurebase frames/overlays; one read-only Stripe portal host/title/current-plan/payment/cancellation/return snapshot if safely reachable; post-Back unchanged Billing proof; mobile same-call viewport and geometry; exact final Historical and tab cleanup. Use screenshots for material hierarchy or overlap when capture is trustworthy, with exact geometry as fallback.
