#### What is good

- Keep the credit ledger mathematically auditable. The displayed total exactly reconciled monthly remaining, welcome, and purchased packs, instead of presenting an unexplained aggregate.
- Keep cost and reset language beside the balance. One credit for a normal reply, two for charts/deep analysis, the August 21 monthly reset, and persistent welcome credits answer the immediate usage questions before a trader opens AI.
- Keep per-turn provenance and pagination. The 20 visible rows named date, time, source (`Web`), and credits, explicitly said most recent first, and offered Load more without exposing tokens or dollar estimates.
- Keep Stripe ownership and payment safety explicit. TradingFlow says payments are processed by Stripe and card data is not stored on its servers; the portal itself clearly separated Update, Cancel, payment methods, billing information, and invoice history.
- Keep the external landing informative before mutation. The portal root exposed plan name, exact monthly price, next billing date, payment method, and paid invoices without requiring a click into Cancel or Update.
- Keep the mobile content reflow. Cards, descriptions, history rows, and full-width management buttons remained readable within 390 pixels; the 16px hidden internal scroll-width excess did not correspond to a meaningful clipped text/action element in the measured inventory.
- Keep read-only exploration nondestructive. No billing or credit state changed, Historical recovered populated under the same account, temporary emulation was cleared, and all controlled tabs were finalized.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-047 · High · Billing contradicts the customer's plan identity and withholds the charge contract**
>
> TradingFlow called this account `Premium Plan` and `Premium access`, while the authoritative Stripe landing called it `TradingFlow Basic Plan`. The app also showed the 500-credit monthly allowance associated with the Basic credit tier, yet exposed none of Stripe's `$69.00 per month`, August 21, 2026 next billing date, or Visa 4242 payment method. It did not say what access or credits would remain after cancellation. Exact deployed source explains the omission: the Billing access snapshot keeps only price id/nickname and explicitly delegates full payment/invoice detail to Stripe; the badge falls back to `Premium` when nickname is absent. That implementation boundary is not a good customer contract. A first-time trader cannot reconcile product access, billable product, and AI tier before leaving the app. Acceptance signal: one canonical Current plan card shows the same product name everywhere, amount/currency/interval, next charge or access-end date, payment brand/last four, cancellation state/consequence, included access, and AI tier; app and Stripe values are contract-tested against the same price mapping.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-048 · High · The page buries subscription management beneath the AI usage archive**
>
> The title promises `Manage your subscription, payment methods, and billing history`, but the page orders Active status → AI Credits → three credit-pack purchase buttons → 20 usage rows → Load more → Subscription Overview. `Manage Subscription` began at y=1,726 desktop and y=2,124 mobile; on mobile, the user had to traverse most of a 3,048px scroller before reaching the first plan-management control. Exact deployed source hard-codes that order. Usage auditing is useful, but it is a secondary job and grows with account activity; it should not push the primary billing contract and escape controls farther away for the most engaged users. Acceptance signal: Current plan, next charge/access date, payment method, and Manage/Cancel consequences appear first; credit balance follows; Usage History is collapsed, paged in a bounded region, or placed on a separate tab/detail route whose length cannot move subscription controls.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-049 · Medium · Opening Stripe consumes the Billing history entry**
>
> The read-only portal was safe and had a `Return to TradingFlow` link, but ordinary Browser Back jumped directly from Stripe to the originating Historical workstation rather than restoring Billing. Exact deployed source uses `window.location.replace`, intentionally removing the app page from browser history. That makes the external return link a required navigation mechanism and loses Billing scroll/context for users who use the browser's most familiar recovery action. Acceptance signal: the Stripe handoff preserves a Billing history entry or opens with an explicit external-navigation contract; Browser Back and Stripe Return both restore the same account's Billing route, relevant section/scroll state, and refreshed subscription status without duplicating or trapping history.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-050 · Medium · Credit packs look like unlabeled price chips, not a one-time purchase**
>
> The AI Credits card presents `Starter — 300 credits · $19`, `Plus — 800 credits · $39`, and `Pro — 2000 credits · $79` as three 32px buttons with no `Buy` verb or section label. Nothing visible says these are one-time purchases, that activation leaves TradingFlow for Stripe Checkout, that pack credits persist, how monthly/welcome/pack balances are spent, or that exhausting credits cannot create a usage-based invoice. Exact deployed source confirms a click immediately creates Checkout and navigates with `window.location.assign`; the durable domain contract contains the omitted persistence, debit-order, and no-silent-invoice rules. Acceptance signal: controls read like `Buy 300 one-time credits for $19`, state `Opens secure Stripe Checkout`, explain pack persistence, monthly reset/no rollover, debit order, and the stop-at-zero/no-automatic-overage behavior, and require a touch-safe target plus a review/confirmation boundary.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-021 · High · Billing still carries third-party messenger chrome across its conversion boundary**
>
> This active round did not reproduce the prior notification/survey takeover, so the automatic update-popup suppression worked. It still left a pointer-active 50×50 Featurebase messenger launcher and separate badge fixed over Billing at x=1,210/y=618 desktop and x=320/y=742 mobile. The domain contract requires automatic popup, notification, survey, and messenger surfaces to be suppressed during Billing/paywall. Deployed source suppresses Billing update popups, but its floating-prompt route list includes Home, Option Trades, and Cookbooks—not Billing—and the launcher is preserved even on those routes. Payment/account pages already provide Contact Support; vendor chrome should not compete for attention or become a route for accidental disclosure. Acceptance signal: `/app/billing` and its return/error paths hide launcher, badge, notification, survey, banner, and automatic popup frames while preserving the explicit app-owned support link; route tests and Browser geometry prove zero visible/pointer-active Featurebase chrome.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · High · Billing route selection can leave the account menu mounted over the destination**
>
> On the initial desktop path, selecting Billing changed the URL and rendered the full page but left all five account-menu items visible with `aria-expanded=true`; the menu had to be toggled again before the page was clean. The fresh mobile repetition closed normally, making this an intermittent recurrence rather than a deterministic Billing-only defect. It extends the shared route/dismissal problem already proven across Watchlists, Scheduled deliveries, Skills, and Account Center. Acceptance signal: every account-menu destination atomically closes the menu before or with navigation, removes its overlay from hit testing, moves focus to the destination heading, and passes rapid desktop/mobile route changes without a stale mounted menu.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Every mobile Billing and credit action remains below a robust touch target**
>
> At 390 pixels, all three pack purchases, Load more, Manage Subscription, Manage Payment Methods, View Billing History, and View Plans were 32px high. These controls start paid Checkout, leave the app, expose payment records, or change subscription context; missed taps and adjacent actions matter more than compact visual density. The page did make management buttons 294px wide, but width does not compensate for a one-dimensional 32px target. Acceptance signal: every billing/credit action has at least a 44×44px effective, non-overlapping target; purchase and destructive/external management are visually separated; focus and pressed states are obvious; the 390px layout remains contained without shrinking controls.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Billing should be the account's concise, auditable subscription contract, with Stripe as the mutation processor rather than the only place where the customer can learn the contract. The current AI-credit ledger is unusually coherent and the Stripe root is informative, but TradingFlow contradicts the billable plan name, hides the actual charge/renewal/payment facts, reverses the information hierarchy, makes Browser Back skip Billing, and presents credit purchases without a purchase/lifecycle contract.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-047` (High), `CM-UX-048` (High), `CM-UX-049` (Medium), and `CM-UX-050` (Medium). Strengthened `CM-UX-021` (High) with active desktop/mobile messenger chrome, `CM-UX-034` (High) with another account-menu route-cleanup recurrence, and `CM-UX-036` (Medium) with Billing/credit targets. Recorded exact credit arithmetic, cost/reset/source disclosure, readable Stripe contract and mutation labels, mobile containment, unchanged billing state, Historical recovery, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Billing now has desktop/mobile findings coverage for visible discovery, immediate/settled plan/status, charge-contract omissions, AI-credit composition and history, management hierarchy, one read-only Stripe portal root, Browser Back behavior, Featurebase state, responsive geometry, touch targets, no-mutation recovery, and exact-build source classification.
- What remains unknown: Scheduled cancellation, past-due, paused, incomplete/setup, failed payment, no-payment-method, true trial, canceled desktop, no-subscription desktop, loading/error/retry, portal-session failure, stale-refresh, zero/low/expired credit, empty/long/multi-source usage history, Load more result, credit-pack flag-off/Checkout/success/failure, direct Payment Methods and Billing History deep links, Stripe Return-link result, plan update/cancel pre-confirmation and consequences, mobile Stripe, localization/long labels, keyboard/screen-reader/coarse-pointer behavior, production parity, and screenshot appearance.
- Next recommended round: Round 021 — test active Option Trades Saved Filter Sets and export on desktop/mobile: distinguish durable versus transient scope, inspect current-filter/column review and recovery, and validate a read-only export without saving, overwriting, or deleting a view.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, greenfield concept boundary, strict Stripe mutation boundary, Browser-only evidence rule, screenshot fallback, single-fresh-tab recovery limit, same-call viewport proof, source-classification boundary, deduplication rules, and report schema covered this round. Only this living report changed.

#### What is good

- Keep the toolbar's honest in-use label. `Filters, Default` did not change while ETF was only a draft, and reload restored the same label and checkbox state.
- Keep default and current use as separate visible markers. The simultaneous `IN USE` badge on `Default` and star on `Negative gamma · OTM calls` at least give the correct two concepts distinct representations.
- Keep Apply/Use explanation at the top of the editor. It truthfully says Apply updates, makes the selected set in use when needed, and auto-saves, while Use loads the server version without the draft.
- Keep the draft isolated from the live table. The ETF-only draft did not change rows, totals, route, date, time, or the in-use label, and a reload recovered the committed state without a server mutation.
- Keep descriptive saved-set names and separate Use controls. Names such as `Opening sweeps · $250K+`, `Earnings momentum`, and `0DTE index tape` describe a research intent more usefully than generic View 1/View 2 labels.
- Keep the responsive structural adaptation. Mobile uses real Saved Sets/Filters tabs, a bounded internal scroller, and a dialog contained within 390 pixels rather than squeezing the desktop split pane horizontally.
- Keep export's busy state and immediate feedback. The action disabled as `Exporting` and produced a success toast instead of silently triggering a file.
- Keep read-only recovery deterministic. The exact active account, build, Saved Filter label, represented window, 100 rows, total, and desktop dimensions were restored before all controlled tabs were finalized.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-051 · High · “Set as default” still changes the live workbench and protects the wrong row**
>
> Browser exposed the ideal test state without mutating it: `Default` was `IN USE`, while `Negative gamma · OTM calls` carried the star labeled Default. Yet the dialog never says that Default is only a future fallback. Exact source for visible build `01ad1e9` shows the stronger defect the safety boundary prevented Browser from activating: both the optimistic `setDefault` patch and successful response path feed the clicked id into `activeFilterSetId`/current `tableFilters`. A control named Set as default therefore also behaves like Use, contradicting INV-6A and silently replacing what the trader is analyzing now. The same source defines `isBuiltInDefault` as either starred *or named* `Default`, so Delete was disabled on the in-use `Default` row even though another row was already starred; its tooltip would tell the user to make another set default, a condition already satisfied. Acceptance signal: Set as default changes only one star and future fallback; the current `IN USE` badge, table filters, rows, sort, and columns remain unchanged until Use/Apply. Delete protection follows actual-default/last-set rules or visibly explains a separately documented immutable factory set, and current-state/domain tests cover the observed split state.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-052 · High · Apply can overwrite a complex Saved Filter Set without any dirty-state or review boundary**
>
> Apply was enabled in the untouched dialog and remained enabled after ETF changed from selected to unselected. The only visible difference was the checkbox itself: no Modified/unsaved marker appeared on `Default`, no changed-field count or before/after summary appeared, no estimated/current result count was shown, and nothing restated that Apply would overwrite `Default`. This editor contains categorical filters, 20+ numeric fields/codes, two switches, Reset, seven saved sets, and management actions; expecting a trader to remember every touched control is unreasonable. Header copy truthfully mentions auto-save, but awareness is not review. Acceptance signal: Apply is disabled until a valid delta exists; the chosen target set shows Modified; a review line names additions/removals/resets and says `Save changes to Default`; clear Discard restores committed state; Apply success names the saved set and settled row count; unchanged Apply never writes.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-053 · Medium · Columns silently use a different persistence model and a different vocabulary**
>
> The Columns menu offers direct checkboxes and drag handles with no Save, Cancel, undo, reset, active-set name, or `Changes save immediately` message. Exact deployed source confirms each visibility/order change mutates the table and queues an auto-save to the active Saved Filter Set. That is materially different from the adjacent staged Filter View, but the UI does not announce the boundary. Its list also calls the visible `OI`, `Vol/OI`, `IV`, `DEI`, and `DEX` headers `Oi`, `Vol Oi Ratio`, `Iv`, `Dei`, and `Dex`, increasing doubt about whether the same fields will be saved or exported. Acceptance signal: the menu says `Changes save to Default immediately`, uses exact table terminology, exposes saving/saved/error state, and offers Undo or an explicit staged Save/Cancel model; ordering and visibility recovery are testable per set.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-054 · High · Export presents an unqualified raw page dump as if it were the trader's represented table**
>
> The screen showed 100 rows out of 6,513,253, but the visible action said only Export and the toast only said the download should begin shortly. It did not disclose page-only scope, represented date/time, all-symbol scope, `Default` filters, selected columns, raw versus formatted values, filename, truncation, or actual completion. Mobile reduced it to a 40×36 icon. Browser could not expose the file, so no downloaded-content claim is made. Exact visible-build source nevertheless establishes the implementation contract: only the 100-row `visibleTableList` is passed, the filename is always `option-trades-export.csv`, the configured table columns are not passed to the CSV helper, and headers are inferred from every enumerable raw row key. That row type includes internal ids, raw option fields, hidden Greeks, timestamps, and frontend objects/arrays such as score breakdown and chain info; the generic serializer stringifies nested values rather than deliberately formatting them. A trader can unknowingly analyze a partial, schema-leaky file that does not match the table they reviewed. Acceptance signal: a preflight or explicit label states `Export current page (100 of 6,513,253)` versus a separately supported all-matches job; the filename and metadata carry session/window/scope/generated time; the schema is an explicit, documented projection of selected export columns with formatted/raw units; success identifies the file and row count; failure remains visible; artifact tests prove content rather than only anchor dispatch.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · High · Filter View and Columns repeat the shared stale-overlay failure on a core workstation**
>
> Desktop Close and Escape each left the 768×648 Filter View visible and pointer-active for more than five seconds with focus still on Close. Mobile Close left a 358×720 dialog and a full 390×844 pointer-active backdrop. The Columns trigger reached `aria-expanded=false`, but its 220×455 desktop and 220×431 mobile menus stayed visibly painted for at least three seconds; those menus became pointer-inert yet still obscured the workbench. Reload was the only safe recovery available without Apply/Use. This is the same shared defect already seen in Watchlists, Scheduled deliveries, Skills, and account navigation, now blocking the product's primary filtering job. Acceptance signal: Close, Escape, outside click where supported, Apply success, Use, and trigger-toggle all unmount the layer after a bounded animation; remove backdrop/hit testing; restore focus to the originating control; preserve or discard the correct draft; and pass rapid desktop/mobile open-close cycles without requiring reload.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Saved Filter and export controls are substantially below a robust mobile touch target**
>
> At 390 pixels, toolbar Filters/Columns were 36px high and Export was 40×36. Saved Sets/Filters tabs were 25.7px high; saved-set name buttons 15.2px; Use, rename, duplicate, default, delete, Apply, and Reset 22.8px; Close 30.4px; and column checkboxes 15.2px. These are not decorative controls: a missed tap can overwrite the active view, change the future default, delete a set, auto-save a column preference, or export data. Tight one-line packing also truncated `Negative gamma · OTM calls`. Acceptance signal: every actionable row/tab/icon/checkbox has at least a 44×44px effective non-overlapping target, consequential icons remain identifiable with visible or reliably discoverable labels, long names preserve the default/use markers, and the contained 390px layout remains intact.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: A reusable workstation should make four states unmistakable—future fallback, current in-use view, unsaved draft, and momentary session/scope—and it should export a declared projection of the represented table. TradingFlow has the beginnings of that model in separate In use/default markers and staged filter isolation, but its default implementation still changes live research, its editor has no dirty/review boundary, Columns silently switch persistence models, dismissal is broken, and export is an unqualified raw page dump.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-051` (High), `CM-UX-052` (High), `CM-UX-053` (Medium), and `CM-UX-054` (High). Strengthened `CM-UX-034` (High) with desktop/mobile Filter View and Columns dismissal failures and `CM-UX-036` (Medium) with exact Saved Filter/Columns/export target geometry. Recorded truthful Apply/Use copy, separate markers, staged-table safety, descriptive set names, responsive containment, export busy/toast feedback, unchanged recovery, and cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Historical now has desktop/mobile findings coverage for the resolved seven-set state, default-versus-in-use comprehension, one discarded draft, Columns review, one page-export dispatch, responsive tabs/overflow, overlay dismissal, exact-build classification, and unchanged recovery.
- What remains unknown: Authorized Create/Rename/Duplicate/Set default/Delete/Use/Apply mutations and cleanup; actual persistence across reload, Live/Historical, sign-out, and account switch; save/error/conflict/loading/zero-set/last-set/very-long-name states; keyboard and screen-reader operation; column drag, undo, save error, and per-set restore; actual CSV filename/content/encoding/nested values, browser/OS Downloads behavior, empty export, blocked export, dispatch failure, very large row values, localization, and production parity. Screenshot appearance remains unavailable because all permitted captures timed out.
- Next recommended round: Round 022 — test active Rank Contracts Saved Views, filters, session selection, and recovery on desktop/mobile without saving or overwriting a view; distinguish future default, current use, staged criteria, and runtime session state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: updated the chaos runbook's safety boundary so the companion product-review lifecycle cannot authorize Saved Filter mutations, and clarified how one scoped browser-managed export may be dispatched without turning Downloads or repository files into review evidence.

### Round 022 — Rank Contracts Saved Views, session context, and recovery

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 18:05:18 EDT (UTC-04:00)
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; enter from a user-reachable app surface, confirm the exact host, and capture the visible build before accepting evidence
- Why this round is next: Rank Saved Views remain untested even though they govern both Contracts and Symbols presentation. This is the highest-value open Rank state because a trader must distinguish reusable analytical intent from the current date, symbol/watchlist scope, active tab, page, and drawer before safely exploring or returning to a setup.
- User job: Understand which Rank Saved View will open on a future visit, which one is in use now, what its reusable payload contains, whether an exploratory filter draft changes live research, how changing the represented session interacts with saved intent, and how to return to the original Contracts workstation without saving or overwriting anything.
- Surface and entry point: Start from a normal signed-in app entry, use visible primary navigation to reach the Contracts leaf at `/app/rank/contracts`, and interact only with the rendered session, scope, Saved Views/filter, and responsive controls.
- Persona/account proof: Active seeded test account; prove `active+clerk_test@example.com`, paid Rank rows, the current plan/build context, and the current Saved View from the UI. No account switch is planned.
- Viewport and state(s): Desktop `1280 × 720`, then mobile `390 × 844` with same-call dimension proof, then restored desktop; latest-session success state, Saved View manager inspection, one unapplied filter draft and discard, one reversible session change if a prior session is visibly available, responsive dismissal/recovery, and unchanged final state.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: The workbench should expose one simple ownership model: a Saved View owns reusable analytical filters plus both tabs' columns and sorting; the mounted session owns date, all/symbol/watchlist scope, active tab, pagination, drawer, and refresh state. `Default`, `In use`, `Unsaved changes`, manager draft, and paused historical-only intent should be distinct visible states, and every Cancel/Close/reload path should have an obvious consequence.
- Novice expectation: Before touching a filter, understand what `Default` versus `In use` means, what will survive reload, whether the view also captures Symbols presentation, whether changing the session or scope changes the saved view, whether a draft is live, what Save/Revert/Use will do, and how to get back without corrupting the setup.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Reach settled Rank Contracts through visible navigation; record account/build, route, session/freshness, scope, Saved View/default/in-use/modified indicators, effective-filter count/chips, headline/table totals, pagination, and all visible persistence/help copy. Open the Saved View manager without choosing Use, Save, Create, Rename, Duplicate, Set default, Delete, Revert, Reset, or export.
  2. Inventory every saved row, marker, management action, manager tab/section, filter group, and explanation of what a view captures. Make one harmless analytical-filter change only inside the manager draft, verify whether it is visibly staged and whether the live cohort remains unchanged, then Cancel/Close or reload if dismissal is broken. Reopen to prove the draft was discarded and no Saved View or live filter changed.
  3. If the visible session control offers a prior completed date without warning of a durable write, switch once, wait for settled historical results, inspect whether Saved View/filter count/chips/default/in-use/modified state remains truthful, then return to Latest. Repeat Saved View comprehension, fit, target-size, overflow, and dismissal checks at a proved `390 × 844`; restore/prove `1280 × 720`, the original session/scope/view/filters/rows/account/build, clear temporary viewport state, and finalize every controlled tab.
- Domain sources read by the operator: webapp `AGENTS.md`; `knowledge/basic_concepts.md`; Rank `domain-invariants.md` and `functionality.md`; the chaos-monkey runbook; companion Browser setup/safety guidance; current runbook-maintainer, greenfield, and in-app Browser skills; older Rank Saved View memory used only as a potentially stale hypothesis and rechecked against current docs and Browser state.
- Evidence to collect: Exact host/account/build/route/viewport/DPR; active session and freshness; all/symbol/watchlist scope; Saved View trigger text and default/in-use/modified markers; manager title, copy, row names, actions, enabled states, filters, draft/dirty feedback, focus, dimensions, scrolling, dismissal; before/draft/discard row/card/filter counts; historical-before/after/return session and Saved View state; mobile document/dialog/control rectangles and overflow; screenshots only if capture succeeds; final unchanged state and tab inventory.
- Explicit non-goals: No Saved View Save/Use/create/rename/duplicate/default/delete/revert/reset action; no durable filter, column, sorter, scope, Watchlist, page-size, pagination, drawer, refresh, export, AI, notification, account, Billing, vendor, or external-service mutation; no Flow consent/load; no guest/unpaid, Symbols, cross-account, production, keyboard/screen-reader, conflict/error injection, or persistence certification; no repository Playwright execution; and no application code, tests, product/domain docs, configuration, or deployment change.

#### What is good

- Keep the settled workbench baseline explicit. Session, exact last-trade timestamp, market state, current view, effective-filter count, scope control, total rows, page, and current build were all independently inspectable before management.
- Keep the concise Saved View ownership sentence. `Save filters, columns, and sorting for both Rank tabs` describes the reusable payload positively, and the underlying manager separates Default, In use, and Use rather than collapsing them into one state. The transition defect currently prevents this good model from reaching a sighted user reliably.
- Keep the date control fail-closed. With only one retained session, it stayed disabled, named the sole date, and exposed the exact limitation instead of offering an empty or fabricated historical choice.
- Keep responsive containment. Both the 340.1-pixel opening geometry and 358-pixel closed geometry stayed inside the 390-pixel document; the failure was state/animation, not horizontal escape.
- Keep reload recovery deterministic. Every recovery restored the same view/filter/scope/session/rows/page/build and removed the blocking layer without persisting a draft.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · High · Rank Saved Views reverses open and closed visibility, trapping the core workstation**
>
> On two desktop reproductions, opening Saved Views set the trigger expanded, focused the Default row, installed a full-screen backdrop, and mounted a 972.8×629.3 `data-open` manager—but it stayed completely transparent, scaled to 95%, and pointer-active from immediate inspection through three seconds. The user sees the workbench but cannot click it. Escape then set the trigger false and the manager `data-closed`, yet made a 1024×662 surface fully visible and pointer-active for more than 3.2 seconds. Mobile repeated the inversion: transparent/pointer-active at 340.1×737.7 while open, then visible/pointer-active at 358×776 after Escape. A first-time trader cannot even reach the Saved View model, let alone review or discard a draft; using hidden locators would falsely turn a blocked job into a pass. Acceptance signal: open reaches stable opacity 1 and scale 1 within the declared transition; close/Escape/Cancel remove the dialog and backdrop from painting, hit testing, focus, and the accessibility tree; focus returns to the trigger; rapid desktop/mobile open-close cycles never require reload.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-009 · Critical · Saved Views turns the forecast-like GEX mapping into reusable screening intent**
>
> Once Escape made the manager visible in its broken closed state, the Contracts filter called its choices `Resistance`, `Bullish Target`, `Support`, and `Bearish Target` and explained that they come directly from symbol GEX regime times contract moneyness. This is the same unsupported mapping already present in the Rank table, now elevated into account-owned Saved View criteria that a trader can repeatedly apply. Persistence makes the misleading mental model more durable, not more valid. Acceptance signal: Saved View labels, summaries, chips, filters, and migrated tokens use the canonical descriptive GEX Context vocabulary only, never support/resistance/target or dealer-intent language; saved legacy values normalize without reintroducing predictive copy.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Rank compresses core mobile research and management controls below safe touch size**
>
> At `390 × 844`, Contracts and Symbols tabs were only 27 pixels high. Trading date, Saved Views, Clear filters, Refresh, Columns, and Export were 36 pixels high. These controls change the represented session, reusable analytical state, cohort, presentation, and exported data; they are not decorative. A greenfield mobile workbench should size consequential actions by outcome rather than desktop density. Acceptance signal: every tab, date, scope, Saved View, filter, refresh, column, and export action has an effective non-overlapping 44×44 target while the toolbar remains readable and the 390-pixel document stays contained.

> [!WARNING]
> **R022-ENV-001 · Alternate-session behavior could not be exercised**
>
> The paid Trading date control was disabled with `Only the latest retained session is available.` This round therefore does not prove historical Saved View behavior, paused GEX intent, date preservation, or return-to-Latest recovery. Re-run that slice when testapp exposes at least two retained sessions; do not infer it from source or older Browser memory.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Rank's intended ownership model is sound—Saved Views own reusable filters/presentation while the mounted workbench owns session context—but the shared overlay transition prevents a sighted trader from entering that model at all. The shortest credible product path is a reliably visible manager with Default/In use/draft states, explicit Save/Cancel outcomes, and session controls that remain visibly separate; predictive GEX labels should not be persisted as research intent.
- Findings added or strengthened: No new ID was created because the transition failure shares the established overlay root. Strengthened `CM-UX-034` (High) with deterministic desktop/mobile reversed open/closed states, `CM-UX-009` (Critical) with Saved View persistence of forecast-like GEX labels, and `CM-UX-036` (Medium) with exact Rank mobile control geometry.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Rank Contracts now has desktop/mobile evidence for the current Saved View/session baseline, manager-entry blocker, responsive containment, recovery, and one-session environment limit. Draft/discard, Save/Use/default/management semantics, historical behavior, and persistence remain explicitly unproved.
- What remains unknown: Manager behavior after the shared transition is repaired; filter draft/dirty/review/Cancel behavior; inactive-view selection; toolbar Save/Revert; Create/Rename/Duplicate/Set default/Delete/Use; both-tab column/sorter restoration; cold-load/default and reload behavior; conflict/load/save errors; symbol/watchlist scope independence; authorized lifecycle cleanup; actual historical-session and GEX-pause behavior; alternate-session data; keyboard/screen-reader behavior; screenshot appearance; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 023 — test canceled-user Rank Contracts and Symbols deep-link access gates plus exact intent recovery on desktop/mobile, restoring the active account afterward and making no billing or account-data mutation.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: updated. A semantic overlay is no longer treated as visible evidence when computed opacity/visibility/pointer state contradicts it; future agents must record geometry/focus/hit testing, try one ordinary recovery, and mark hidden-control journeys blocked instead of operating them through locators.

#### Browser Execution

The Round Test Plan above was written before Browser interaction.

- Browser began from the ordinary testapp root at desktop `1280 × 720`, DPR 2. The active session redirected to populated `/app/option-trades/live`; `User menu` proved `active+clerk_test@example.com`, and the shell showed `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`.

Later inspection proved that open Login, OTP, Paywall, and account-menu surfaces shared the same transparent/pointer-active failure. The initial canceled login is therefore fixture setup only, not proof that a novice could operate auth. Once the canceled session was established, its identity and no-subscription state were independently proved through visible post-reload User-menu and Billing content. During final cleanup, restoring the required active account also needed an explicitly scoped hidden-control exception because both Login and OTP remained transparent while open; those interactions are excluded from journey success and recorded only as account restoration.
- The canceled Billing page visibly stated `NO ACTIVE SUBSCRIPTION` and `Start your subscription today to unlock premium features, real-time data, and advanced analytics.` No plan, Checkout, Stripe, payment, history, or subscription action was activated. Ordinary Browser navigation and DOM/state inspection remained healthy. No Round 023 claim is labeled screenshot or visual evidence; layout and overlap claims use exact rectangles, document dimensions, computed state, focus, and hit testing.
- After Browser evidence, only exact visible-build source at commit `01ad1e9` was inspected read-only for classification. Login and Paywall both use the shared `DialogContent`; that primitive applies the same `data-open`/`data-closed` transition classes observed at runtime. Its additional closed-node safeguard targets `[data-state="closed"]`, while Browser observed `data-closed`; this is consistent with stale closed layers but does not by itself prove the full animation root cause. The same build explicitly derives `conversionOverlayVisible` from Login/Paywall stores, disables the Featurebase messenger, and adds a conversion-suppression class whose CSS is intended to hide injected Featurebase chrome. The live CTA overlap is therefore a runtime contract failure, not an intended coexistence rule.
- No plan, payment, subscription, account profile, Rank state, filter, Saved View, Watchlist, Featurebase state, AI turn, application file, test, configuration, or domain document changed. Browser ended with the active identity restored, a clean populated Rank Contracts workstation, temporary viewport state cleared, and every controlled tab finalized with `keep: []`.

#### What is good

- Keep the unresolved access state neutral. Both Rank routes retained their exact path/query while `Loading` and then `Verifying access` appeared; neither prematurely showed a purchase CTA or false denial.
- Keep the premium boundary before the research mount. Across both routes there were zero Rank tables, rows, drawers, filter controls, contract/symbol metrics, or prior-account artifacts while the canceled session resolved.
- Keep account status direct on Billing. `NO ACTIVE SUBSCRIPTION` was substantially clearer than inferring ended access from a disabled feature.
- Keep canonical deep links expressive. Under the active account, the same URLs reached the exact LGO Tradeability and SPY Gamma Exposure tabs with current-session rows, proving the product can represent the user's intended destination precisely.
- Keep the mobile paywall geometrically contained. Once the reversed transition exposed it, the surface fit the 390px document without meaningful horizontal overflow; notification and survey takeovers were also collapsed during the conversion state.
- Keep cleanup deterministic. Reload removed stale gate/menu layers, the active account and populated Rank baseline were restored, temporary emulation was cleared, and controlled tabs were finalized. The hidden auth interactions used only for that restoration are explicitly not counted as a UX pass.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · Critical · The shared overlay transition now makes sign-in and access restoration invisible**
>
> This is no longer only a stale-manager problem. Login, OTP, Paywall, Rank Saved Views, and account-menu surfaces all reproduced the same reversed contract: `data-open` stayed transparent while pointer-active, and Escape could produce a fully visible `data-closed` surface that remained mounted. A sighted first-time trader cannot enter an email, read an OTP form, choose a plan, or dismiss the gate through the visible open state. Reload clears the obstruction but also abandons the job. Exact-build source places Login and Paywall on the same shared `DialogContent`, and its closed-node safety selector does not match the runtime `data-closed` attribute. Acceptance signal: every shared overlay is opaque, focused, and hit-testable only while open; Close/Escape/backdrop make it non-visible and non-interactive before focus returns; auth, OTP, paywall, menus, and managers pass desktop/mobile timing tests without reload or locator-only recovery.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-003 · High · Canceled Rank gates discard exact, valid research intent**
>
> The app initially retained both deep links while verifying access, but then replaced them with generic `/app/home` and a generic paid-feature paywall. Neither LGO, its OCC identity, Tradeability, SPY, Gamma Exposure, nor the original route was named. After reload, one ordinary Browser Back stayed on Home rather than returning to canceled Billing or the requested contract. The same exact URLs later settled correctly under the active account, so this is discarded intent—not an invalid-link fallback. Acceptance signal: one pending-destination object retains path/query/hash and a human label, the gate says exactly what will resume, Close/Back consequences are explicit, and successful entitlement recovery returns to the exact drawer/tab after reload and account transition.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-021 · High · Featurebase violates the app-owned conversion boundary and steals part of the primary CTA**
>
> Canceled desktop Billing carried an automatic notification and survey alongside messenger chrome. More seriously, on the mobile Rank paywall the 50×50 Featurebase launcher overlapped `Start This Plan` by 22×40 pixels and won hit testing inside the CTA. Exact deployed code explicitly intends to disable Messenger and hide Featurebase frames whenever Login or Paywall is visible, so the observed frame is a live contract failure. Acceptance signal: Login, OTP, Billing, paywall, checkout-return, and error states expose zero visible or pointer-active Featurebase launcher, badge, notification, survey, banner, or popup frames; geometry and hit tests prove the app-owned primary/close controls own their entire target areas.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-022 · High · The mobile paywall still cannot explain the blocked job**
>
> When Escape made the otherwise transparent surface visible, `Upgrade to Premium` and its access explanation each occupied roughly a one-pixel-wide box. The only descriptive sentence was generic paid-feature copy; it did not name SPY, Gamma Exposure, Rank, or what would resume. The promotion and interval choices therefore precede a usable explanation of why the trader is here. Acceptance signal: a normally open, readable heading and two-line destination-specific explanation appear before pricing, retain at least the content width of the plan card at 390px, identify the pending research destination, and state whether the primary action shows plans, starts Checkout, or restores access.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Mobile conversion controls remain small and one primary target is partially unavailable**
>
> The paywall used 27px interval tabs, a 32×32 X, a 36px Close plans button, and a 40px primary CTA. The CTA's rightmost 22 pixels were additionally intercepted by Featurebase. These are not dense research-table affordances; they are account-recovery and paid-conversion controls where missed taps carry commercial consequence. Acceptance signal: every interval, close, and purchase/recovery action has a non-overlapping effective target of at least 44×44px, retains clear focus/pressed states, and passes center/edge hit testing at 390px without vendor interception.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-001 · Medium · Logout still forces an unsolicited auth job over public Home**
>
> Logging out the canceled account again settled on public `/app/home` and automatically opened `Welcome back` even though no gated action was selected. In this build the dialog was also invisible while open, so the user receives both an unwanted modal interruption and an unexplained interaction-blocking layer. Acceptance signal: logout returns to an orienting public Home with a visible, user-initiated Sign in action; automatic auth appears only when a destination truly requires it and is then visibly tied to that pending job.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: the correct system boundary is clear and partly implemented—route guards should resolve entitlement before Rank mounts, while one pending-destination object should own exact path/query/hash and its human label across auth, purchase, dismissal, and return. The current guard protects data well, but the shared overlay primitive makes the recovery UI unusable and the generic Home redirect throws away the product context that justified conversion.
- Findings added or strengthened: No new ID was created. `CM-UX-034` was upgraded from High to Critical because the shared transition now blocks visible sign-in, OTP, and paywall completion. Strengthened `CM-UX-003`, `CM-UX-021`, `CM-UX-022`, `CM-UX-036`, and `CM-UX-001` with exact canceled desktop/mobile Rank evidence. Recorded neutral entitlement verification, hard no-data boundaries, explicit canceled Billing state, canonical active deep-link resolution, responsive containment, and deterministic cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Canceled Billing/access now includes desktop and mobile; Rank Contracts has exact canceled LGO Tradeability gate coverage; Rank Symbols has exact canceled SPY GEX gate coverage; cross-surface continuity records canceled-to-active validation of both canonical destinations.
- What remains unknown: Actual post-resubscription return; plan-selection/Checkout/return behavior; guest, trial, past-due, paused, incomplete, and other unpaid Rank states; access-error/timeout/retry; destination restoration across a successful same-session entitlement change; deep links with hash or older sessions; keyboard/screen-reader/coarse-pointer behavior; screenshot appearance; production parity; and whether shared overlay repair alone resolves all affected surfaces.
- Next recommended round: Round 024 — test active Market Recap directory/article first value on a true mobile viewport, including date/freshness comprehension, responsive long-form reading, one read-only ticker-evidence handoff, Browser Back recovery, and clean desktop restoration.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: updated. Future rounds must check computed visibility before the first child interaction in every newly opened auth/access/billing/account overlay; any hidden-control use required solely for account restoration must be labeled cleanup-only and excluded from journey success.

#### What is good

- Keep the dated, prose-first article contract. The URL, full session dateline, UTC update time, read time, title/deck/tags, sections, sources, and disclaimer let a novice distinguish the represented session from the editorial update.
- Keep newest-first directory ordering and generous card targets. Nine cards were in descending covered-session order, and the current mobile card used the full 358px content width with a 224.25px target.
- Keep the mobile inline TOC. Unlike the previously tested Cookbooks report, Market Recap exposes a real disclosure with all nine sections; the one tested jump updated the hash and landed below the fixed shell.
- Keep body charts responsive even while repairing the appendix. The six charts pinned to prose sections fit the 358px column, proving the chart renderer can work at this breakpoint.
- Keep sources and the model boundary in the main reading column. Eight external sources used safe new-tab attributes, and the complete disclaimer remained readable at the bottom instead of being hidden in a rail.
- Keep one-step Back restoration. Even though the data handoff failed, one Back returned the exact date/hash and made the MU evidence link visible near the top rather than restarting the article.
- Keep cleanup deterministic. The active paid directory, desktop dimensions, and empty overlay state were re-proved before every Browser tab and temporary viewport override was cleared.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-014 · Critical · Mobile still turns unsigned Net DEI percentage into a bullish-looking score**
>
> The first-screen key stat says `Largest DEI · TSLA 169`, colors it green, and adds an upward-trend icon. The later prose finally says the magnitude is unsigned versus typical volume, but still omits `%`; the chart likewise plots bare 169/110/93 values. A novice is invited to read both direction and score-like magnitude where the contract defines an unsigned percentage. Acceptance signal: every key stat, chart axis/tooltip, prose mention, and accessible label says `Net DEI (%)` or equivalent; unsigned magnitude is visually neutral, while direction is a separate named field with its own evidence.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-009 · Critical · The current recap still converts modeled GEX into magnet, pin, and odds language**
>
> The article first acknowledges modeled exposure, then calls 7755 a `gamma magnet`, says SPX is `pinned between` walls, and says IWM short gamma `raises the odds` that small-cap swings amplify rather than fade. Those phrases turn a conditional estimate into a destination and forward path; the bottom disclaimer arrives too late to undo the reading frame. Acceptance signal: Market Recap describes estimated exposure, snapshot scope, assumptions, and conditional sensitivity without magnet, pin, support/resistance, known dealer action, probability, or predicted-path language.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · Critical · The shared reversed transition blocks the primary mobile menu**
>
> `Toggle menu` opened a correctly sized 320×844 dialog with all top-level destinations in the semantic tree, but the entire dialog stayed `opacity: 0` while pointer-active after 1.25 seconds. The test correctly refused to click invisible `Market Recap` and had to recover by direct URL. This expands the shared defect from auth/paywall/managers/account menus into ordinary product navigation. Acceptance signal: mobile Main menu becomes opaque, focused, and hit-testable only while open; Close/Escape makes it invisible and inert before returning focus; every top-level route is visibly operable at 390px without reload or direct-route recovery.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-055 · High · Every mobile appendix chart loses roughly 227 pixels with no recovery path**
>
> All six appendix figures computed to 600.9px in a 358px column and ended at x=616.9. The main surface knew it contained 617px of width but explicitly hid horizontal overflow inside a 390px viewport, so the right side of Sentiment composition, Flow vs cumulative, Move distribution, Intraday premium, Open interest, and Net dealer gamma was inaccessible. Exact-build source is consistent with an intrinsic-width grid item escaping its track, but the live geometry is decisive. Acceptance signal: every appendix card and chart computes at or below its mobile column width (or exposes a deliberate labeled scroller), every axis/legend remains reachable, main `scrollWidth === clientWidth`, and screenshot/geometry tests cover all stored chart kinds.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-015 · High · MU evidence still opens unrelated all-symbol Live data**
>
> The article exposed MU plus Aug 10 in its URL, then immediately replaced that state with clean `/app/option-trades/live`. Historical was not selected, the symbol control was empty, no date was committed, and 25 loaded rows began with XSP/SPXW/SPX. Exact-build source still writes the intent into query parameters even though the current product contract requires a clean one-shot internal handoff. Acceptance signal: the click lands on clean Historical, visibly commits MU and 2026-08-10 before rows count as settled, every rendered row matches the scope, and failure preserves the article plus a retryable pending intent.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-025 · High · The mobile directory can look complete while all recaps are still missing**
>
> The first post-navigation state showed the Market Recap title, description, ticker tape, and footer but zero cards, spinner, skeleton, progress message, error, or retry. Nine cards appeared only on the later five-second observation. Because the shell looks settled, a first-time trader can conclude there are no recaps or that premium access is empty. Acceptance signal: directory loading owns an explicit stable state in the card region, timeout/error owns a retry, empty is reserved for a confirmed zero-result response, and the final card count replaces—not silently appears beneath—a complete-looking shell.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-013 · Medium · Mobile still presents session and publication time as unlabeled peers**
>
> The newest card reads `Aug 10, 2026 · Aug 10, 18:06`; older cross-date cards such as `Jul 2, 2026 · Jul 4, 09:41` make it impossible to infer reliably whether the second value means Published, Generated, or Updated, and no timezone is shown. The article itself solves this with `Updated ... UTC`, so the directory is the inconsistent boundary. Acceptance signal: each card labels `Session` and `Published` or `Updated`, includes a timezone or localized-zone contract, and explains why the first card is the newest available session.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-036 · Medium · Mobile recap navigation and evidence targets are materially below touch size**
>
> The TOC's visible carrier is 40px high but its actual summary target is 16px; each expanded section link is also 16px high. The first MU evidence link is 24.88×18.5px, share controls are 32×32px, and the session picker is 170×32px. Dense text does not justify precision tapping for core navigation and evidence. Acceptance signal: summary, section, ticker, share, and session controls expose non-overlapping effective targets of at least 44×44px with visible focus/pressed states while retaining readable article density.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: the correct boundary is partly present—Market Recap owns one exact dated narrative, provenance, sources, disclaimer, and recoverable reading position—but its mobile layout and downstream evidence contract are not trustworthy enough for a first-use decision. The article should own neutral units and bounded structural language; an internal handoff object should own ticker/date/Historical state; the appendix should own a mobile-safe chart container. None of those responsibilities should be reconstructed from color, query strings, clipped plots, or a disclaimer at the end.
- Findings added or strengthened: Added `CM-UX-055` (High) for the clipped mobile appendix. Strengthened `CM-UX-009` (Critical), `CM-UX-013` (Medium), `CM-UX-014` (Critical), `CM-UX-015` (High), `CM-UX-025` (High), `CM-UX-034` (Critical), and `CM-UX-036` (Medium). Recorded newest-first ordering, exact session/update provenance, inline TOC behavior, responsive pinned charts, sources/disclaimer, one-step Back restoration, and deterministic cleanup as strengths.
- Coverage rows updated: Active Market Recap now has distinct desktop and true-mobile directory/article coverage; active mobile ticker-evidence continuity records a failed destination with good one-step Back; guest/unpaid access, session-picker/older-session continuity, and forced loading/error remain separate untested states.
- What remains unknown: Guest/unpaid Market Recap access; session-picker behavior and persistence; older-session and copied-link handoffs; reload at a hash; keyboard/screen-reader/coarse-pointer behavior; chart tooltip/legend readability after a responsive repair; forced empty/error/retry; screenshot appearance because Browser capture remained unavailable; production parity; and whether the shared overlay repair alone restores visible mobile navigation.
- Next recommended round: Round 025 — test the guest desktop/mobile shared ticker-search and Watchlist boundary: symbol discovery, result selection, temporary versus persisted scope, recent-ticker behavior, auth gating, and exact destination/Back recovery without saving a Watchlist change.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The current runbook and companion Browser guidance already required pre-interaction overlay visibility checks, same-call viewport proof, screenshot fallback disclosure, desktop restoration, viewport reset, and final tab cleanup; Round 024 followed those rules and exposed no new durable operator instruction.

### Round 025 — Guest ticker discovery and temporary Watchlist boundary

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 19:14:10 EDT (UTC-04:00)
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; prior visible build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, to be re-proved before sign-out, after guest entry when visible, and after final active-account restoration. Any build drift will be recorded before findings are attributed.
- Why this round is next: The active shared ticker-search/Watchlist workspace has desktop/mobile evidence from Round 016, but the corresponding guest row is still untested. The platform contract deliberately makes search broadly available while reserving Watchlist management for signed-in users and giving guests only a temporary default list. That boundary is a core first-use and conversion promise: a novice must understand whether an action discovers, filters, navigates, saves temporarily, or persists to an account before acting.
- User job: As a post-logout first-time guest, find `NVDA` from the public product shell, understand its official identity and the consequence of selecting it, tell whether any visible Watchlist or Recent state is temporary, identify the safe route to persistent Watchlist management, follow one non-persistent symbol continuation when the UI makes that consequence explicit, and recover through dismissal or Browser Back without changing Watchlist membership.
- Surface and entry point: Begin on the active desktop shell only to prove the current account/build, then use the ordinary visible `User menu` → `Sign out` path. Before any menu child interaction, prove the newly opened account overlay is actually visible and operable. If it is transparent, off-viewport, inert, or hit-test contradictory, perform only one ordinary Escape/reload recovery; do not drive invisible Logout, and record the guest setup/journeys as product-blocked. If sign-out succeeds, open the root URL and use only visible public Home, shell ticker-search, combined symbol/Watchlist workspace, safe guest gate, and Browser Back/Close controls. A post-logout guest is valid evidence for this round but will not be mislabeled as a pristine never-used browser profile.
- Persona/account proof: Start and finish with `active+clerk_test@example.com`; the tested persona is the signed-out state proven by `Sign in`, absence of authenticated account controls, and public `/app/home`. No registration or alternate account. Final account restoration must use the visible sign-in path when operable; if the shared auth overlay makes that impossible after a successful visible logout, one seeded-fixture interaction may be used only as a documented cleanup exception, never as journey-success evidence.
- Viewport and naturally reachable states: Desktop `1280 × 720` for visible logout, guest Home, search/Watchlist information architecture, exact result identity, one explicitly transient result continuation, gate/dismissal/Back, reload, and Recent/temporary-state observation. Mobile `390 × 844` for the same guest trigger/dialog hierarchy, fit, touch targets, visible navigation, and temporary/account boundary without repeating a result selection. Restore and prove desktop dimensions, active identity, a useful clean route, reset viewport emulation, and finalize every controlled tab.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: The official symbol catalog owns discovery; the current surface owns transient filter or destination intent; a clearly labeled guest store may own temporary browser-only recents or a temporary list; and the account Watchlist domain alone owns durable membership and the default list. A greenfield UI would make those owners visible through direct verbs and lifecycle copy, never use the same row/heart workspace to blur `Open`, `Filter this page`, `Save for this visit`, and `Save to my account`, and preserve the exact ticker intent through any proportional sign-in gate and Back recovery.
- Novice expectation: Typing `NVDA` should put the official Nvidia identity first, show what selecting the result will do before selection, and avoid suggesting that a heart or list survives sign-out/reload unless it truly does. If persistence needs an account, the UI should say so at the relevant action and explain what will resume after sign-in. Closing a dialog, dismissing a gate, using Back, or reloading should return to an honest public state rather than apply a hidden Watchlist change or lose the named ticker without explanation.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Re-prove the active desktop route, build, account, viewport, and absence/presence of covering overlays. Open `User menu`; before touching Logout, capture its open/closed attributes, opacity, visibility, pointer state, geometry, focus, and hit testing. If visible, activate its ordinary `Sign out`; otherwise stop persona setup after one normal recovery and classify the remaining guest journeys as blocked. On successful sign-out, open root, prove guest `/app/home`, dismiss an unsolicited auth surface only if it is visibly operable, and inventory public search, navigation, Updates, Sign in, and any automatic overlay.
  2. Open the visible guest ticker-search/Watchlist control, verify overlay operability before child interaction, inventory title/copy, temporary/default/current/applied labels, Recent section, management controls and gating, then type `NVDA`. Inspect exact result ticker, company, exchange, logo/fallback, option role/name/value, consequence label, nested heart/add affordance, and keyboard/focus behavior. Activate the exact result once only when its visible/accessibility copy describes a non-membership filter or navigation consequence; never activate a heart, add/remove, New, rename, default, auto-apply, or whole-Watchlist action. Wait for route/scope/gate/data to settle, then use the visible Close or Browser Back once and verify the named ticker/public context recovery.
  3. Reopen search and inspect whether the result now appears under Recent and whether the UI calls that state temporary, account-owned, or persistent. Reload once and open one fresh same-session public tab to distinguish visible session continuity from account persistence without inspecting cookies, local storage, profiles, or session stores. This tests only Browser-visible behavior; it does not claim cross-visit persistence. If an explicit `Sign in to manage Watchlists`-style action exists, activate it only when it cannot itself add/remove membership, inspect the authentication boundary before any child interaction, then dismiss/reload so no pending membership action can replay.
  4. Set and same-call prove `390 × 844`, DPR and document width; use visible guest navigation, open search without another selection, and measure trigger/dialog/list/result/gate labels, overflow, focus, Close, and touch rectangles. Restore desktop. Sign back into the seeded active account through visible controls when possible, prove the active email/build/useful route and unchanged Watchlist membership when Browser-visible, clear temporary scope where the UI provides an explicit action, reset viewport state, and finalize all tabs. Any browser-local Recent entry that cannot be visibly removed will be disclosed rather than manipulated through hidden storage.
- Domain sources read by the operator: webapp `AGENTS.md`; `knowledge/basic_concepts.md`; shared `domain-invariants.md` and `functionality.md`; `ops/harness/ui-style.md`; `ops/webappp-fullstack/chaos-monkey-ux-review.md`; setup/safety/Browser guidance in `browser-e2e-product-review.md`; current runbook-maintainer, greenfield, and in-app Browser skills. Prior validated audit memory supplied only the independent runtime-scope and fail-closed Watchlist cautions; current Browser evidence remains authoritative.
- Evidence to collect: Exact host/route/build/account/viewport/DPR; user-menu and every new overlay's semantic/open state versus computed visibility, geometry, focus and hit testing before child interaction; signed-out proof; Home/search trigger names and target sizes; dialog sections, guest/temporary/persistence copy, Watchlist names/counts/states, Recent inventory, management gate affordances; exact NVDA identity/role/name/value/logo and nested action semantics; before/immediate/settled/dismissed/Back/reload/fresh-tab URLs, scope labels, gate copy, visible data or no-data boundary; mobile dimensions/overflow/touch geometry; screenshots when capture is trustworthy and exact DOM geometry otherwise; final active identity/build/route, viewport reset, Watchlist/no-mutation evidence, and empty controlled-tab inventory.
- Explicit non-goals: No Watchlist add/remove/create/rename/duplicate/delete/default/auto-apply/whole-list application; no registration; no account/profile/billing/subscription change; no Saved Filter Set/View, table filter/sort/column/export/row-detail mutation; no AI, Recipe, Portfolio, notification permission, Featurebase dropdown/read-state, support/feedback, or external link; no claim of pristine-profile or cross-visit persistence; no cookie/local-storage/profile/session-store inspection; no direct API/curl, repository Playwright, Chrome, ego-browser, Computer Use, web search, application code/test/product/domain-doc/configuration edit, production claim, or external-service mutation.

#### Browser Execution

Execution-time adjustments and Browser evidence limits:

- The plan above was written and diff-checked before Browser opened the product. The existing active session was valid, so the round began at `https://testapp.tradingflow.com/app/option-trades/historical` with `1280 × 720`, DPR 2, a 1280/1280 document, 100 entitled rows, `User menu`, no `Sign in`, and visible build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`. Opening the menu itself proved `active+clerk_test@example.com`.
- The first ordinary `User menu` open was inspected before any child interaction. After 1.2 seconds, the trigger said `aria-expanded=true` and focus had moved into the menu, but the entire 212.8×250.8 menu at x=1038.4–1251.2/y=43.8–294.6 remained `opacity: 0`, `visibility: visible`, `display: block`, and `pointer-events: auto`; its center hit tested inside the invisible menu. The nested Logout row had its own 201.4×34.2 geometry and `opacity: 1`, but the transparent ancestor made it unusable to a sighted trader. Browser did not activate it.
- One ordinary Escape succeeded. The trigger changed to `aria-expanded=false`, but the same content became a visibly painted 224×264 menu at x=1028–1252/y=52–316 with `opacity: 1` and `pointer-events: none`; focus still reported the account-menu content rather than returning to the trigger. Reload cleared that stale closed layer. A second normal open after 1.5 seconds reproduced the original expanded, fully transparent, pointer-active 212.8×250.8 menu. Per the prewritten safety boundary, persona setup stopped and no hidden Logout was driven.

Those planned slices remain explicitly blocked rather than inferred from active-account behavior, internal documentation, or prior rounds.

Browser re-proved the active Historical route at `1280 × 720`, DPR 2, document 1280/1280, 100 rows, `User menu`, no `Sign in`, build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, and zero pointer-active account menus, then finalized every controlled tab with `keep: []`.

#### What is good

- Keep the underlying paid workstation stable across recovery. Both reloads returned to the same active Historical route, 100 rows, build, desktop dimensions, and signed-in shell without changing table or account state.
- Keep semantic trigger state and deterministic reload recovery available to diagnostics. `aria-expanded`, focus, geometry, pointer state, and hit testing made the contradiction measurable, and reload removed the stale layer without data mutation.
- Keep the review boundary strict. The guest claim was withheld when the persona could not be established visibly; no invisible Logout, cookie/storage manipulation, alternate browser, or inferred guest result was used to manufacture coverage.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · Critical · The transparent account menu now prevents any safe guest transition**
>
> On two normal desktop opens, `User menu` said expanded and moved focus into a 212.8×250.8 account menu containing the active email and Logout, while the ancestor remained fully transparent and pointer-active. A sighted trader cannot discover or activate Logout, yet the invisible layer owns pointer hits. Escape reverses the contradiction instead of resolving it: the trigger says closed, but a larger 224×264 account menu becomes visibly painted, pointer-inert, and retains focus until reload. This blocks sign-out, account switching, and every review or customer journey that requires an ordinary transition to guest. A greenfield overlay owns one coherent state machine: open means visible, focused, and interactive; closing removes interaction and pixels before returning focus to the trigger. Acceptance signal: after opening, the account menu reaches opacity 1 and Logout is visibly operable; Escape/selection/outside dismissal leaves no painted or hit-testable menu, returns focus to `User menu`, and a visible Logout → public Home → visible Sign in loop completes without reload or hidden controls.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: No guest ticker-search design verdict is possible from this round because the prerequisite persona boundary itself is broken. The higher-order product judgment is still decisive: account/session chrome owns identity transitions and must be trustworthy before discovery, temporary Watchlists, or conversion can be evaluated. A transparent open state plus a painted closed state is not a local animation blemish; it makes ordinary sign-out and account switching unusable and forces downstream coverage to fail closed.
- Findings added or strengthened: No new ID was created. Strengthened `CM-UX-034` (Critical) with two normal desktop account-menu reproductions, exact opacity/pointer/focus/geometry evidence, one Escape inversion, reload recovery, and the resulting guest-coverage blocker.
- Coverage rows updated: The guest shared ticker-search/Watchlist desktop/mobile row is now `blocked` by Round 025. This is not a finding or pass for search, temporary lists, Recent behavior, auth gating, navigation, announcements, or mobile fit; all remain unobserved behind `R025-BLOCKER-001`.
- What remains unknown: Every intended guest search/Watchlist question in the Round Test Plan; a truly pristine guest profile; whether visible Logout works after the shared transition is repaired; guest temporary-list lifecycle within and across reload/new tab/visit; exact NVDA result selection and Recent behavior; guest management gate copy and pending intent; desktop/mobile Back recovery; keyboard/screen-reader/coarse-pointer behavior after repair; screenshot appearance; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 026 — test active desktop Market Recap session-picker and older-session continuity: choose one prior completed session, verify article/session/provenance replacement, reload and copied-link stability, then use Back to recover the directory without following another ticker handoff.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing mandatory visibility check, one ordinary recovery, blocker-reporting rule, no-hidden-child boundary, exact remaining-coverage requirement, and deterministic tab cleanup correctly handled the failure; Round 025 revealed no new durable operator procedure.

#### What is good

- Keep the archive's ownership model. Directory cards, picker choices, clean dated URLs, document titles, picker values, datelines, update lines, headlines, stats, sections, sources, and disclaimers all agreed on the selected published session.
- Keep the picker as a real navigation control. It is semantically open, visibly painted, pointer-operable, keyboard-focused, correctly selected, and ordered consistently with the directory rather than acting like a cosmetic local filter.
- Keep canonical rehydration deterministic. The observed Aug 7 URL survived reload and fresh-tab entry with the same full artifact, and no stale Aug 10 headline survived the session switch.
- Keep history human-readable. One Back returned to the exact newest article and the next returned to the archive directory; the user did not have to reconstruct the path or lose the intended route sequence.
- Keep direct-entry progress explicit. Full reload and fresh-link entry both exposed session-verification feedback before content settled; that is the right conceptual contract to reuse for client-side navigation.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-025 · High · Client-side recap navigation briefly turns a known destination into a silent blank page**
>
> Picker selection and both Browser Back transitions immediately committed the correct target URL/title, but temporarily removed the article or all directory cards without a loader, skeleton, target-date status, alert, or recovery copy. The same product already uses `Loading…` / `Verifying session…` on reload and fresh-link entry, so progress truth depends on how the trader arrived rather than what the route is doing. Even though content returned by the 1.2-second sample in this round, a blank main region under a fully committed route reads as missing content or a broken archive, and prior rounds show these silent gaps can last materially longer. A greenfield archive gives every route transition one shared state contract: retain an inert previous artifact or reserve the final layout with a target-date skeleton/status, mark the content region busy, replace it only when the complete target artifact is ready, and expose the same progress/recovery semantics for picker, Back/Forward, reload, and copied-link entry. Acceptance signal: immediate and delayed samples for all four entry modes always show either the complete target artifact or explicit target-date progress/error UI—never a current route with an empty article/card region.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The core design is sound. A newest-first index, explicit session picker, immutable dated route, deterministic deep-link hydration, and chronological Browser history form a trustworthy archive model for a new trader. The unreasonable part is the split loading state: client navigation commits destination identity before it can render destination content, then provides less feedback than a cold load. The route owner should also own one coherent pending/error presentation across every navigation mechanism.
- Findings added or strengthened: No new ID was created. Strengthened `CM-UX-025` (High) with immediate and 1.2-second picker, reload, fresh-tab, and two-step Back evidence; the direct-load loader proves this is an inconsistent transition contract rather than an absence of available progress UI.
- Coverage rows updated: Added active desktop Market Recap picker/older-session/reload/fresh-link/Back coverage and archive-continuity coverage for Round 026. ### Round 027 — Active Rank exact-contract one-time handoff and reload boundary

#### Round Test Plan

- Date/time and timezone: 2026-08-10 19:38:51 EDT (UTC-04:00)
- Environment and build/version if visible: `https://testapp.tradingflow.com`; prior visible build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, to be re-proved from the active shell, Rank rows, and entitled Option Trades destination. Any build, current-session, or route drift will be recorded before the handoff.

Both rounds explicitly left destination reload and copied-generic-URL behavior untested. This is a distinct high-trust boundary because Rank promises exact identity while Option Trades intentionally consumes cross-app context only once and keeps shareable URLs free of workstation state. The user-facing question is not merely whether state persists; it is whether the product makes the transient/private boundary understandable before a novice trusts the address bar, reload, or a copied link.
- User job: Discover one currently ranked contract through visible desktop UI, open its exact tape evidence in Historical, verify the destination really matches the selected OCC identity, then learn—without internal documentation—what reload, opening the observed URL in another tab, and Browser Back do to that research context.
- Surface and entry point: Start from a fresh active testapp tab at the ordinary root or current paid shell, use visible primary navigation to `/app/rank/contracts`, and open one ordinary currently rendered contract row on its default Tradeability tab. Use only the drawer's visible `Open Option Trades` continuation. Direct navigation is allowed only later, in a fresh controlled comparison tab, to the exact destination URL already exposed by Browser; it is the copied-link test, not a shortcut into the journey.
- Persona/account proof: Existing paid fixture `active+clerk_test@example.com`, established without opening the known-broken account menu: visible `User menu`, absent `Sign in`, current build, settled paid Rank rows/drawer, entitled Historical rows, and no paywall. Naturally encountered loading/empty/error states count as evidence; forced failure, mobile, guest, unpaid, and production are outside this round. Finish on a clean active desktop Rank Contracts list or exact drawer and finalize every controlled tab.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Rank owns the chosen OCC identity and represented session until it hands off; Option Trades owns the consumed tape workstation afterward. The cleanest privacy-preserving design may keep the destination URL generic, but then the destination must visibly distinguish `From Rank` transient identity from the user's Saved Filter Set, state whether reload/share will clear it, and provide an obvious recovery path. Arrival must atomically commit all five identity fields and show only matching rows. Reload or a copied generic URL may intentionally return to the user's own default view, but that reset must never look like durable exact-contract evidence or happen without context-loss feedback. Browser Back should restore the same Rank contract, tab, session, and list context.
- Novice expectation: The source drawer names one exact contract; the destination visibly repeats its symbol, date, side, expiration, and strike and does not inherit unrelated Rank screening filters; the address bar and page explain whether this is a bookmarkable contract view or a one-time handoff; reload/fresh entry either reproduce the exact evidence or explicitly say why it reset; and Back returns to the inspected contract without manual reconstruction.

Wait for Contracts to settle; record route, session/freshness, market state, Saved View/effective-filter count, scope, total/page/row count, and one ordinary visible row's exact OCC identity. Require every sampled row to match the selected symbol, put/call, expiration, and strike. Inventory visible text for `Rank`, temporary/one-time, reload, share/copy/bookmark, saved/default, reset, and return/recovery semantics; do not infer them from source.
