Verify whether the exact Rank URL, OCC identity, Tradeability tab, session, table context, and scroll/list state return. Close the drawer only if needed for clean final proof, then finalize all tabs.

| Leave the fixture unchanged | Recovery returns to a clean active workbench | Clean direct-route recovery restored the same Rank baseline; no data, account, filter, view, or vendor state changed | Good |

#### What is good

- Keep the Rank list's explicit represented-session contract. Exact session, last-trade timestamp, market state, current Saved View name, effective-filter affordance, total, page, and row count are independently inspectable before a trader chooses a contract.
- Keep exact OCC identity reconstructable in both accessible and human-readable form. `Inspect VICE260821C00030000` agrees with VICE, 2026-08-21, $30, and CALL rather than forcing the user to infer a contract from a rounded shorthand.
- Keep the privacy-preserving ownership boundary in the documented design: Rank owns the selected exact identity, Option Trades consumes it once, and a generic URL does not leak private workstation state. That model still needs a visibly operable sheet and explicit transient-state copy before Browser can judge the user experience.
- Keep fail-safe review behavior. Refusing an invisible child prevented a synthetic locator click from turning an unavailable product journey into a false pass, and clean-route recovery left the paid fixture unchanged.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · Critical · The shared reversed transition now makes an ordinary Rank contract sheet invisible while it blocks the workbench**
>
> A new trader can discover VICE, read its exact 2026-08-21 $30 CALL identity, and activate an ordinary non-action cell, but the resulting exact-contract sheet remains fully transparent for more than ten seconds. The 1280×612 parent still accepts pointer interaction and its invisible `Open Option Trades` anchor wins hit testing, so the visible table appears available while a hidden full-width layer intercepts it. This is not cosmetic: it prevents reading the contract caveat, verifying the represented structure snapshot, or following the core evidence handoff. The same shared transition has already blocked account actions, auth/conversion, Saved Views, and guest setup; Round 027 proves it now interrupts the primary paid Rank research path. Acceptance signal: every Sheet/Dialog/Menu open state reaches stable opacity 1, scale 1, correct stacking, and visible focus within its declared transition; closed state is removed from painting, hit testing, focus, and the accessibility tree; an ordinary Rank row exposes a visibly operable exact-contract drawer and handoff on repeated desktop/mobile open-close cycles without reload.

> [!WARNING]
> **R027-BLOCKER-001 · Exact-contract one-time durability remains unproved**
>
> The planned Historical arrival, reload, fresh generic-link entry, and Browser Back checks were intentionally not performed because their only normal source control was inside the transparent pointer-active sheet. Re-run this exact slice after `CM-UX-034` is repaired. Do not infer success or failure from the generic href, consume-once implementation, older successful rounds, or unit tests.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: the documented ownership design is coherent—Rank owns exact OCC/session context and Option Trades privately consumes the five-field intent once—but the visible source boundary currently fails before that model can serve a human. Fix the shared overlay state first; then the destination should visibly label transient `From Rank` context, explain reload/share reset, and provide return or private-save recovery without putting workstation state in the URL.
- Findings added or strengthened: no new ID was created. Strengthened `CM-UX-034` (Critical) with a current paid core-research reproduction: exact Rank route and semantics mounted, while the 1280×612 parent stayed transparent, pointer-active, and child-hit-testable beyond ten seconds.
- Coverage rows updated: current desktop Rank drawer/handoff coverage and exact-contract continuity now carry findings, and the distinct one-time reload/fresh-link boundary is explicitly `blocked`. Round 004 remains valid historical success evidence; it is not current-build proof.
- What remains unknown: visibly operable VICE drawer content; exact five-field Historical arrival; sampled-row equality; transient `From Rank` versus Saved Filter Set labeling; same-URL reload behavior; fresh generic-link reset; reset feedback; Browser Back restoration; close/Escape behavior through a user-visible sheet; Forward behavior; mobile parity; slow/error behavior; screenshot appearance; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 028 — test active Cookbooks private-versus-official report entry semantics, represented session/provenance, and Browser Back using existing reports only, without recipe edit, Run, save, AI, or account actions.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing plan-before-Browser, computed-visibility reconciliation, hidden-control stop rule, blocker recording, clean recovery, source-after-evidence boundary, and deterministic tab cleanup all worked as intended; Round 027 revealed no new durable operator procedure.

#### What is good

- Keep identity isolation at the data/router boundary. The opaque private ref and official slug resolved to two genuinely distinct documents; the user's edited fork did not shadow or overwrite the current official template.
- Keep the current official Vol Surface interpretation contract. `Vendor HV — window unspecified`, `Not IV30 − RV20`, the provider-method caveat, uncertainty framing, and explicit no-trader-identity skew explanation match the documented metric boundary and are materially safer for a novice.
- Keep complete settled report evidence. Both routes produced the same represented date, useful reader params, four chapters, 19-row term table, 15-row cross-section, charts, methodology, and canonical disclaimer rather than silently dropping unsupported content.
- Keep native history restoration. One ordinary Back after each report returned to the exact source scroll and card neighborhood with no extra hash/history step, reconstruction, or gallery reload gap.
- Keep the review boundary fail-safe. The visible private `View report` path was enough to compare ownership; edit/delete/Run/date/params/AI were unnecessary and remained untouched.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-056 · High · A private fork loses its provenance and can look like the current official report**
>
> The gallery clearly marked the chosen Vol Surface card `Private` and `Forked · Jul 22, 2026`, but its destination removed all Private, Forked, source-template, owner, visibility, and updated markers. The matching official destination also omitted `Official template`. Both showed the same title, Aug 10 date, controls, tables, charts, method position, and disclaimer; a novice would have to decode `r_6dd…` versus `vol-surface` or notice prose differences to know which authority they were reading. That difference matters: the private fork retained older `HV · Realized`, `IV − HV`, catalyst, and positioning claims while the current official report correctly disclosed Vendor HV's unspecified method and avoided trader-identity/event inference. A user-owned report may legitimately diverge, but it must not visually inherit official authority. Acceptance signal: every report header names `My recipe · Private` or `Official template`; a fork names and links its source plus its updated/version date; a materially diverged or older fork offers a non-destructive `Compare with current template` status; Browser/tab titles are human-specific; no user must infer ownership from an opaque ref.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-025 · High · Private and official live reports still begin as a silent footer-only route**
>
> The private Vol Surface route had no report heading, table, loading/status copy, alert, or recovery through 1.75 seconds; the official counterpart had the same empty main contract through 1.4 seconds. Both had already committed their final URL and browser title. Full reports appeared shortly afterward, but the user sees no report identity, requested session, progress, bounded wait, or failure path during the interval. This repeats the seven-second Daily Market Recap evidence and contradicts the current Cookbooks promise of announced route and live-resolution pending states. Acceptance signal: every private/official/latest/pinned report route immediately paints and announces the requested report plus session, marks the content region busy, preserves final layout with a labeled skeleton or inert prior result, and always terminates in complete content or explicit Run/Back recovery—never footer-only main content.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-023 · High · Eighteen private recipes still displace the curated starting point**
>
> `New recipe` and all 18 private cards remained above `Official templates`; the curated heading began at y=1,483 in a 656-pixel desktop scroller, and the matching official Vol Surface action required scroll 2,264. A novice comparing a fork with its authoritative source must traverse the entire personal archive first. Acceptance signal: official questions/templates and current-source comparisons are available in the opening viewport or one ordinary scroll regardless of private-recipe count, while personal work remains one explicit tab/filter away.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-024 · Medium · `Open` still changes from editor entry to report viewing by section**
>
> Every private card used `View report` for the read-only route and `Open` for `/edit`; every official card used `Open` for its read-only report. The same verb therefore means durable authoring entry in My recipes and safe reading in Official templates. The successful private/official comparison proves the product already has the clearer noun. Acceptance signal: all read-only destinations use `View report`; all mutation-capable destinations use `Edit` or `Open editor`; no label changes task type because the card belongs to another section.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Cookbooks has the correct internal identity model and the official Vol Surface now demonstrates excellent metric caveats, but the customer-facing model stops at the gallery. Report headers should carry authority, ownership, source, update/version, and represented-session status across navigation; opaque refs are implementation detail, not information architecture. Loading is part of that identity contract and cannot temporarily erase it.
- Findings added or strengthened: added `CM-UX-056` (High) for private-versus-official destination provenance and stale-fork authority risk. Strengthened `CM-UX-025` (High) with two current footer-only pending routes, `CM-UX-023` (High) with current private-count/official-scroll evidence, and `CM-UX-024` (Medium) with the exact matching-report comparison.
- Coverage rows updated: added active desktop private-versus-official report identity coverage and expanded Cookbooks continuity. Distinct ref isolation, current official Vendor-HV language, complete outputs, disclaimer, exact one-Back restoration, and cleanup are good; provenance and pending-state truthfulness have findings.
- What remains unknown: latest-versus-pinned labeling; date-picker/calendar semantics; canonical pinned URL and reload/fresh-entry behavior; explicit Run freshness/loading/error feedback; whether private forks can compare/rebase against current templates without overwriting user work; other legacy/custom/private documents; unsupported/newer-schema placeholders; guest/unpaid report access; mobile provenance; screen-reader pending announcements beyond the observed empty status tree; forced timeout/offline recovery; screenshot appearance; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 029 — test one official Cookbooks date-picker/pinned-session journey end to end: latest versus historical labeling, loading, canonical URL, reload/fresh entry, one explicit Run freshness cycle, and Back recovery, without AI, edit, save, or recipe mutation.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing private-versus-official planning, hidden-authoring boundary, immediate/settled sampling, source-after-Browser classification, exact Back-state proof, and deterministic tab cleanup exposed the issues without any new durable operator procedure.

#### What is good

- Keep the exact represented date and complete settled evidence. The default report named Aug 10 and delivered the expected params, chapters, 19-row/15-row tables, charts, method limitations, and canonical disclaimer.
- Keep the internal route model. Source classification shows a historical selection would receive a distinct `slug~date` identity while latest keeps the stable slug; the blocker occurred before that boundary and no guessed route was needed.
- Keep native gallery history. One ordinary Back repeatedly restores the exact official-card scroll and visible action without a gallery reload gap.
- Keep deterministic trigger dismissal. Even though the open state was unpainted, a second ordinary trigger click cleanly collapsed and unmounted it, returned focus, and allowed safe recovery without reload.
- Keep the chaos boundary fail-safe. The semantic calendar made it technically possible to click Aug 7, but the review correctly treated parent opacity as user truth and left the pinned/Run journey unclaimed and the fixture untouched.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · Critical · The shared transparent overlay now blocks Cookbook history at its source**
>
> The visible Aug 10 trigger expanded, focused the calendar, exposed an enabled Aug 7 in the semantic tree, and mounted a correctly positioned dialog that won hit testing—but the entire 235.6×313.8 parent stayed at opacity 0 for three seconds. Closing through the visible trigger worked; reopening once reproduced the transparent pointer-active state after 1.2 seconds. A sighted trader cannot choose any prior session, and the overlay can intercept the report while appearing absent. This is the same shared root already blocking sign-in, paywalls, managers, mobile navigation, account setup, and Rank drawers; a new ID would hide its cross-product severity. Acceptance signal: every shared popover/dialog/sheet reaches stable opacity 1, scale 1, visible focus, and child hit targets while open; closing makes it absent and inert before focus returns; the Cookbook date picker visibly selects an enabled trading session without locator-only interaction on desktop and mobile.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-025 · High · The latest official report still resolves behind an unlabeled footer-only shell**
>
> The final Vol Surface route and browser title were already committed, but through 2.145 seconds its main region offered only the footer and nine pulse blocks—no report name, requested latest/date identity, busy semantics, status, bounded wait, or recovery. Complete content appeared at 2.565 seconds, so this was not missing data; it was an unannounced two-phase wait. A novice can reasonably believe the report failed or that the footer is the whole page, and assistive technology receives no meaningful pending contract. Acceptance signal: route-code and live-resolution phases immediately preserve and announce the requested report plus `Latest completed` or historical date, mark the report busy, keep stable inert structure, and terminate in complete content or explicit Run/Back recovery rather than an anonymous footer.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-057 · Medium · A bare date never says whether the report is latest or historical**
>
> The default report displayed `Aug 10, 2026` twice, but nowhere said `Latest completed`, `Historical`, `Selected`, `As of`, or whether this clean URL would advance on the next completed session. Its two visible `latest` words described the cross-section and chain snapshot, not report mode. The gallery had explained latest-versus-past before navigation, but the destination did not carry that mental model; a direct or shared visitor would have to infer mode from `vol-surface` versus an internal `~date` suffix. Exact date prevents total ambiguity, yet missing mode weakens freshness trust and future link behavior. Acceptance signal: the report header and date control expose `Latest completed · Aug 10, 2026` or `Historical · Aug 7, 2026`, use that same mode in loading/error/Run completion feedback and the accessible name, and explain whether the URL follows latest or remains pinned without requiring route decoding.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Cookbooks has the correct latent identity model—latest and pinned are different route/query states—but the user cannot reach or interpret that model reliably. Session mode belongs beside the report date through pending, success, refresh, reload, and error; the shared overlay must visibly mediate the only transition into history. A calendar in the semantic tree and a mode in analytics are not customer-facing state.
- Findings added or strengthened: added `CM-UX-057` (Medium) for represented-session mode ambiguity. Strengthened `CM-UX-034` (Critical) with a seventh major user job blocked by the shared transparent transition and `CM-UX-025` (High) with a measured 2.145-second unlabeled latest-report pending interval. No finding was filed for past weekends being semantically enabled because the transparent parent prevented a valid sighted-calendar journey; non-trading-date behavior remains untested.
- Coverage rows updated: added a blocked active-desktop latest-versus-pinned lifecycle row and expanded Cookbooks/report continuity. Latest settled content, exact date, internal route separation, direct Back restoration, safe dismissal, and cleanup are good; historical selection, pinned URL, reload/fresh entry, and pinned Run are explicitly unproved.
- What remains unknown: any visible historical selection; whether past weekends/holidays fail safely; pinned mode/date copy, canonical URL, reload, fresh entry, output equality, params, and disclaimer; explicit latest or pinned Run busy/disabled/completion/failure semantics; report timeout/offline recovery; other official/private recipes; mobile calendar/session labeling; keyboard/screen-reader behavior after a visible-overlay repair; screenshot appearance; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 030 — test the official Cookbook latest-session explicit `Run` lifecycle plus same-route reload/fresh entry: busy and disabled feedback, preserved report identity, termination, freshness wording, content stability, and Back recovery, without opening the blocked date picker or changing params, AI, recipes, or account state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, overlay visibility reconciliation, single ordinary retry, hidden-child stop rule, exact blocker handoff, source-after-Browser boundary, no-screenshot evidence label, and deterministic cleanup rules handled this round without a new durable operator procedure.

Two enabled controls were labeled `Run`: the header action at x=728.3/y=191 and the parameter-bar action at x=1,178.3/y=438, each 69.7×32 pixels. The round targeted only the first button through its `recipe-header-actions` owner.
- One ordinary activation of the exact header Run created one resolver request. At the 0.284-second pending sample, only that button was disabled and its icon had become one spinner while its text stayed `Run`; focus had moved to `BODY`. The full report heading, Aug 10 date, params, both tables with 19/15 rows, first/last row identities, method note, disclaimer, URL, and browser title all stayed visible and exact. There was no report-level `aria-busy`, refreshing text, toast, alert, updated/as-of text, or completion target; the only shared status text collected from the page remained `Cookbooks`.
- The explicit Run had terminated by 2.144 seconds. The header button was enabled again and its spinner was gone, but the screen was otherwise identical to baseline: same clean URL, title, date, headings, params, table arrays, method note, and disclaimer, with no `Updated`, `Fresh`, `Completed`, success toast, or completion announcement. No second Run or retry was issued.
- One reload of the exact clean URL exposed a different multi-phase contract. At 0.276 seconds it announced `Loading…` and `Verifying session…` over session skeletons. Later samples lost those meaningful labels: at 1.858 seconds only the generic shared `Cookbooks` status remained, and at 2.120 and 2.382 seconds the main region again held only the footer plus nine pulse blocks with no report heading or report-owned busy state. Complete content appeared at 3.674 seconds.
- A second fresh in-app Browser tab then navigated to the exact observed clean URL. It began with `Loading page content`, followed by `Verifying session…`; by 1.867 seconds the report heading and both 19/15-row tables were present while shared `Loading…` still remained. The next sample reached the fully settled report with only the shared `Cookbooks` status. Reload and fresh entry reproduced the exact route, title, Aug 10 date, params, headings, method note, disclaimer, and table counts.
- Core content was stable, but ordinal presentation was not fully deterministic. Baseline and post-Run cross-sections ended with rank 13 `ORCL · $214M` and rank 14 `LITE · $214M`; reload and fresh entry consistently reversed those two equal-displayed rows while all other rows matched. The review did not sort or otherwise manipulate either table.
- Back was tested on the original tab after the reload/fresh comparisons. Immediately after one native Back, the browser returned to `/app/cookbooks`; the settled gallery restored exact main scroll 2,264, all three section headings, the official Vol Surface `Open` action at y=588.2, current build, active shell, and zero dialogs. Before cleanup the two controlled tabs were the gallery and untouched fresh report. `keep: []` finalized both; the closing Browser inventory was empty.
- After Browser evidence, read-only classification at exact visible-build commit `01ad1e9` matched the observations. `RecipeReportView.runFresh` marks the request fresh and calls `query.refetch`; its header button uses native `disabled={query.isFetching}`, swaps only the icon for `Spinner`, and keeps the label `Run`. The shared Spinner owns a generic accessible `Loading` status, but this view supplies no report-scoped label, `aria-busy`, refreshed timestamp, or completion announcement. Initial pending returns Skeleton blocks without report identity. The focused contract tests cover owned deadlines, offline launch, timeout, and retry policy—not the customer-facing progress/completion lifecycle. In `vol_screen`, source rounds premium to whole millions before `ORDER BY premium_m DESC` and gives tied values no secondary order. Source classified Browser evidence; it is not visible proof, and no test was run.
- No date picker, historical route, parameter, sort, recipe, AI, account, billing, Watchlist, external service, application code, test, domain doc, configuration, or deployment state was changed. The single explicit latest resolver request created no visible durable state. No screenshot was captured, so all visual/semantic claims are DOM, geometry, timing, and state evidence rather than screenshot evidence.

| Journey checkpoint | Novice expectation | Browser-observed result | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Enter the official latest report | Requested report/session identity remains present and announced while data resolves | Clean route/title committed, but only footer plus nine unlabeled pulse blocks remained through 2.238 seconds | **Bad** · `CM-UX-025` |
| Request one fresh latest run | One clear action identifies scope, preserves evidence, and exposes meaningful busy state | Correct header button disabled with a spinner and prior evidence stayed exact, but the label remained `Run`, focus fell away, and no report-level busy/scope text appeared | Mixed · **Bad** `CM-UX-058` |
| Know the run completed and what is fresh | Completion is explicit even when returned data is unchanged | By 2.144 seconds the button simply returned to its identical enabled state with no updated/fresh/completed proof | **Bad** · `CM-UX-057`, `CM-UX-058` |
| Reconstruct from reload and a fresh clean URL | The same latest report loads through one coherent pending contract | Core report identity reproduced, but reload moved from announced loading into a silent footer-only phase and fresh entry briefly mixed complete rows with shared Loading | Mixed · **Bad** `CM-UX-025` |
| Compare stable ranked evidence | Unchanged represented data produces stable ranks or visibly honest ties | Equal-displayed `$214M` ORCL/LITE rows swapped ranks 13/14 after reload/fresh entry | **Bad** · `CM-UX-059` |
| Return to source and clean up | One Back restores source context without durable mutation | Exact gallery route, 2,264 scroll, official action, active shell, and zero dialogs returned; cleanup left zero tabs | Good |

#### What is good

- Keep stale-while-refresh evidence preservation. The report did not erase, dim into illegibility, or replace the complete Aug 10 analysis while Run was pending; date, params, tables, method caveats, disclaimer, route, and title remained available for comparison.
- Keep single-action concurrency safety. The exact header Run visibly disabled and displayed motion during the request, preventing a normal second activation; the separate parameter-bar Run remained untouched.
- Keep clean-route reconstruction. Reload and an independent fresh entry reproduced the same latest date, inputs, chapter structure, table counts, core rows, method note, and disclaimer without relying on guessed route state.
- Keep the official volatility honesty. The settled report consistently retained Vendor-HV limitations and explicitly avoided relabeling that field as governed IV30−RV20 across Run, reload, and fresh entry.
- Keep native gallery history. One Back again restored the exact official-card neighborhood and scroll after a longer report lifecycle, and deterministic Browser finalization left no hidden test tabs.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-025 · High · One report exposes three contradictory loading contracts and still drops its identity**
>
> The ordinary SPA entry committed the final clean route/title, then showed only footer plus nine anonymous pulse blocks through 2.238 seconds. Reload initially did better with `Loading…` and `Verifying session…`, but then dropped those labels and returned to the same footer-only shell through 2.382 seconds before completing at 3.674 seconds. Fresh entry briefly exposed complete rows while its shared `Loading…` state still remained. These are not three different user jobs; they are three entrances to one latest report. A novice cannot tell whether the recipe, auth shell, or data resolve owns the wait, and assistive technology loses the requested report identity between phases. Acceptance signal: every entry path immediately names `Vol Surface`, its latest/historical identity and date if known, owns one stable `aria-busy` region with inert retained structure, and transitions monotonically from requested → resolving → complete or explicit Run/Back recovery without a footer-only gap or stale global loading state.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-058 · Medium · Explicit Run has a spinner but no verifiable refresh transaction**
>
> One header Run correctly disabled and preserved the complete report, yet its text stayed `Run`, the only new accessible status was the generic Spinner label `Loading`, the report never became semantically busy, and focus fell to `BODY`. At completion the spinner vanished and the same enabled button returned over the same compared report signature—no `Updated`, completion time, freshness proof, toast, or live announcement distinguished success from a canceled/no-op request. This matters precisely when a market-closed refresh returns identical data: the trader asked for fresh evidence and receives no proof that freshness was established. Acceptance signal: label the in-flight scope (`Refreshing latest completed session…`), expose report-level busy/live semantics while retaining readable prior evidence, keep or deliberately restore a stable focus target, and terminate with `Updated <time> · Aug 10, 2026` or a specific recoverable error even when data is unchanged.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-057 · Medium · Run, reload, and fresh entry never explain why Aug 10 is represented**
>
> Round 030 carried the exact Aug 10 date through every state, but none said `Latest completed`, `Historical`, `As of`, or whether the clean URL will advance on the next completed session. Run completion offered no new freshness wording, and independent entry required the same route-decoding or remembered gallery context already identified in Round 029. Exact date is necessary and worth keeping; it is not sufficient to explain selection mode or link behavior. Acceptance signal: expose `Latest completed · Aug 10, 2026` in the report header, date control, pending region, Run progress/completion, errors, and accessible name; use `Historical · <date>` for pinned reports and explain whether each URL follows latest or stays fixed.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-059 · Low · Equal displayed premium rows exchange numbered ranks across identical entries**
>
> The original and post-Run screen numbered ORCL 13 and LITE 14; reload and fresh entry numbered LITE 13 and ORCL 14, while both rows displayed `$214M` and the represented date, params, table count, and all other rows were unchanged. Exact-build source explains the instability: it orders only on premium already rounded to whole millions. A novice sees precise ordinal ranks without enough precision or a tie signal to understand why they reverse. Acceptance signal: order on the unrounded metric with a deterministic secondary key and display sufficient precision to justify different ordinals, or present equal displayed values as a shared rank/tie with stable symbol order.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The right owning boundary is one live-report refresh transaction: represented session, retained evidence, start, progress, completion, failure, and stable output order belong together. The current implementation gets retained evidence and concurrency safety right, but makes customers infer the transaction from a small icon and infer completion from its disappearance; entry-path loading and ranked ties expose additional state that the report does not coherently own.
- Findings added or strengthened: added `CM-UX-058` (Medium) for unverifiable explicit-Run progress/completion and `CM-UX-059` (Low) for unstable equal-displayed ranks. Strengthened `CM-UX-025` (High) with measured SPA/reload/fresh phase disagreement and `CM-UX-057` (Medium) with mode ambiguity surviving Run completion and independent reconstruction. No new finding was filed for unchanged data itself; identical market-closed output is reasonable when the UI proves the refresh transaction.
- Coverage rows updated: added active-desktop latest explicit Run and clean-route durability, expanded the main Cookbooks and gallery/report continuity rows through Round 030, and narrowed the blocked latest-versus-pinned row to its still-unreachable historical half. Evidence retention, single-action disabling, core reload/fresh equality, method honesty, Back restoration, and cleanup are good.
- What remains unknown: explicit latest failure/timeout and genuinely long-running feedback; visible historical selection and non-trading-date handling after the shared picker is repaired; pinned mode, URL, reload/fresh entry, Run, and Back chronology; other official/private recipes; mobile Run/session behavior; keyboard/screen-reader experience beyond inspected DOM semantics; screenshot appearance; and production parity.
- Next recommended round: Round 031 — test one alternate active Home research workflow from ordinary question selection through checklist comprehension, first evidence handoff, clean-route durability, and Back recovery on desktop and mobile, without invoking AI, saving a template, or mutating research/account state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, one-safe-action limit, exact control ownership, polling/termination evidence, source-after-Browser classification, no-screenshot label, Back proof, and deterministic `keep: []` cleanup rules handled this round without a new durable operator procedure.

#### What is good

- Keep the intent-first question and evidence boundary. `Did options flow precede this stock move?` is a recognizable trader job, and the guardrail correctly separates observable chronology from unproven causality and private information.
- Keep the four-step sequence. A bounded Historical window, contract concentration, post-move symbol structure, and later Live follow-through are distinct evidence jobs in a defensible order; none claims prediction, a magnet, dealer intent, or causation.
- Keep canonical, inspectable destinations. The four cards and loaded checklist agreed on `/app/option-trades/historical`, `/app/rank/contracts`, `/app/rank/symbols`, and `/app/option-trades/live`.
- Keep honest demo labeling. Every preview was visibly labeled `Interactive demo` and `Local test capture · 2026-08-04`, so the novice was not asked to treat design art as current market evidence.
- Keep the explicit browser-local/reset disclosure and load confirmation. The plan's temporary nature and the successful replacement were visible before navigation, even though the actual Back boundary does not yet honor that promise.
- Keep the clean Historical URL generic. Reload and independent entry reconstructed the same current workstation without pretending to preserve a private Home task or fabricating symbol/date inputs. The defect is the initial handoff guidance, not the absence of hidden query parameters.
- Keep neutral destination data until the trader supplies identity. Historical used empty symbol scope, current completed-session controls, and broad rows rather than silently choosing a security or date that Home never knew.
- Keep outer mobile containment and accessible control names. Home and Historical stayed within 390 pixels, Start/AI fit at 310×44, and date/time accessible names retained `2026-08-10` / `Full day` even though their visible mobile presentation remains weak.
- Keep deterministic cleanup. No persistent research/account state changed; the viewport override was reset and every controlled Browser tab was finalized.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-002 · Medium · The same novice journey still exposes the internal builder model instead of customer language**
>
> Round 031 reproduced `Atomic ability library`, `atomic ability`, `lenses`, `Local composition`, and `Current Build` on active desktop/mobile, while the documented customer terms `Analysis tools`, `focus`, `Your checklist`, and `Load into your checklist` were absent. Even the customer-facing load action remains `Use template`. A trader who asked one chronology question must understand templates, abilities, lenses, a composition, a build, step links, and AI customization before gathering evidence. Exact-build source confirms these are deployed default messages rather than a transient translation failure. Acceptance signal: the primary path consistently uses question, analysis tools, focus, and checklist language; builder taxonomy appears only after an explicit customize choice.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-020 · High · The selected chronology explanation and next action are more than two mobile screens away**
>
> At `390 × 844`, the selected question began at content y=524, but its selected heading began at y=2,304.3, guardrail at y=2,456.3, and `Use template` at y=2,598.3; Start began at y=4,102.3 in a 6,146-pixel scroller. The explanation was 1,780 pixels, the guardrail 1,932 pixels, the load action 2,074 pixels, and Start 3,578 pixels below the choice. Desktop still put `Use template` 862 pixels below the question. Four large demos and the ability catalog make a successful selection feel inert and bury the safety boundary. Acceptance signal: selecting any question reveals its compact summary, guardrail, and load/start action in the current or next mobile viewport and adjacent to the selected summary on desktop; deeper demos remain progressively disclosed.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-031 · High · The first evidence handoff drops the exact task across both Rank and Historical**
>
> Round 015's `Compare IV with realized volatility` step opened broad Rank Symbols. Round 031's `Inspect the pre-move window` step opened generic Historical with empty symbol scope, `Latest trading day`, current date/full day, and 100 broad rows. Nothing named chronology, pre-move, the originating question, Step 1, the required symbol/move date/window, or a way back. Reload and a fresh clean URL correctly remained generic, so private workflow state need not be encoded in the URL; the initial same-tab transition still needs transient guidance. Exact-build source sends Start through a plain link to the capability href with no handoff payload. Acceptance signal: the initial destination visibly says `Step 1 · Rebuild the pre-move window`, names the missing symbol/move-date/window inputs, scopes the relevant controls, and offers return to the checklist; reload/fresh clean entry may remain honestly generic.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-032 · High · Start and Back leak scroll while silently replacing the research plan**
>
> Desktop Back restored scroll 884.5 but replaced chronology with the default call-sale question and steps; mobile direct Start → Back reproduced the substitution at scroll 2,230.5 without any destination reload, reset, or undo message. The chronology choice was 1,642.5 pixels above the mobile viewport, so the user first sees a plausible but wrong default template. Mobile Start also arrived in Historical at scroll 206, pushing its warning title to y=-50 and its page title above the main viewport start. Exact-build source explains the reset: Home and its workbench each initialize local state to `call_sale_check` / `DEFAULT_HOME_WORKFLOW`, while Start unmounts Home through a plain route link. That implementation conflicts with copy saying reset occurs on page refresh. Acceptance signal: same-tab Start → destination → Back restores the identical selected question, ordered checklist, and meaningful scroll; every destination begins at its intended top or focused handoff banner. If route-away intentionally discards state, disclose it before Start and return to the top with an explicit reset explanation.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-033 · Medium · AI is still visually primary over doing step 1**
>
> After loading the complete chronology plan, desktop Start and AI were adjacent 336×44 controls; mobile used two 310×44 controls. Start was neutral gray (`oklch(0.269 0 0)`), while `Customize with AI` was TradingFlow blue (`rgb(30, 77, 123)`). Source confirms Start uses the secondary button variant and AI uses the default primary variant. The hierarchy tells a novice to compose more before observing evidence. Acceptance signal: after load, Start is the unmistakable primary action with a destination-specific label such as `Start in Historical`; AI is secondary and does not outrank first evidence.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-016 · High · “Mobile View Optimized” still means a five-screen-wide desktop table**
>
> The chronology handoff's Historical page fit the 390-pixel document, yet the actual research table measured 1,865 pixels inside a 374-pixel horizontal scroller. Its mobile status says `Mobile View Optimized` while immediately recommending a desktop, and the table still offers no persistent symbol/date identity, swipe cue, column prioritization, or compact alternative. The warning acknowledges the limitation but does not make the task usable. Acceptance signal: the mobile mode exposes a compact row summary or frozen identity columns with a clear horizontal affordance and preserves symbol/time context while reading distant metrics; copy states the real support level without calling a desktop table optimized.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-017 · Medium · The chronology window disappears into icons at the exact moment it matters most**
>
> Desktop visibly said `Date range: 2026-08-10` and `Time range: Full day`. Mobile retained those strings only in accessible names while reducing each control to a 40-pixel calendar/clock icon. For a task whose first instruction is to rebuild a pre-move window, a sighted novice cannot verify the represented date/time without opening controls or inferring it from rows. Acceptance signal: mobile keeps a concise visible window summary—such as `Aug 10 · Full day`—adjacent to Historical mode and the originating step, with icons as supplements rather than the only visible representation.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: the chronology workflow has the right research model, guardrail, destinations, and clean-route honesty, but it is not yet a good first-use design. The selected plan is buried, internal taxonomy competes with the job, AI outranks doing evidence, the first destination loses the task, mobile weakens the critical window/table, and Back can silently substitute a different plan while preserving misleading scroll.
- Findings added or strengthened: no new ID was required. Strengthened `CM-UX-002` (Medium), `CM-UX-016` (High), `CM-UX-017` (Medium), `CM-UX-020` (High), `CM-UX-031` (High), `CM-UX-032` (High), and `CM-UX-033` (Medium). `CM-UX-031` now covers the shared Home evidence-handoff root across Rank and Historical rather than treating each destination as a separate defect.
- Source classification: exact visible-build source at `01ad1e9`, inspected only after Browser, initializes `HomePage.activeWorkflowKey`, workbench `sourceWorkflowKey`, and `sequence` to the default call-sale workflow. `Use template` changes component state only; Start is a plain link to the capability href; Start uses `variant="secondary"`; AI uses the default primary button; and the customer copy promises reset on refresh. This explains the observed handoff/reset/style behavior but does not replace Browser evidence.
- Coverage rows updated: expanded active Home through both IV→Rank and chronology→Historical journeys; added active desktop/mobile Historical arrival/reload/fresh/Back coverage; and added a dedicated Home-to-Historical continuity row. Clean-route determinism, canonical destinations, neutral unscoped data, guardrail quality, mobile outer containment, and cleanup are recorded as strengths.
- What remains unknown: the actual symbol/date/window continuation after generic Historical arrival; direct Home refresh and fresh Home entry reset feedback; the remaining call-sale and catalyst first-step handoffs; later steps; Home loading/error/degraded behavior; AI/Recipe/save paths; guest/unpaid parity; production parity; and screenshot appearance.
- Next recommended round: Round 032 — continue the same active chronology job inside Historical with one explicitly bounded symbol/session fixture, testing discovery and setting of the missing symbol/window, visible task/window identity, pending/empty/success/recovery, row relevance, mobile comprehension, and return without saving a Filter Set, exporting, sorting, invoking AI, or mutating account state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, one-safe-path boundary, exact control ownership, clean-route distinction, screenshot-stop rule, source-after-Browser classification, reset/cleanup proof, and living-report schema handled the round. `R031-BROWSER-001` was contained without turning failed screenshots into visual evidence; only this living report changed.

### Round 032 — Can a novice assemble and trust one bounded Historical research scope?

#### Round Test Plan

- Planning timestamp: 2026-08-10 20:54:01 EDT (UTC-04:00). This charter was written before opening or manipulating the product for Round 032.

The planned fixture is `AAPL · 2026-08-07 · 15:00–16:00 ET`; it tests control and evidence integrity only and makes no claim that AAPL moved afterward, that flow caused a move, or that displayed sentiment identifies a buyer, seller, or dealer intent. - Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Historical owns one visible committed research scope—symbol, completed session, and time window—while Saved Filter Sets own reusable analytical criteria. Each scope edit should be staged or explicitly applied; labels, busy state, total, metrics, rows, pagination, and empty explanation should change atomically; every row should expose enough symbol/time identity to verify relevance; and one clear/revert path should restore the previous usable scope without silently widening, saving, or resetting unrelated criteria.

Date and time controls show their current values before and after editing, distinguish draft from committed state, explain invalid/off-session input, and keep Apply/Revert consequences obvious. During changes the old rows cannot masquerade as the new scope. A legitimate empty result says exactly which scope matched nothing and offers a nearby fix. On mobile, the committed `AAPL · Aug 7 · 15:00–16:00 ET` identity remains visibly readable without decoding icon-only controls or horizontally exploring a desktop-width table.

  1. Prove opening tab inventory, host/build/desktop viewport/DPR, active shell, Historical route, mode, Saved Filter label, all-symbol scope, date/time/lookback labels, totals, pagination, sample rows, visible loading/status regions, and zero unsolicited overlays. Capture a screenshot only if Browser capture succeeds and actually represents a later appearance claim.

#### What is good

- Keep the generic Historical contract explicit. The page named its mode and purpose, separated Historical from Live, kept `Default` visible, and gave desktop traders exact date/time labels before any edit.
- Keep row identity at the left edge. The first table cells exposed the symbol and exact trade timestamp, which is the minimum evidence a trader will need to verify a future scoped result.
- Keep the date model aligned with completed sessions. The semantic calendar selected Aug 10, kept Aug 7 enabled, and disabled weekends and future days instead of inviting impossible session requests. This is structural/accessibility evidence only; the transparent parent prevented sighted calendar use.
- Keep draft actions conceptually separate from committed scope. The semantic date/time editors exposed Apply, Revert or Clear session hours, explicit Start/End values, and did not change the table merely because the trigger opened. Their visibility defect currently prevents the good interaction model from reaching users.
- Keep transient scope out of Saved Filter ownership. `Default` remained unchanged through every reload and final fresh entry; no saved criteria, Watchlist membership, account preference, URL query, or hash was written.
- Keep deterministic reconstruction and cleanup. The clean Historical URL rebuilt the same generic workstation after every blocked editor, and the final fresh desktop tab matched the opening baseline before all tabs and viewport state were released.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-034 · Critical · Historical's three required scope editors are now invisible at the exact point of first useful research**
>
> Date, time, and symbol were not isolated rendering glitches. On the same active desktop build, their expanded parents measured 463.6×417.3, 197.6×282.1, and 851.2×615.6 while computed opacity was 0 and pointer events remained enabled. The mobile date parent repeated at 235.6×417.3. The semantic children existed—enabled Aug 7, time fields, Apply, Watchlists, and AAPL-related identity—but a sighted trader saw none of them and clicks would still land inside the invisible surface. Escape then reversed the failure: date/time became semantically closed yet stayed visibly painted at opacity 1 with pointer events disabled until reload. This blocks the only documented controls that may set mounted Historical date/time/symbol scope, so Round 031's missing-input handoff cannot be completed through ordinary UI. Acceptance signal: every shared Popover/Dialog paints while open, accepts input only while painted, fully disappears and releases hit testing/focus while closed, and passes sighted desktop/mobile open → edit/discard/apply → dismiss journeys for Historical date, time, and symbol before any downstream scope test resumes.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-017 · Medium · Mobile hides the committed window in icons, then hides the editor itself**
>
> At `390 × 844`, date and time remained two unlabeled 40×36 glyphs; only their accessible names retained `2026-08-10` and Full day. Opening the date glyph did not reveal a usable summary or calendar: the 235.6×417.3 editor stayed at opacity 0 while accepting pointer events. For a chronology task, the sighted trader can neither verify the represented window nor change it. Acceptance signal: a concise visible summary such as `Aug 10 · Full day` remains adjacent to Historical mode, and each editor paints inside the viewport with committed-versus-draft identity, clear Apply/Revert, and a fully dismissed close state.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-016 · High · The bounded research job still opens a five-screen-wide mobile table**
>
> Even before AAPL scope could be applied, the current Historical table was 1,865 pixels wide inside a 374-pixel scroller—almost five viewport widths. Symbol and time are the very fields needed to verify the planned fixture, yet no frozen identity, compact row, swipe cue, or persistent scope summary protects them while inspecting distant columns. The warning still calls the page `Mobile View Optimized` while recommending desktop. Acceptance signal: mobile offers a compact evidence row or frozen symbol/time columns, an honest support label, and a persistent committed-scope summary while any horizontal detail remains scrollable.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The intended ownership model is sound—Historical owns transient symbol/session/window scope, Saved Filters remain reusable criteria, and the generic workstation reconstructs honestly—but this is not a viable first-use design. The only three controls that can turn a broad feed into the promised evidence job are invisible while open and linger while closed. A chronology workflow that cannot accept its symbol/date/window has no first useful value, regardless of the quality of its hidden form semantics.
- Findings added or strengthened: no new ID was created. Strengthened `CM-UX-034` (Critical) with direct desktop date/time/symbol and mobile date reproductions, `CM-UX-017` (Medium) with the invisible mobile editor, and `CM-UX-016` (High) with a fresh 1,865/374-pixel table measurement. `CM-UX-005` and `CM-UX-035` were deliberately not strengthened because no lookback or AAPL scope was applied.
- Source classification: exact visible-build source at `01ad1e9`, inspected only after Browser, routes Historical date/time through the shared Base UI `Popover` primitive and the shared symbol workspace through `Dialog`. Those primitives own the common `data-open`/`data-closed` fade/zoom animation classes. Feature tests inspected at that commit assert imports, copy, action wiring, and source structure; none of the inspected tests proves painted open state, absent closed state, hit testing, or focus restoration. This supports the shared blast-radius classification but does not replace Browser evidence, and no test was run.
- Coverage rows updated: added a dedicated blocked active desktop/mobile Historical scope-construction row while preserving the proven generic baseline/restoration and the still-unproved combined fixture, empty state, recovery, and row relevance.
- What remains unknown: every downstream part of `AAPL · 2026-08-07 · 15:00–16:00 ET`—draft validity, Apply, pending state, atomic metrics/rows, AAPL relevance, deliberate `00:00–00:01` empty explanation, clear/revert, mobile scoped summary, and route-away/return—remains product-blocked behind `CM-UX-034`. Historical lookback behavior, especially `Past 3 days` after `CM-UX-005`, was untouched this round.
- Next recommended round: Round 033 — use only the native visible `Lookback window` control to re-test `Past 3 days` applying/success/timeout/reload behavior, row dates, metrics/OI disclosure, mobile comprehension, and recovery without opening a shared overlay or mutating saved/account state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The existing prewritten charter, visibility-before-child rule, one ordinary recovery, product-blocked accounting, screenshot stop rule, Browser-kernel recovery, same-call viewport proof, exact-build source-after-Browser classification, and deterministic cleanup handled the round. Only this living report changed.

### Round 033 — Can a novice trust a three-session Historical comparison?

#### Round Test Plan

- Planning timestamp: 2026-08-10 21:16:21 EDT (UTC-04:00). This charter was written before opening or manipulating the product for Round 033.
- Why this round now: Round 002 selected `Past 3 days` twice from independently restored latest-day states; both attempts published a new date range and `Applying Past 3 days lookback.` but then made settled-state inspection unresponsive until reload. Round 032 could not reach a bounded date/time/symbol fixture because shared overlays were transparent, while the Historical `Lookback window` is a separate visible native select. Re-testing this one control is the highest-value safe way to resolve whether core cross-session research can now produce trustworthy rows, metrics, and OI context without depending on the broken overlay family.
- User job: Starting from a trustworthy latest-session tape, compare option trades across the three most recent completed sessions, understand exactly which dates are represented, distinguish the primary trade rows from additive latest-known OI context, and return to the latest-session view without changing reusable criteria or account data.
- Surface and entry point: Start from a fresh active paid testapp tab, enter Option Trades through visible navigation, use the visible `Historical` mode link, and operate only the native `Lookback window` select on the clean `/app/option-trades/historical` route. `Default` must remain the active Saved Filter Set; lookback, date range, time range, symbol scope, and page are runtime-only state and must not enter the URL or Saved Filter persistence.
- Persona/account proof: Existing active paid test fixture, proved without opening the known-broken account menu: visible `User menu`, absent `Sign in`, current visible build, settled entitled rows, and no auth/paywall interruption. If entitlement or the initial latest-day dataset does not settle, record an environment blocker rather than changing identity, billing, or hidden browser state.
- Viewports and naturally reachable states: Desktop `1280 × 720` for baseline, one `Past 3 days` commit, applying/success/explicit-failure/timeout evidence, date and row consistency, metrics, OI interpretation, and recovery. Mobile `390 × 844` only after the desktop transition either settles or is deterministically recovered; inspect the committed lookback/range identity, pending or settled comprehension, primary rows, and any visible OI explanation without issuing another multi-day change. Cover only naturally rendered loading, applying, success, empty, error, timeout, and reload-recovery states.
- Greenfield first-use hypothesis: Historical should own one committed runtime lookback and publish one coherent evidence snapshot—represented range, composition metrics, total, pagination, rows, and bounded OI disclosure—while Saved Filters own reusable analytical criteria. A lookback change should keep the shell responsive, visibly distinguish old evidence from pending evidence, settle or fail within a stated bound, and offer deterministic recovery without persisting the transient window.
- Novice expectation: `Past 3 days` means three completed trading sessions, not three calendar days or an unexplained date span. The select and a nearby visible summary should say what is committed versus still applying; totals, metrics, page count, and row dates should agree once settled. Open interest shown beside older trades should be plainly labeled as the latest known comparison snapshot—not historical point-in-time OI or a same-day change—and rows should remain useful if that additive enrichment is late or unavailable.
- Journeys and safe actions:
  1. Prove an empty opening tab inventory, exact host/build/desktop viewport, active shell, clean Historical route, `Default`, all-symbol scope, latest-trading-day lookback and date label, Full day, totals, pagination, composition metrics, sampled row dates, visible status/live regions, native-select value/geometry, OI-related headers/help/copy, and zero unsolicited overlays. Use DOM/state evidence for identity and settlement; capture a screenshot only if a genuinely visual claim requires it and Browser capture is healthy.
  2. Select `Past 3 days` exactly once through the visible native select. Record the immediate committed/select value, range, applying/status copy, shell responsiveness, old-versus-new totals/metrics/rows, and then use short bounded polls through authoritative success, explicit failure, or the documented 22-second rows / 25-second metrics request bounds plus a small render allowance. Do not select the same value a second time or operate another data control. A successful result requires the select/range, total, pagination, metrics state, and sampled row dates to agree, while the rows remain primary and any OI enrichment/disclosure is bounded and additive. If ordinary Browser reads become unresponsive, make one deterministic same-route reload or fresh-tab recovery and record the timeout boundary rather than retrying the mutation.
  3. If the multi-day state settles and remains responsive, prove `390 × 844` in the same Browser call as accepted mobile measurements and inspect whether a sighted novice can read the active lookback/range and distinguish row-date/OI meaning without reopening an overlay or changing data. Then restore desktop dimensions and choose `Latest trading day` once through the same native select when operable; otherwise use the documented runtime-only reload recovery. Prove `Default`, all symbols, latest completed session, Full day, coherent rows/metrics/total/page, no visible dialog, close every controlled tab, prove inventory `[]`, reset viewport, and make `tabs.finalize({keep: []})` the final Browser action.

Source/tests may be inspected read-only only after Browser to classify an observed state, transition, copy, or persistence mismatch.

The only product-side mutation is one reversible runtime lookback change and, if responsive, one restoration through that same native select; ordinary navigation/reload and viewport emulation are allowed, with no durable user/account data.

All 100 sampled row timestamps were Aug 10; the first rows began XSP/XSP/SPXW at `08-10 16:59:59`.

The immediate Browser snapshot changed the select to `past_3_days`, published `Date range: 2026-08-08 - 2026-08-10`, announced `Applying Past 3 days lookback.`, marked the select and Refresh busy, removed the composition strip and exact total/page count, and said `Total row count is loading or unavailable — pagination uses this page size only.` The prior 100 Aug 10 rows remained visible. The route stayed clean with no query/hash and `Default` remained visible.

#### What is good

- Keep the deterministic latest-day baseline. The visible date, all sampled row dates, metrics, total, page count, `Default`, and clean route agreed before the experiment and agreed again after reload.
- Keep the lookback as a visible native control. It was a sighted, accessible 176×36 select and avoided the shared transparent-overlay family that blocked Round 032.
- Keep explicit pending feedback. The control changed immediately, exposed `Applying Past 3 days lookback.`, marked itself and Refresh busy, and replaced exact total/pagination with an honest unavailable/loading statement instead of publishing a made-up count.
- Keep primary rows present while broader work begins, but make their pending identity even clearer. The retained Aug 10 rows were still inside the requested Aug 8–10 interval, so they did not contradict the pending range; a persistent stale/current-snapshot label would make this preservation safer.
- Keep lookback runtime-only. Reload returned to Latest trading day, the clean URL never gained state, and `Default` plus all-symbol/full-day scope stayed intact.
- Keep deterministic operator and product cleanup. The mutation was not repeated after the first timeout, no hidden or adjacent control was used, and Browser state ended at zero tabs with viewport reset.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-005 · High · The current build still cannot deliver or recover a three-day Historical result without reload**
>
> The one permitted `Past 3 days` action did everything a novice could initially read as progress: its value changed in 199 ms, the date widened, the select/Refresh became busy, a live-region applying message appeared, and exact metrics/total/page count were withdrawn. The first subsequent Browser read then timed out on `Page.getFrameTree`. Reload did not begin until 54.7 seconds after that immediate state—well beyond the exact-build source's intended 22-second rows and 25-second metrics bounds—and no multi-day rows, metrics, total, pagination, OI context, failure explanation, cancel, or retry became inspectable. This is now a current-build reproduction of Round 002, not a stale one-off. Reload eventually restored the latest-day workstation, but it erased the user's research intent. A greenfield transition would keep navigation and recovery controls responsive, visibly label retained rows as the prior snapshot, terminate within a customer-facing budget, and either atomically publish the matching range/metrics/rows/OI state or show a specific failure with Retry and Return to latest. Acceptance signal: three consecutive visible `Past 3 days` attempts on representative weekday and Monday anchors each settle or fail within the documented bound while the shell remains operable; the final select, range, metrics, total, page, row dates, and OI state agree without reload.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-060 · Medium · `Past 3 days` silently means calendar days, so Monday does not produce a three-session comparison**
>
> With Latest trading day anchored to Monday Aug 10, the visible preset immediately produced Aug 8–10—Saturday, Sunday, and Monday. Exact-build source confirms that `past_3_days` simply subtracts two calendar days; it does not resolve three completed trading sessions. The adjacent preset is named `Latest trading day`, so the unit quietly changes from a market session to calendar time. A novice choosing this control to compare recent tapes can pay the cost of a multi-day query yet receive only one data-bearing session after a weekend. This is a product-design ambiguity rather than a documented-invariant violation, but fixing only the hang would leave the research job under-served. Acceptance signal: either offer `Last 3 trading sessions` using the authoritative available-session dates, or label the current choice `Past 3 calendar days` and show the number of included sessions before applying.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The ownership boundary remains sound—Historical owns transient lookback and Saved Filters remain reusable criteria—and the immediate pending contract has several honest pieces. The core outcome is still not a viable design: the broadening action becomes uninspectable past its own timeout budget, recovery discards the research window, and the preset's calendar-day unit does not match the three-session comparison a trading workstation naturally invites.
- Findings added or strengthened: strengthened `CM-UX-005` (High) with a one-action current-build reproduction, exact immediate state, 54.7-second recovery boundary, and deterministic reload result. Added `CM-UX-060` (Medium) for the distinct calendar-day-versus-trading-session design. No product finding was assigned to mobile because the viewport proof failed, and no OI finding was assigned because the multi-day state never rendered.
- Source classification: exact visible-build source at `01ad1e9`, inspected only after Browser, resolves `past_3_days` by subtracting two Day.js calendar days; renders the pending select/range, spinner, and live-region text before awaiting `fetchDataWithFilters`; keeps the prior rows until a successful response; serializes multi-day stats behind rows; and defines 22-second rows plus 25-second stats timeouts. The OI enrichment path is additive after multi-day rows, but its source tooltip still says `Change in OI from previous day` even though the governing invariant defines latest-known OI. Because Browser never reached that enriched state, this is recorded as a required future validation target rather than Browser evidence or a new ID. Inspected tests assert source wiring and mocked timeout behavior; no Browser/E2E test covers the real preset transition. No test was run.
- Coverage rows updated: the active desktop Historical row now cites Rounds 002 and 033, preserves the proven latest-day/narrow-empty recovery, and records current multi-day responsiveness plus period-semantics findings. Mobile multi-day comprehension remains uncounted.
- What remains unknown: any settled multi-day row/date distribution, exact total/page count, composition metrics, row-first OI enrichment timing, visible latest-known-OI disclosure, missing-OI behavior, timeout toast/retry/cancel behavior, same-control recovery without reload, Past week/month/custom behavior, accepted mobile lookback/range comprehension, screenshot appearance, and production parity. The Round 032 AAPL/session/time-window path remains separately product-blocked behind shared overlays.
- Next recommended round: Round 034 — from the active paid Home, select `Is unusual options positioning building before a catalyst?`, load its browser-local checklist, follow only the first ranked-contract evidence step, and verify task/context preservation plus Back recovery on desktop and mobile without changing Rank filters, views, drawers, account state, or durable data.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The prewritten charter, one-mutation rule, bounded settled-state probe, deterministic reload recovery, Browser-capability accounting, exact-build source-after-Browser classification, and close/list/reset/finalize cleanup handled the round. Only this living report changed.

#### What is good

- Keep the catalyst question and its evidence boundary. The title is recognizable, the four-step sequence separates discovery, multi-session persistence, broader structure, and current follow-through, and the caveat rejects identity, intent, and inside-information claims at the point of choice.
- Keep the atomic first step pointed at Rank Contracts. Domain truth says contract-list discovery is the correct ability before a contract-specific drawer, and both desktop/mobile Browser paths reached the first-class Contracts leaf without fabricating a symbol, contract, date, filter, or private workflow URL.
- Keep the clean destination honest and useful. Rank showed its own title, session, mixed-horizon explanation, saved-view identity, full ranked-universe count, and real rows; the general workstation did not pretend the catalyst had been scoped when it had not.
- Keep destination scroll initialization. Unlike Round 031's Historical mobile arrival, both Rank handoffs began at scroll 0 with `Contract-level analysis` visible.
- Keep outer responsive containment and full-sized Home actions. The accepted 390-pixel Home had no document/main overflow, and the question, load, Start, and AI controls fit their column. The dense Rank table remains a separate comprehension defect.
- Keep explicit local-build feedback and deterministic cleanup. Loading emitted a confirmation plus Undo toast, Browser Back used no destination reload, no Rank state was changed, and the run ended with no tabs or viewport override.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-031 · High · A third first-step handoff turns a precise Home task into a generic workstation**
>
> The catalyst checklist promised `Screen ranked contracts` with the lens `Scan unusual contracts`. Desktop and mobile instead opened a broad list of 356,187 contracts with no catalyst question, Step 1, unusual-contract lens, missing symbol/watchlist/catalyst-window input, non-causality boundary, or path back. The correct clean route and real rows are strengths; private Home state does not need to become a durable query parameter. The defect is the one-time transition: the trader is dropped at a large table and must remember both the question and the evidence standard. Exact-build source makes Start a plain link to the capability href, while the richer canonical workflow seed is sent only to AI. Acceptance signal: the initial same-tab destination shows a dismissible transient banner such as `Step 1 · Screen ranked contracts`, names the missing symbol/watchlist and catalyst window, repeats what would contradict the hypothesis, points to the relevant list evidence, and offers `Back to checklist`; reload/fresh clean entry may remain generic.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-032 · High · Back preserves a deep location but reconstructs the wrong research plan**
>
> Desktop Back returned at scroll 884.5 and mobile at 2,220.5, yet both silently replaced catalyst with the default call-sale question and four default steps. Mobile put the catalyst question 1,696.5 pixels above the viewport, so the first visible Current Build looked plausible while belonging to a different job. The only reset copy says the browser-local build resets when the page refreshes; neither journey refreshed. Exact-build source explains the mismatch: Home and its workbench initialize independent local state to `call_sale_check` and `DEFAULT_HOME_WORKFLOW`, and route-away unmounts them. Acceptance signal: Start → Rank → one Back restores the exact catalyst selection, ordered checklist, Start target, and meaningful scroll. If route-away intentionally discards the build, disclose that before Start, return to Home top, and announce the reset rather than preserving a misleading deep position.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-020 · High · Home makes the novice cross most of the page before acting on the selected question**
>
> At mobile width the catalyst question was visible at y=426, while its selected explanation began near y=2,286, its guardrail at y=2,438, and `Use template` at y=2,554 after four demo cards. Desktop likewise put the selected question at y=328 and `Use template` at y=1,254. The novice cannot compare intent, evidence, limitation, and action as one decision; the page asks them to remember a question across multiple screens of demonstrations. Acceptance signal: selecting a question reveals its description, guardrail, ordered step summary, and load/start action immediately below or beside that question; demos and ability catalogs move behind progressive disclosure.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-002 · Medium · The catalyst path still exposes implementation taxonomy instead of the documented customer model**
>
> Desktop and mobile contained `Atomic ability library`, `atomic ability`, `lenses`, `Local composition`, and `Current Build`, while none of `Analysis tools`, `focus`, `Your checklist`, or `Load into your checklist` appeared. Those internal concepts compete with the otherwise strong question-led model. Acceptance signal: the default path uses the documented customer vocabulary and keeps ability composition behind an explicit advanced/customize action.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-033 · Medium · AI remains visually primary over beginning the evidence work**
>
> After the exact catalyst template was loaded, desktop used adjacent 336×44 actions and mobile two 310×44 actions. `Start step 1` stayed neutral gray while `Customize with AI` used the blue primary treatment. Exact-build source confirms Start uses the secondary variant and AI the default variant. Acceptance signal: the task-specific evidence action—preferably `Start in Rank Contracts`—is primary, while optional AI composition is secondary.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-016 · High · The first catalyst evidence screen is still eleven mobile viewports wide**
>
> The accepted 390-pixel path ended at a 4,095-pixel Rank Contracts table inside a 310-pixel scroller. The outer document fit, but a novice cannot retain contract identity while scanning distant evidence, and there was no visible compact mode, frozen identity, or horizontal-navigation cue. Acceptance signal: mobile presents a contract-summary row with prioritized identity/activity fields or frozen identity columns plus an explicit horizontal affordance; the user can compare candidates without dragging across roughly eleven screen widths.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: The domain boundary is right—Home should frame the question and Rank Contracts should own complete-session discovery—but the current product implements only the route boundary, not the task handoff or return contract. The trader receives a sound research plan, loses it at the moment evidence begins, and returns to a different plan at the old scroll. That is not a coherent first-use workflow despite correct routes and live data.
- Findings added or strengthened: no new ID was required. Strengthened `CM-UX-002` (Medium), `CM-UX-016` (High), `CM-UX-020` (High), `CM-UX-031` (High), `CM-UX-032` (High), and `CM-UX-033` (Medium). `CM-UX-031` now has three destination families—Rank Symbols, Historical, and Rank Contracts—and `CM-UX-032` has direct desktop/mobile catalyst reproduction.
- Source classification: exact visible-build commit `01ad1e9c73cca1b31ae431ff05ec126e19210dd4` was inspected only after Browser with `git show`, avoiding the dirty working tree. `HomePage.tsx` initializes the selected question to `call_sale_check`; `HomeGuidedSections.tsx` separately initializes the source workflow and sequence to `call_sale_check` / `DEFAULT_HOME_WORKFLOW`, says reset occurs on refresh, renders Start as a secondary plain `Link` to only the first capability href, and renders AI as the primary action. `catalog.ts` correctly defines catalyst Step 1 as `rank_contract_discovery` / `unusual_contract_scan` at `/app/rank/contracts` and carries a clarifying question about symbol/watchlist, catalyst window, and contradictory evidence, but none of that seed reaches the ordinary Start link. Catalog/unit tests validate route and template structure; no exact-build Home/E2E test found by the targeted search covers selection → Start → destination guidance → Back continuity. No test was run.
- Coverage rows updated: active Home now includes catalyst desktop/mobile; Home-to-Rank continuity now covers IV → Symbols and catalyst → Contracts; Rank Contracts desktop/mobile records the current Home arrival, correct settled destination, task loss, direct Back behavior, and current mobile table width.
- What remains unknown: the default call-sale template's idempotent load/Start semantics, direct Home refresh/fresh-entry reset behavior, a destination-side transient handoff prototype, AI customization/save behavior, Home loading/error/degraded states, screenshot appearance, production parity, and the broader residual ledger.
- Next recommended round: Round 035 — from active paid Home, exercise the default call-sale checklist's idempotent `Use template` action, first Symbol-volatility handoff, and direct Back on desktop/mobile to finish the four-template first-step matrix without changing Rank state or durable data.
