#### What is good

- Keep the default call-sale question and four-part evidence plan. It separates volatility, structure, contract quality, and recent flow, and the nearby guardrail correctly says this is research rather than a recommendation to sell a covered or naked call.
- Keep the untouched equality signal. Before action, both viewports truthfully showed badge `Template`, no `Modified`, the same four preview/build steps, and the correct first target. The state model already knows that the checklist equals its source template.
- Keep the destination route and real evidence. Both Start actions reached first-class Symbol-level analysis at top, exposed the current session/default view, settled to real rows, and included the IV/RV fields needed for the promised comparison. Rank did not fabricate a symbol, option position, time horizon, risk constraint, or private workflow URL.
- Keep the default path's visible Back result. Unlike non-default templates, one Back showed the same selected question, ordered checklist, Start target, and retained workbench location on desktop/mobile. This is a user-visible pass even though equality with the default means it cannot prove in-memory instance continuity.
- Keep outer mobile containment and full-sized actions. Home fit the accepted 390-pixel document, and load, Start, and AI actions were at least 44 pixels high. The dense Rank table is a separate comprehension problem.
- Keep an accessible announcement channel and deterministic cleanup. The assertive live region reliably emitted feedback, no Rank or durable state changed, and the run ended with no controlled tabs or viewport override. The channel's current wording remains the defect.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-061 · Medium · An already-loaded checklist falsely reports replacement and offers Undo**
>
> Untouched Home already showed the selected call-sale template and Current Build as exactly equal, badge `Template`, no `Modified`, and the same Start target. Nevertheless, `Use template` remained enabled. One activation changed none of those facts but moved scroll, announced `Template loaded into Current Build.`, and created `Current Build replaced` plus enabled Undo. The sighted toast was worse than weak: at the first desktop/mobile timing samples it began exactly below the viewport and disappeared before the next sample, while assistive technology retained the false loaded message. Exact-build source already computes workflow-step equality for the badge but never consults it in `loadActiveTemplate`; the handler always replaces arrays and emits success/Undo. Acceptance signal: when selected template and Current Build are equal, remove or disable the load action with `Already in your checklist`, or make activation an acknowledged no-op. Do not emit `replaced`, offer Undo, change scroll, or claim a state change. Tests should cover equality before the UI handler, visible/assistive feedback, focus, and scroll.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-031 · High · The fourth Home question also loses its job at the first evidence screen**
>
> The call-sale checklist promised `Compare IV with realized volatility` inside a research boundary that explicitly distinguishes covered and naked calls. Rank Symbols did contain useful IV30/RV20/RV30 evidence, but the transition supplied no call-sale question, Step 1, instruction to choose a symbol, covered/naked context, decision horizon, risk constraint, or way back. This completes the matrix: IV-regime, flow chronology, catalyst, and call-sale questions all become generic workstations at their first destination. Acceptance signal: the initial same-tab Rank arrival shows a dismissible transient task such as `Step 1 · Choose a symbol, then compare IV30 with RV20`, preserves the covered/naked, horizon, and risk-constraint question, points toward the Vol evidence surface after selection, and offers `Back to checklist`; reload/fresh clean entry may remain generic.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-033 · Medium · The default path still makes AI more prominent than starting evidence work**
>
> Mobile placed equal-width 310×44 actions together, but `Start step 1` used a neutral surface while `Customize with AI` used the blue primary treatment; desktop uses the same variants. With all four question variants now covered, this is a consistent product hierarchy rather than a template accident. Acceptance signal: make the task-specific first evidence action—here `Choose a symbol and compare IV/RV`—primary, while optional AI composition is secondary.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-016 · High · The call-sale evidence screen still requires scanning a twelve-times-wide mobile table**
>
> The accepted 390-pixel path ended at a 3,891-pixel Rank Symbols table inside a 310-pixel scroller. Although the outer document fit and relevant IV/RV columns exist, a novice cannot retain symbol identity while reaching distant volatility fields, and no visible compact mode, frozen identity, or horizontal-navigation cue was observed. Acceptance signal: mobile presents a symbol-summary row with prioritized identity and IV/RV fields or frozen identity columns plus an explicit horizontal affordance.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: Home correctly owns the call-sale research question and Rank Symbols correctly owns symbol discovery plus volatility evidence, but the transition model is not trustworthy. It labels an already-equal default checklist as replaceable, claims a change that did not occur, then routes only to the generic surface and discards every question-specific input. Correct routes and live IV/RV data do not make that a coherent first-use workflow.
- Findings added or strengthened: added `CM-UX-061` (Medium) for false idempotent-load feedback. Strengthened `CM-UX-016` (High), `CM-UX-031` (High), and `CM-UX-033` (Medium). `CM-UX-031` now covers all four Home questions; `CM-UX-032` was not strengthened because the default plan after Back is visibly identical and cannot prove whether local state persisted or remounted.
- Source classification: exact visible-build commit `01ad1e9c73cca1b31ae431ff05ec126e19210dd4` was inspected only after Browser with `git show`, avoiding the dirty working tree. `HomePage.tsx` initializes the selected question to `call_sale_check`; `HomeGuidedSections.tsx` separately initializes source workflow and sequence to the same default, computes equality for `Modified`, but leaves `Use template` unconditionally enabled and makes `loadActiveTemplate` always set state, announce loaded, toast replaced, and offer Undo. Its Start action is a secondary plain `Link` containing only the first capability href, while AI is primary. `catalog.ts` correctly defines the first ability as the Vol drawer's governed IV/RV context and preserves the canonical symbol, covered/naked, horizon, and risk-constraint clarifying question, but none of that seed reaches ordinary Start. Exact-build unit tests cover catalog/route structure and the equality helper, not UI no-op feedback or Start → destination task continuity; targeted search found no matching Home E2E coverage. No test was run.
- Coverage rows updated: active Home now includes all four question variants and no-op feedback; Home-to-Rank continuity includes the default call-sale → Rank Symbols path and its visibly correct default Back result; Rank Symbols desktop/mobile record current Home arrival, useful IV/RV data, missing task context, and current mobile width.
- What remains unknown: direct Home refresh/fresh-entry reset behavior, whether a non-default loaded checklist is intentionally discarded with explicit feedback, a destination-side transient handoff prototype, AI customization/save behavior, Home loading/error/degraded states, screenshot appearance, production parity, and the broader residual ledger.
- Next recommended round: Round 036 — load one non-default Home checklist, then compare the stated browser-local reset contract under one same-route refresh and one fresh clean Home entry on desktop/mobile, without following a destination or changing durable state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: no change. The prewritten charter, exact no-op boundary, timing samples, same-call mobile viewport proof, DOM-observed fallback for one Browser role-locator miss, source-after-Browser rule, deduplication, and close/list/reset/finalize cleanup were sufficient. Only this living report changed.

- Evidence to collect: Opening/final tab inventories; exact host/build/viewport/active proof; loaded chronology versus post-reload versus fresh-entry question/build/badge/Modified/Start equality; pre/post scroll and relevant element rectangles; reset promise and any visible/assistive reset feedback; navigation type/history and route; accepted mobile containment and screenshots only if needed; exact-build source explanation; structural report validation. Deduplicate first against `CM-UX-020`, `CM-UX-032`, and `CM-UX-061`; create a new ID only if refresh/fresh-entry behavior has a distinct root problem.
- Explicit non-goals: No Start/preview-step/destination navigation, Browser Back/Forward, default-template no-op load, second non-default template, checklist edit/reorder/remove/lens change, Undo, AI/customization/prompt/Recipe/save, account action, registration, Billing/subscription, notification/Featurebase, Rank or Option Trades interaction, product-data control, guest/unpaid/production, forced loading/error/offline state, clipboard/share/external service, repository Playwright, Chrome, ego-browser, Computer Use, web search, direct API/curl, or application code/test/product/domain-doc/configuration/deployment edit. The only product-side state change is one reversible browser-memory template load per viewport plus ordinary same-route reload, fresh-tab entry, and viewport emulation.

#### Browser Execution

Execution-time adjustments and Browser evidence limits:

- The round used only the in-app Browser. Opening controlled-tab inventory was exactly `[]`. The desktop tab entered through the ordinary testapp root, settled on entitled Live, and proved the active fixture through visible `User menu`, absent `Sign in`, 22 rendered Live rows, build `v0.2.1+01ad1e9`, and no paywall or visible dialog. Home was entered through its visible shell navigation.
- `R036-BROWSER-001`: the one chronology load completed, but the attempted immediate sample failed because Browser did not expose the requested history value; later attempts also found navigation-history and performance APIs unavailable. The operator did not repeat the action. Settled DOM, route, focus, scroll, checklist, feedback, and geometry remain valid evidence; navigation type and the first post-action timing sample are Browser-capability unknowns, not product findings.
- `R036-BROWSER-002`: the first mobile reset → set → fresh-root setup still reported `1280 × 720`. The one permitted reset → set → reload retry briefly produced `390 × 844`, DPR 1, a 390-pixel document, visible Home navigation, `User menu`, and no `Sign in` on Live. Reissuing the viewport set immediately before the first Home action reverted the same tab to `1280 × 720`; that action was aborted before click, and one reload did not restore the override. No Home product action occurred at accepted mobile dimensions, so the mobile refresh/fresh-entry comparison is Browser-capability blocked rather than failed product behavior.
- No screenshot was needed for the desktop semantic, state-equality, loading-label, scroll, and element-geometry claims. No screenshot was attempted for mobile because Home never reached an accepted mobile state. No Start, destination, Back/Forward, default no-op load, checklist edit, AI action, account action, data control, or durable state was operated.

#### What is good

- Keep the explicit ephemeral-state contract. Before reload, Current Build plainly said that the browser-local checklist resets when the page refreshes; the observed refresh did exactly that.
- Keep the labeled pending state. Reload and fresh entry exposed `Loading page content` through a polite busy status while the shell transitioned, avoiding an unexplained blank interval.
- Keep the refresh orientation. The non-default plan was not paired with stale geometry: Home returned to scroll 0, kept both question choices in view, visibly identified the selected default question, and showed its explanation before the below-fold Current Build.
- Keep deterministic fresh-entry behavior. The reloaded tab and separate untouched Home tab converged on the same question, checklist, badge, Start target, feedback state, dimensions, and geometry.
- Keep the chronology plan's internal fidelity before reset. Selection and load produced the expected four evidence surfaces and made Historical the first step without fabricating ticker, date, or private workflow state.
- Keep conservative evidence boundaries and cleanup. The operator aborted before the first mobile Home action when dimensions drifted, counted no desktop geometry as mobile proof, changed no durable product state, and ended with no controlled tabs or viewport override.

#### Bad — highlighted findings

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **CM-UX-032 · High · Browser Back—not refresh—reconstructs the wrong plan at a stale deep location**
>
> Round 036 provides the missing control: direct desktop refresh intentionally reset the chronology checklist through a labeled pending state, returned to scroll 0, visibly identified the default call-sale question, and exactly matched a fresh Home entry. Prior non-default journeys instead used ordinary route-away/Back and returned to the old deep workbench scroll while silently reconstructing the default question and steps; the selected question was hundreds to thousands of pixels above the viewport, and no reset message explained the substitution. Exact-build source initializes Home state to the default on mount, while the visible contract names page refresh as the reset boundary. The unreasonable behavior is therefore not ephemeral refresh itself; it is undisclosed route-away remount plus stale scroll restoration. A novice believes the research plan survived because the viewport survived, but the content underneath has changed. Acceptance signal: same-tab destination Back restores the exact selected question, source checklist, and scroll together; or it intentionally resets all three, returns to the top, and visibly explains that leaving Home discards the browser-local checklist. Tests should distinguish direct refresh, fresh entry, and route-away/Back.

#### Round Conclusion

- Greenfield verdict: a page-lifetime research checklist is reasonable when the boundary is explicit and reset returns the trader to a coherent orientation state. Desktop refresh now earns that label. The design failure is coupling a new default checklist with the old deep location after route-away/Back, which preserves the appearance of continuity while discarding its substance.
- Findings added or strengthened: no new ID was required. Strengthened and narrowed `CM-UX-032` (High): direct refresh is the correct control, while route-away/Back is the distinct broken boundary.
- Source classification: exact visible-build commit `01ad1e9c73cca1b31ae431ff05ec126e19210dd4` was inspected only after Browser with `git show`, avoiding the dirty working tree. `HomePage.tsx` initializes the selected question to `call_sale_check`; `HomeGuidedSections.tsx` separately initializes the source question and sequence to the same default and explicitly says the browser-local build resets on refresh. `route-pending-state.tsx` provides a polite, busy `Loading page content` status, and the router installs it as the default pending component. Targeted exact-build search found a unit test for that pending-state label/router registration but no Home UI test for refresh/reset/fresh-entry or route-away/Back distinction. This source supports the observed page-remount explanation; Browser remains the evidence for user-visible behavior. No test was run.
- Coverage rows updated: active Home now records desktop non-default load → direct refresh → untouched fresh-entry equality and the accepted loading/orientation behavior. Mobile Home refresh/fresh-entry remains explicitly Browser-capability blocked.
- What remains unknown: the same Home reset/fresh-entry comparison at a stable accepted mobile viewport; Home error/degraded and AI/save paths; whether a destination-side continuity repair would preserve or explicitly reset non-default state; screenshot appearance; production parity; and the broader residual ledger.
- Next recommended round: Round 037 — re-attempt only the mobile Home non-default load → refresh → fresh-entry comparison with one accepted viewport setup and read-only dimension checks. If Browser cannot hold the dimensions, record terminal Browser-capability coverage for this slice and pivot to the next uncovered high-risk state.
- Repository Playwright E2E scripts were not run.
- Runbook maintenance: changed. The durable Browser rule now allows one reset → set → reload setup retry, then requires read-only dimension verification before counted mobile actions and forbids a redundant viewport set as a verification step. This prevents a successfully accepted override from being reverted and desktop geometry from being mislabeled as mobile evidence.
